such that
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{a})\in S_{c,a}^{b-1}
. The number is given by

\begin{split}R(a,\ b,\ c)=\sum_{j=1}^{min\left(a,\ \left[\frac{c-a}{b-1}\right]\right)}(-1)^{j+1}{a\choose j}{c-j(b-1)-1\choose a-1}.\end{split}

where
S(a,\ b,\ c)
denotes the total number of integer solution
x_{1}+x_{2}+\cdots+x_{a}=c
such that
0<x_{i}<b
for
i=1,2,\ldots,a
. The number is given by

\begin{split}S(a,\ b,\ c)=\sum_{j=0}^{min\left(a,\ \left[\frac{c-a}{b-1}\right]\right)}(-1)^{j}{a\choose j}{c-j(b-1)-1\choose a-1}.\end{split}

See, e.g.
Charalambides, 2002
.

Definition 3.12
.

For
0<q\leq 1
, we define

\begin{split}\overline{H}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(u,v,s)={\sum_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}}}\ \sum_{y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s})x_{s}},\ \end{split}

where the summation is over all integers
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s},
and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}
satisfying

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{u,s}^{0},\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}0<y_{j}<k_{2}\ \text{for}\ j=1,\ldots,s,\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{v,s}^{0}.\end{split}

The following gives a recurrence relation useful for the computation of
\overline{H}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(u,v,s)
.

Lemma 3.11
.

\overline{H}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(u,v,s)
obeys the following recurrence relation.

\begin{split}\overline{H}&{}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(u,v,s)\\
&=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\sum_{a=1}^{u-(s-1)}\sum_{b=1}^{k_{2}-1}q^{va}\overline{H}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(u-a,v-b,s-1),&\text{for}\ s>1,\ s\leq u\\
&\text{and}\ s\leq v\leq s(k_{2}-1)\\
1,&\text{for}\ s=1,\ 1\leq u,\ \text{and}\ 1\leq v\leq k_{2}-1\\
0,&\text{otherwise.}\\
\end{array}\right.\end{split}

Proof.

For
s>1
,
s\leq u
and
s\leq v\leq s(k_{2}-1)
, we observe that
x_{s}
may assume any value
1,\ldots,u-(s-1)
, then
\overline{H}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(u,v,s)
can be written as

\begin{split}\overline{H}&{}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(u,v,s)\\
=&\sum_{x_{s}=1}^{u-(s-1)}{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{u-x_{s},s-1}^{0}\end{subarray}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{2}-1\}\\
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{v,s}^{0}\end{subarray}}}q^{vx_{s}}\ \ q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s-1}}.\end{split}

Similarly, we observe that since
y_{s}
can assume the values
1,\ldots,k_{2}-1
, then
\overline{H}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(u,v,s)
can be rewritten as

\begin{split}\overline{H}&{}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(u,v,s)\\
=&\sum_{y_{s}=1}^{k_{2}-1}\sum_{x_{s}=1}^{u-(s-1)}{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{u-x_{s},s-1}^{0}\end{subarray}}}\ {\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{2}-1\}\\
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{v-y_{s},s-1}^{0}\end{subarray}}}q^{vx_{s}}\ q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s-1}}\\
=&\sum_{b=1}^{k_{2}-1}\sum_{a=1}^{u-(s-1)}q^{va}\overline{H}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(u-a,v-b,s-1)\end{split}

The other cases are obvious and thus the proof is completed.
∎

Remark 13
.

We observe that
H_{1,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(m,r,s)
is the number of integer solutions
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})
and
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})
of

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{m,s}^{k_{1}-1},\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{r,s}^{0}\end{split}

which is

\begin{split}H_{1,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(m,r,s)=R(s,\ k_{1},\ m)S(s,\ k_{2},\ r),\end{split}

where
R(a,\ b,\ c)
denotes the total number of integer solution
y_{1}+x_{2}+\cdots+y_{a}=c
such that
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{a})\in S_{c,a}^{b-1}
. The number is given by

\begin{split}R(a,\ b,\ c)=\sum_{j=1}^{min\left(a,\ \left[\frac{c-a}{b-1}\right]\right)}(-1)^{j+1}{a\choose j}{c-j(b-1)-1\choose a-1}.\end{split}

where
S(a,\ b,\ c)
denotes the total number of integer solution
x_{1}+x_{2}+\cdots+x_{a}=c
such that
0<x_{i}<b
for
i=1,2,\ldots,a
. The number is given by

\begin{split}S(a,\ b,\ c)=\sum_{j=0}^{min\left(a,\ \left[\frac{c-a}{b-1}\right]\right)}(-1)^{j}{a\choose j}{c-j(b-1)-1\choose a-1}.\end{split}

See, e.g.
Charalambides, 2002
.

The probability function of the
q
-later waiting time distribution of order
(k_{1},k_{2})
when a quota is imposed on runs of successes and failures is obtained by the following theorem. It is evident that

P_{q,\theta}(W_{L}=n)=0\ \text{for}\ n<k_{1}+k_{2}

and so we shall focus on determining the probability mass function for
n\geq k_{1}+k_{2}
.

Theorem 3.2
.

The PMF
f_{q,L}(n;\theta)=P_{q,\theta}(W_{L}=n)
satisfies

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}(W_{L}=n)=P_{q,L}^{(1)}(n)+P_{q,L}^{(0)}(n)\ \text{for}\ n\geq k_{1}+k_{2},\end{split}

where

\begin{split}P_{q,L}^{(1)}(n)=\sum_{i=k_{2}}^{n-k_{1}}\theta^{n-i}q^{ik_{1}}{\prod_{j=1}^{i}}(1-\theta q^{j-1})\sum_{s=1}^{i-k_{2}+1}\bigg[E_{q,0,k_{2}-1}^{k_{1},\infty}&(n-k_{1}-i,\ i,\ s)\\
&+F_{q,0,k_{2}-1}^{k_{1},\infty}(n-k_{1}-i,\ i,\ s)\bigg],\\
\end{split}

and

\begin{split}P_{q,L}^{(0)}(n)=\sum_{i=k_{1}}^{n-k_{2}}\theta^{i}{\prod_{j=1}^{n-i}}(1-\theta q^{j-1})\sum_{s=1}^{i-k_{1}+1}\Bigg[G_{q,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(i,\ n-k_{2}-i,\ s)+H_{q,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(i,\ n-k_{2}-i,\ s)\Bigg].\\
\end{split}

Proof.

We start with the study of
P_{q,L}^{(1)}(n)
. It is easy to see that
P_{q,L}^{(1)}(k_{1}+k_{2})={\prod_{j=1}^{k_{2}}}(1-\theta q^{j-1})(\theta q^{k_{2}})^{k_{1}}.
From now on we assume
n>k_{1}+k_{2},
and we can rewrite
P_{q,L}^{(1)}(n)
as follows

\begin{split}P_{q,L}^{(1)}(n)&=P_{q,\theta}\left(L_{n-k_{1}}^{(1)}<k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n-k_{1}}^{(0)}\geq k_{2}\ \wedge\ X_{n-k_{1}}=0\ \wedge\ X_{n-k_{1}+1}=\cdots=X_{n}=1\right).\\
\end{split}

We partition the event
W_{q,L}^{(1)}=n
into disjoint events given by
F_{n-k_{1}}=i,
for
i=k_{2},\ldots,n-k_{1}.
Adding the probabilities we have

\begin{split}P_{q,L}^{(1)}(n)=\sum_{i=k_{2}}^{n-k_{1}}P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n-k_{1}}^{(1)}<k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n-k_{1}}^{(0)}\geq k_{2}\ \wedge\ F_{n-k_{1}}=i&\ \wedge\ X_{n-k_{1}}=0\ \wedge\\
&X_{n-k_{1}+1}=\cdots=X_{n}=1\Big).\\
\end{split}

If the number of
0
’s in the first
n-k_{1}
trials is equal to
i,
that is,
F_{n-k_{1}}=i,
then in the
(n-k_{1}+1)
-th to
n
-th trials with probability of success

\begin{split}p_{n-k_{1}+1}=\cdots=p_{n}=\theta q^{i}.\end{split}

We will write
E_{n,i}^{(0)}
for the event
\{L_{n}^{(1)}<k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\geq k_{2}\ \wedge\ X_{n}=0\ \wedge\ F_{n}=i\}.
We can now rewrite as follows

\begin{split}P_{q,L}^{(1)}(n)=\sum_{i=k_{2}}^{n-k_{1}}&P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n-k_{1}}^{(1)}<k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n-k_{1}}^{(0)}\geq k_{2}\ \wedge\ F_{n-k_{1}}=i\ \wedge\ X_{n-k_{1}}=0\Big)\\
&\times P_{q,\theta}\Big(X_{n-k_{1}+1}=\cdots=X_{n}=1\mid F_{n-k_{1}}=i\Big)\\
=\sum_{i=k_{2}}^{n-k_{1}}&P_{q,\theta}\Big(E_{n-k_{1},\ i}^{(0)}\Big)\Big(\theta q^{i}\Big)^{k_{1}}.\\
\end{split}

We are going to focus on the event
E_{n-k_{1},i}^{(0)}
. For
i=1,\ldots,n-k_{1}
, a typical element of the event
\left\{L_{n}^{(1)}<k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\geq k_{2}\ \wedge\ X_{n}=0\ \wedge\ F_{n}=i\right\}
is an ordered sequence which consists of
n-k_{1}-i
successes and
i
failures such that the length of the longest success run is less than
k_{1}
and the length of the longest failure run is greater than or equal to
k_{2}
. The number of these sequences can be derived as follows. First we will distribute the
i
failures. Let
s

(1\leq s\leq i-k_{2}+1)
be the number of runs of failures in the event
E_{n-k_{1},i}^{(0)}
. Next, we will distribute the
n-k_{1}-i
successes. We divide into two cases: starting with a failure run or starting with a success run. Thus, we distinguish between two types of sequences in the event
\left\{L_{n}^{(1)}<k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\geq k_{2}\ \wedge\ X_{n}=0\ \wedge\ F_{n}=i\right\},
respectively named
(s-1,\ s)
-type and
(s,\ s)
-type, which are defined as follows

\begin{split}(s-1,\ s)\text{-type}\ :&\quad\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{1}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{2}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{2}}\mid\ldots\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s-1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{s-1}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s}},\\
\end{split}

with
i

0
’s and
n-k_{1}-i

1
’s, where
x_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s-1)
represents a length of run of
1
’s and
y_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s)
represents the length of a run of
0
’s. And all integers
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1}
, and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}
satisfy the conditions

\begin{split}0<x_{j}<k_{1}\mbox{\ for\ }j=1,...,s-1,\mbox{\ and\ }x_{1}+\cdots+x_{s-1}=n-k_{1}-i,\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{2}-1},\end{split}

\begin{split}(s,\ s)\text{-type}\ :&\quad\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{1}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{2}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{2}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{3}}\mid\ldots\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s-1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{s}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s}},\end{split}

with
i

0
’s and
n-k_{1}-i

1
’s, where
x_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s)
represents a length of run of
1
’s and
y_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s)
represents the length of a run of
0
’s. Here all of
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1},
and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}
are integers, and they satisfy

\begin{split}0<x_{j}<k_{1}\mbox{\ for\ }j=1,...,s,\mbox{\ and\ }x_{1}+\cdots+x_{s}=n-k_{1}-i,\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{2}-1},\end{split}

Then the probability of the event
E_{n-k_{1},i}^{(0)}
is given by

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(E_{n-k_{1},\ i}^{(0)}&\Big)=\\
\sum_{s=1}^{i-k_{2}+1}\Bigg[\bigg\{&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}+\cdots+x_{s-1}=n-k_{1}-i\\
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{1}-1\}\end{subarray}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{2}-1}\end{subarray}}}\big(1-\theta q^{0}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}-1}\big)\times\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}}\Big)^{x_{1}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}-1}\big)\times\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\Big)^{x_{2}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}+y_{3}-1}\big)\times\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\vdots\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}}\Big)^{x_{s-1}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}-1}\big)\bigg\}+\\
\end{split}

\begin{split}\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad&\bigg\{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}+\cdots+x_{s}=n-k_{1}-i\\
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{1}-1\}\end{subarray}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{2}-1}\end{subarray}}}\big(\theta q^{0}\big)^{x_{1}}\big(1-\theta q^{0}\big)\cdots(1-\theta q^{y_{1}-1})\times\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}}\Big)^{x_{2}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}-1}\big)\times\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\Big)^{x_{3}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}+y_{3}-1}\big)\times\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\vdots\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}}\Big)^{x_{s}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}-1}\big)\bigg\}\Bigg],\\
\end{split}

and then using simple exponentiation algebra arguments to simplify,

\begin{split}&P_{q,\theta}\Big(E_{n-k_{1},\ i}^{(0)}\Big)=\\
&\theta^{n-k_{1}-i}{\prod_{j=1}^{i}}\ (1-\theta q^{j-1})\times\\
&\sum_{s=1}^{i-k_{2}+1}\Bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}+\cdots+x_{s-1}=n-k_{1}-i\\
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{1}-1\}\end{subarray}}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{2}-1}\end{subarray}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s-1}}+\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}+\cdots+x_{s}=n-k_{1}-i\\
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{1}-1\}\end{subarray}}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{2}-1}\end{subarray}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s}}\Bigg]\\
\end{split}

Using Lemma
3.6
and Lemma
3.8
, we can rewrite as follows

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(E_{n-k_{1},\ i}^{(0)}\Big)=\theta^{n-k_{1}-i}{\prod_{j=1}^{i}}(1-\theta q^{j-1})\sum_{s=1}^{i-k_{2}+1}\Bigg[E&{}_{q,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1},\infty}(n-k_{1}-i,\ i,\ s)\\
&+F_{q,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1},\infty}(n-k_{1}-i,\ i,\ s)\Bigg],\\
\end{split}

where

\begin{split}E_{q,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1},\infty}(n&-k_{1}-i,\ i,\ s)\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}+\cdots+x_{s-1}=n-k_{1}-i\\
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{1}-1\}\end{subarray}}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{2}-1}\end{subarray}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s-1}},\end{split}

and

\begin{split}F_{q,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1},\infty}(n&-k_{1}-i,\ i,\ s)\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}+\cdots+x_{s}=n-k_{1}-i\\
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{1}-1\}\end{subarray}}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{2}-1}\end{subarray}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s}}.\end{split}

Therefore we can compute the probability of the event
W_{S}^{(1)}=n
as follows

\begin{split}P_{q,L}^{(1)}(n)=&\sum_{i=k_{2}}^{n-k_{1}}P_{q,\theta}\Big(E_{n-k_{1},\ i}^{(0)}\Big)\Big(\theta q^{i}\Big)^{k_{1}}\\
=&\sum_{i=k_{2}}^{n-k_{1}}\theta^{n-k_{1}-i}{\prod_{j=1}^{i}}(1-\theta q^{j-1})\sum_{s=1}^{i-k_{2}+1}\bigg[E_{q,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1},\infty}(n-k_{1}-i,\ i,\ s)\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+F_{q,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1},\infty}(n-k_{1}-i,\ i,\ s)\bigg]\Big(\theta q^{i}\Big)^{k_{1}}.\\
\end{split}

Finally, applying typical factorization algebra arguments,
P_{S}^{(1)}(n)
can be rewritten as follows

\begin{split}P_{q,L}^{(1)}(n)=\sum_{i=k_{2}}^{n-k_{1}}\theta^{n-i}q^{ik_{1}}{\prod_{j=1}^{i}}(1-\theta q^{j-1})\sum_{s=1}^{i-k_{2}+1}\bigg[E_{q,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1},\infty}(n-k_{1}-i,\ i,\ s)+F_{q,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1},\infty}(n-k_{1}-i,\ i,\ s)\bigg].\\
\end{split}

We are now going to study of
P_{q,L}^{(0)}(n)
. It is easy to see that
P_{q,L}^{(0)}(k_{1}+k_{2})=\theta^{k_{1}}{\prod_{j=1}^{k_{2}}}(1-\theta q^{j-1})
. From now on we assume
n>k_{1}+k_{2},
and we can write

P_{q,L}^{(0)}(n)
as follows.

\begin{split}P_{q,L}^{(0)}(n)&=P\left(L_{n-k_{2}}^{(1)}\geq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n-k_{2}}^{(0)}<k_{2}\ \wedge\ X_{n-k_{2}}=1\ \wedge\ X_{n-k_{2}+1}=\cdots=X_{n}=0\right).\\
\end{split}

We partition the event
W_{L}^{(0)}=n
into disjoint events given by
S_{n-k_{2}}=i
and
F_{n-k_{2}}=n-k_{2}-i,
for
i=k_{1},\ \ldots,\ n-k_{2}.
Adding the probabilities we have

\begin{split}P_{q,L}^{(0)}(n)=\sum_{i=k_{1}}^{n-k_{2}}P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n-k_{2}}^{(1)}\geq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n-k_{2}}^{(0)}<k_{2}\ \wedge\ &S_{n-k_{2}}=i\ \wedge\ X_{n-k_{2}}=1\ \wedge\\
&X_{n-k_{2}+1}=\cdots=X_{n}=0\Big).\\
\end{split}

If the number of
0
’s in the first
n-k_{2}
trials is equal to
n-k_{2}-i,
that is,
F_{n-k_{2}}=n-k_{2}-i,
then the probability of failures in all of the
(n-k_{2}+1)
-th to
n
-th trials is

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}(X_{n-k_{2}+1}=\cdots=X_{n}=0\,\mid\,F_{n-k_{2}}=n-k_{2}-i)={\prod_{j=n-k_{2}-i+1}^{n-i}}\left(1-\theta q^{j-1}\right).\end{split}

We will write
E_{n,i}^{(1)}
for the event
\left\{L_{n}^{(1)}\geq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(1)}<k_{2}\ \wedge\ X_{n}=1\ \wedge\ F_{n}=i\right\}.
We can now rewrite as follows

\begin{split}P_{q,L}^{(0)}(n)=\sum_{i=k_{1}}^{n-k_{2}}&P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n-k_{1}}^{(1)}\geq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n-k_{1}}^{(0)}<k_{2}\ \wedge\ F_{n-k_{2}}=n-k_{2}-i\ \wedge\ X_{n-k_{2}}=1\Big)\\
&\times P_{q,\theta}\Big(X_{n-k_{2}+1}=\cdots=X_{n}=0\mid F_{n-k_{2}}=n-k_{2}-i\Big)\\
=\sum_{i=k_{1}}^{n-k_{2}}&P_{q,\theta}\Big(E_{n-k_{2},\ i}^{(1)}\Big){\prod_{j=n-k_{2}-i+1}^{n-i}}\left(1-\theta q^{j-1}\right).\\
\end{split}

We are going to focus on the event
E_{n-k_{2},i}^{(1)}
. For
i=k_{1},\ldots,n-k_{2}
, a typical element of the event
\left\{L_{n-k_{1}}^{(1)}>k_{1}-1\ \wedge\ L_{n-k_{1}}^{(0)}\leq k_{2}-1\ \wedge\ F_{n-k_{2}}=i\right\}
is an ordered sequence which consists of
i
successes and
n-k_{2}-i
failures such that the length of the longest success run is greater than or equal to
k_{1}
and the length of the longest failure run is less than
k_{2}
. The number of these sequences can be derived as follows. First we will distribute the
i
successes. Let
s

(1\leq s\leq i-k_{1}+1)
be the number of runs of successes in the event
E_{n-k_{2},i}^{(1)}
. Next, we will distribute the
n-k_{1}-i
failures. We divide into two cases: starting with a success run or starting with a failure run. Thus, we distinguish between two types of sequences in the event
\left\{L_{n-k_{1}}^{(1)}\geq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n-k_{1}}^{(0)}<k_{2}\ \wedge\ F_{n-k_{2}}=i\right\},
respectively named
(s-1,\ s)
-type and
(s,\ s)
-type, which are defined as follows.

\begin{split}(s,\ s-1)\text{-type}\ :&\quad\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{1}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{2}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{2}}\mid\ldots\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s-1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{s}},\\
\end{split}

with
n-k_{2}-i

0
’s and
i

1
’s, where
x_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s)
represents a length of run of
1
’s and
y_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s-1)
represents the length of a run of
0
’s. And all integers
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}
, and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1}
satisfy the conditions

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{1}-1},\text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}0<y_{j}<k_{2}\mbox{\ for\ }j=1,...,s-1,\mbox{\ and\ }y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}=n-k_{2}-i,\end{split}

\begin{split}(s,\ s)\text{-type}\ :&\quad\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{1}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{2}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{2}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{3}}\mid\ldots\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{s}},\end{split}

with
n-k_{2}-i

0
’s and
i

1
’s, where
x_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s)
represents a length of run of
1
’s and
y_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s)
represents the length of a run of
0
’s. And all integers
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}
, and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}
satisfy the conditions

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{1}-1},\text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}0<y_{j}<k_{2}\mbox{\ for\ }j=1,...,s,\mbox{\ and\ }y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}=n-k_{2}-i,\end{split}

Then the probability of the event
E_{n-k_{2},i}^{(1)}
is given by

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(E_{n-k_{2},\ i}^{(1)}&\Big)=\\
\sum_{s=1}^{i-k_{1}+1}\Bigg[\bigg\{&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{1}-1}\end{subarray}}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}=n-k_{2}-i\\
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{2}-1\}\end{subarray}}\big(\theta q^{0}\big)^{x_{1}}\big(1-\theta q^{0}\big)\cdots(1-\theta q^{y_{1}-1})\times\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}}\Big)^{x_{2}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}-1}\big)\times\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\Big)^{x_{3}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}+y_{3}-1}\big)\times\\
&\quad\quad\quad\vdots\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}}\Big)^{x_{s}}\bigg\}+\\
\end{split}

\begin{split}\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\bigg\{&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{1}-1}\end{subarray}}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}=n-k_{2}-i\\
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{2}-1\}\end{subarray}}\big(1-\theta q^{0}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}-1}\big)\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}}\Big)^{x_{1}}\times\\
&\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}-1}\big)\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\Big)^{x_{2}}\times\\
&\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}+y_{3}-1}\big)\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}+y_{3}}\Big)^{x_{3}}\times\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\vdots\\
&\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}-1}\big)\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}}\Big)^{x_{s}}\bigg\}\Bigg].\\
\end{split}

and then using simple exponentiation algebra arguments to simplify,

\begin{split}&P_{q,\theta}\Big(E_{n-k_{2},\ i}^{(1)}\Big)=\\
&\theta^{i}{\prod_{j=1}^{n-k_{2}-i}}\ (1-\theta q^{j-1})\times\\
&\sum_{s=1}^{i-k_{1}+1}\Bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{1}-1}\end{subarray}}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}=n-k_{2}-i\\
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{2}-1\}\end{subarray}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s}}+\\
&\quad\quad\quad\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{1}-1}\end{subarray}}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}=n-k_{2}-i\\
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{2}-1\}\end{subarray}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s})x_{s}}\Bigg]\\
\end{split}

Using Lemma
3.10
and Lemma
3.12
, we can rewrite as follows

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(E_{n-k_{2},\ i}^{(1)}\Big)=\theta^{i}{\prod_{j=1}^{n-k_{2}-i}}\ (1-\theta q^{j-1})\sum_{s=1}^{i-k_{1}+1}\Bigg[G_{q,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(i,\ n-k_{2}-i,\ s)+H_{q,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(i,\ n-k_{2}-i,\ s)\Bigg],\\
\end{split}

where

\begin{split}G_{q,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(&i,\ n-k_{2}-i,\ s)\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{1}-1}\end{subarray}}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}=n-k_{2}-i\\
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{2}-1\}\end{subarray}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s}},\end{split}

and

\begin{split}H_{q,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(&i,\ n-k_{2}-i,\ s)\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{1}-1}\end{subarray}}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}=n-k_{2}-i\\
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{2}-1\}\end{subarray}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s})x_{s}}.\end{split}

Therefore we can compute the probability of the event
W_{L}^{(0)}=n
as follows

\begin{split}P_{q,L}^{(0)}(n)=&\sum_{i=k_{1}}^{n-k_{2}}P\Big(E_{n-k_{2},\ i}^{(1)}\Big){\prod_{j=n-k_{2}-i+1}^{n-i}}(1-\theta q^{j-1})\\
=&\sum_{i=k_{1}}^{n-k_{2}}\theta^{i}{\prod_{j=1}^{n-k_{2}-i}}\ (1-\theta q^{j-1})\sum_{s=1}^{i-k_{1}+1}\Bigg[G_{q,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(i,\ n-k_{2}-i,\ s)+H_{q,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(i,\ n-k_{2}-i,\ s)\Bigg]\\
&{\prod_{j=n-k_{2}-i+1}^{n-i}}(1-\theta q^{j-1}).\\
\end{split}

Finally, applying typical factorization algebra arguments,
P_{L}^{(0)}(n)
can be rewritten as follows

\begin{split}P_{q,L}^{(0)}(n)=\sum_{i=k_{1}}^{n-k_{2}}\theta^{i}{\prod_{j=1}^{n-i}}(1-\theta q^{j-1})\sum_{s=1}^{i-k_{1}+1}\Bigg[G_{q,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(i,\ n-k_{2}-i,\ s)+H_{q,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(i,\ n-k_{2}-i,\ s)\Bigg].\\
\end{split}

Thus proof is completed.
∎

It is worth mentioning here that the PMF
f_{q,L}(n;\theta)
approaches the probability function of the later waiting time distribution of order
(k_{1},k_{2})

in the limit as
q
tends to 1 when a quota is imposed on runs of successes and failures of IID model. The details are presented in the following remark.

Remark 14
.

For
q=1
, the PMF
f_{q,L}(n;\theta)
reduces to the PMF
f_{L}(n;\theta)=P_{\theta}(W_{L}=n)
for
n\geq k_{1}+k_{2}
is given by

\begin{split}P(W_{L}=n)=P_{L}^{(1)}(n)+P_{L}^{(0)}(n),\ n\geq k_{1}+k_{2},\end{split}

where

\begin{split}P_{L}^{(1)}(n)=\sum_{i=k_{2}}^{n-k_{1}}\theta^{n-i}(1-\theta)^{i}\sum_{s=1}^{i-k_{2}+1}R(s,\ k_{2},\ i)\Big[S(s-1,\ k_{1},\ n-k_{1}-i)+S(s,\ k_{1},\ n-k_{1}-i)\Big],\\
\end{split}

and

\begin{split}P_{L}^{(0)}(n)=\sum_{i=k_{1}}^{n-k_{2}}\theta^{i}(1-\theta)^{n-i}\sum_{s=1}^{i-k_{1}+1}R(s,\ k_{1},\ i)\Big[S(s-1,\ k_{2},n-k_{2}-i)+S(s,\ k_{2},\ n-k_{2}-i)\Big],\\
\end{split}

where

\begin{split}R(a,\ b,\ c)=\sum_{j=1}^{min\left(a,\ \left[\frac{c-a}{b-1}\right]\right)}(-1)^{j+1}{a\choose j}{c-j(b-1)-1\choose a-1}.\end{split}

and

\begin{split}S(a,\ b,\ c)=\sum_{j=0}^{min(a,\left[\frac{c-a}{b-1}\right])}(-1)^{j}{a\choose j}{c-j(b-1)-1\choose a-1}.\end{split}

See, e.g.
Charalambides, 2002
.

4
Run and frequency quotas for
q
-binary trials

In the present section we shall study of the
q
-sooner and later waiting times to be discussed will arise by setting quotas on both runs and frequencies of successes and failures. First, we consider sooner cases (or later cases) impose a frequency quota on successes and a run quota on failures. Next, we consider sooner cases (or later cases) impose a run quota on successes and a frequency quota on failures.

4.1
A frequency quota on successes and a run quota on failures are imposed

we shall discuss of the
q
-sooner and later waiting times impose a frequency quota on successes and a run quota on failures. Each cases will be described in the following subsection.

4.1.1
Sooner waiting time

The problem of waiting time that will be discussed in this section is one of the ’sooner cases’ and it emerges when a frequency quota on successes and a run quota on failures are imposed. More specifically, Binary (zero and one) trials with probability of ones varying according to a geometric rule, are performed sequentially until
k_{1}
successes in total or
k_{2}
consecutive failures are observed, whichever event occurs first. Let
\widehat{W}_{S}
be a random variable denoting that the waiting time until either
k_{1}
successes in total or
k_{2}
consecutive failures are occurred, whichever event observe sooner.
The probability function of the
q
-sooner waiting time distribution impose a frequency quota on successes and a run quota on failures is obtained by the following theorem. It is evident that

P_{q,\theta}(\widehat{W}_{S}=n)=0\ \text{for}\ 0\leq n<\text{min}(k_{1},k_{2})

and so we shall focus on determining the probability mass function for
n\geq\min(k_{1},\ k_{2})
.

Theorem 4.1
.

The PMF
P_{q,\theta}(\widehat{W}_{S}=n)
satisfies

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}(\widehat{W}_{S}=n)=\widehat{P}_{q,S}^{(1)}(n)+\widehat{P}_{q,S}^{(0)}(n),\ \text{for}\ n\geq\min(k_{1},\ k_{2}),\end{split}

where
\widehat{P}_{q,S}^{(1)}(k_{1})=\theta^{k_{1}}
,
\widehat{P}_{q,S}^{(0)}(k_{2})={\prod_{j=1}^{k_{2}}}(1-\theta q^{j-1})
,

\begin{split}\widehat{P}_{q,S}^{(1)}(n)=\theta^{k_{1}}\ {\prod_{j=1}^{n-k_{1}}}\ (1-\theta q^{j-1})\ \sum_{s=1}^{k_{1}}\bigg[\overline{H}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}&(k_{1},\ n-k_{1},\ s)\\
&+\overline{G}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(k_{1},\ n-k_{1},\ s)\bigg],\ n>k_{1}\\
\end{split}

and

\begin{split}\widehat{P}_{q,S}^{(0)}(n)=\sum_{i=1}^{\min(n-k_{2},k_{1}-1)}\theta^{i}\ {\prod_{j=1}^{n-i}}(1-\theta q^{j-1})\sum_{s=1}^{i}\bigg[\overline{G}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}&(i,\ n-k_{2}-i,\ s)\\
&+\overline{H}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(i,\ n-k_{2}-i,\ s)\bigg],\ n>k_{2}.\\
\end{split}

Proof.

We start with the study of
\widehat{P}_{q,S}^{(1)}(n)
. It is easy to see that
\widehat{P}_{q,S}^{(1)}(k_{1})=\big(\theta q^{0}\big)^{k_{1}}=\theta^{k_{1}}
. From now on we assume
n>k_{1},
and we can write
\widehat{P}_{q,S}^{(1)}(n)
as follows.

\begin{split}\widehat{P}_{q,S}^{(1)}(n)&=P_{q,\theta}\left(L_{n}^{(0)}<k_{2}\ \wedge\ X_{n}=1\ \wedge\ F_{n}=n-k_{1}\right).\\
\end{split}

We are going to focus on the event
\left\{L_{n}^{(0)}<k_{2}\ \wedge\ X_{n}=1\ \wedge\ F_{n}=n-k_{1}\right\}
. A typical element of the event
\left\{L_{n}^{(0)}<k_{2}\ \wedge\ X_{n}=1\ \wedge\ F_{n}=n-k_{1}\right\}
is an ordered sequence which consists of
k_{1}
successes and
n-k_{1}
failures such that the length of the longest failure run is less than
k_{2}
. The number of these sequences can be derived as follows. First we will distribute the
k_{1}
successes. Let
s

(1\leq s\leq k_{1})
be the number of runs of successes in the event
\left\{L_{n}^{(0)}<k_{2}\ \wedge\ X_{n}=1\ \wedge\ F_{n}=n-k_{1}\right\}
. We divide into two cases: starting with a failure run or starting with a success run. Thus, we distinguish between two types of sequences in the event

\left\{L_{n}^{(0)}<k_{2}\ \wedge\ X_{n}=1\ \wedge\ F_{n}=n-k_{1}\right\},

respectively named
(s,\ s)
-type and
(s,\ s-1)
-type, which are defined as follows.

\begin{split}(s,\ s)\text{-type}\ :&\quad\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{1}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{2}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{2}}\mid\ldots\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s-1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{s-1}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{s}},\\
\end{split}

with
n-k_{1}

0
’s and
k_{1}

1
’s, where
x_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s)
represents a length of run of
1
’s and
y_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s)
represents the length of a run of
0
’s. And all integers
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}
, and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}
satisfy the conditions

\begin{split}0<x_{j}\mbox{\ for\ }j=1,...,s,\mbox{\ and\ }x_{1}+\cdots+x_{s}=k_{1},\end{split}

\begin{split}0<y_{j}<k_{2}\mbox{\ for\ }j=1,...,s,\mbox{\ and\ }y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}=n-k_{1}.\end{split}

\begin{split}(s,\ s-1)\text{-type}\ :&\quad\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{1}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{2}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{2}}\mid\ldots\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{s-1}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s-1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{s}},\end{split}

with
n-k_{1}

0
’s and
k_{1}

1
’s, where
x_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s)
represents a length of run of
1
’s and
y_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s-1)
represents the length of a run of
0
’s. Here all of
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s},
and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1}
are integers, and they satisfy

\begin{split}0<x_{j}\mbox{\ for\ }j=1,...,s,\mbox{\ and\ }x_{1}+\cdots+x_{s}=k_{1},\end{split}

\begin{split}0<y_{j}<k_{2}\mbox{\ for\ }j=1,...,s-1,\mbox{\ and\ }y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}=n-k_{1}.\end{split}

Then the probability of the event
\left\{L_{n}^{(0)}<k_{2}\ \wedge\ X_{n}=1\ \wedge\ F_{n}=n-k_{1}\right\}
is given by

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(0)}&<k_{2}\ \wedge\ X_{n}=1\ \wedge\ F_{n}=n-k_{1}\Big)=\\
\sum_{s=1}^{k_{1}}\Bigg[\bigg\{&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}\in S_{k_{1},s}^{0}\end{subarray}}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}=n-k_{1}\\
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{2}-1\}\end{subarray}}\big(1-\theta q^{0}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}-1}\big)\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}}\Big)^{x_{1}}\times\\
&\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}-1}\big)\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\Big)^{x_{2}}\times\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\vdots\\
&\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}-1}\big)\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}}\Big)^{x_{s}}\bigg\}+\\
\end{split}

\begin{split}\quad\quad\quad&\bigg\{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}\in S_{k_{1},s}^{0}\end{subarray}}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}=n-k_{1}\\
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{2}-1\}\end{subarray}}\big(\theta q^{0}\big)^{x_{1}}\big(1-\theta q^{0}\big)\cdots(1-\theta q^{y_{1}-1})\times\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}}\Big)^{x_{2}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}-1}\big)\times\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\vdots\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2}}\Big)^{x_{s-1}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}-1}\big)\times\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}}\Big)^{x_{s}}\bigg\}\Bigg].\\
\end{split}

Using simple exponentiation algebra arguments to simplify,

\begin{split}&P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(0)}<k_{2}\ \wedge\ X_{n}=1\ \wedge\ F_{n}=n-k_{1}\Big)=\\
&\theta^{k_{1}}{\prod_{j=1}^{n-k_{1}}}\ (1-\theta q^{j-1})\times\\
&\sum_{s=1}^{k_{1}}\Bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{k_{1},s}^{0}\end{subarray}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}=n-k_{1}\\
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{2}-1\}\end{subarray}}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s})x_{s}}+\\
&\quad\quad\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{k_{1},s}^{0}\end{subarray}}\ {\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}=n-k_{1}\\
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{2}-1\}\end{subarray}}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s}}\Bigg].\\
\end{split}

Using Lemma
3.9
and Lemma
3.11
, we can rewrite as follows.

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(&L_{n}^{(0)}<k_{2}\wedge\ X_{n}=1\ \wedge\ F_{n}=n-k_{1}\Big)\\
&=\theta^{k_{1}}\ {\prod_{j=1}^{n-k_{1}}}\ (1-\theta q^{j-1})\ \sum_{s=1}^{k_{1}}\bigg[\overline{H}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(k_{1},\ n-k_{1},\ s)+\overline{G}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(k_{1},\ n-k_{1},\ s)\bigg],\\
\end{split}

where

\begin{split}\overline{H}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(k_{1},\ n-k_{1},\ s)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{k_{1},s}^{0}\end{subarray}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}=n-k_{1}\\
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{2}-1\}\end{subarray}}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s})x_{s}},\end{split}

and

\begin{split}\overline{G}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(k_{1},\ n-k_{1},\ s)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{k_{1},s}^{0}\end{subarray}}\ {\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}=n-k_{1}\\
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in\{1,\ldots,k_{2}-1\}\end{subarray}}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s}}.\end{split}

Therefore we can compute the probability of the event
W_{S}^{(1)}=n
as follows.

\begin{split}\widehat{P}_{S}^{(1)}(n)=\theta^{k_{1}}\ {\prod_{j=1}^{n-k_{1}}}\ (1-\theta q^{j-1})\ \sum_{s=1}^{k_{1}}\bigg[\overline{H}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(k_{1},\ n-k_{1},\ s)+\overline{G}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(k_{1},\ n-k_{1},\ s)\bigg].\\
\end{split}

We are now going to study
\widehat{P}_{q,S}^{(0)}(n)
. It is easy to see that
\widehat{P}_{q,S}^{(0)}(k_{2})={\prod_{j=1}^{k_{2}}}(1-\theta q^{j-1})
. From now on we assume
n>k_{2},
and we can write
\widehat{P}_{q,S}^{(0)}(n)
as

\begin{split}\widehat{P}_{q,S}^{(0)}(n)&=P_{q,\theta}\left(L_{n-k_{2}}^{(0)}<k_{2}\ \wedge\ X_{n-k_{2}}=1\ \wedge\ X_{n-k_{2}+1}=\cdots=X_{n}=0\right).\\
\end{split}

We partition the event
\widehat{W}_{S}^{(0)}=n
into disjoint events given by
S_{n-k_{2}}=i,
for
i=1,\ldots,\min(n-k_{2},k_{1}-1).
Adding the probabilities we have

\begin{split}\widehat{P}_{q,S}^{(0)}(n)=\sum_{i=1}^{\min(n-k_{2},k_{1}-1)}P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n-k_{2}}^{(0)}<k_{2}\ \wedge\ S_{n-k_{2}}=i&\ \wedge\ X_{n-k_{2}}=1\ \wedge\\
&X_{n-k_{2}+1}=\cdots=X_{n}=0\Big).\\
\end{split}

Using
F_{n-k_{2}}=n-k_{2}-S_{n-k_{2}},
we can rewrite as follows.

\begin{split}\widehat{P}_{q,S}^{(0)}(n)=\sum_{i=1}^{\min(n-k_{2},k_{1}-1)}P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n-k_{2}}^{(0)}<k_{2}\ \wedge\ F_{n-k_{2}}=n-k_{2}-i&\ \wedge\ X_{n-k_{2}}=1\ \wedge\\
&X_{n-k_{2}+1}=\cdots=X_{n}=0\Big).\\
\end{split}

If the number of
0
’s in the first
n-k_{2}
trials is equal to
n-k_{2}-i,
that is,
F_{n-k_{2}}=n-k_{2}-i,
then the probability of failures in all of the
(n-k_{2}+1)
-th to
n
-th trials is

\begin{split}P(X_{n-k_{2}+1}=\cdots=X_{n}=0\,\mid\,F_{n-k_{2}}=n-k_{2}-i)={\prod_{j=n-k_{2}-i+1}^{n-i}}(1-\theta q^{j-1}).\end{split}

We write
E_{n,i}^{(1)}
for the event
\left\{L_{n}^{(0)}<k_{2}\ \wedge\ X_{n}=1\ \wedge\ F_{n}=n-i\right\}.
We can now rewrite as follows.

\begin{split}\widehat{P}_{q,S}^{(0)}(n)=\sum_{i=1}^{\min(n-k_{2},k_{1}-1)}P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n-k_{2}}^{(0)}<&k_{2}\ \wedge\ F_{n-k_{2}}=n-k_{2}-i\ \wedge\ X_{n-k_{2}}=1\Big)\\
&\times P_{q,\theta}\Big(X_{n-k_{2}+1}=\cdots=X_{n}=0\mid F_{n-k_{2}}=n-k_{2}-i\Big)\\
=\sum_{i=1}^{\min(n-k_{2},k_{1}-1)}P_{q,\theta}\Big(E_{n-k_{2},\ i}^{(1)}&\Big){\prod_{j=n-k_{2}-i+1}^{n-i}}(1-\theta q^{j-1}).\\
\end{split}

We are going to focus on the event
E_{n-k_{2},i}^{(1)}
. A typical element of the event
E_{n-k_{2},i}^{(1)}
is an ordered sequence which consists of
i
successes and
n-k_{2}-i
failures such that the length of the longest failure run is less than
k_{2}
. The number of these sequences can be derived as follows. First we will distribute the
i
successes. Let
s

(1\leq s\leq i)
be the number of runs of successes in the event
E_{n-k_{2},\ i}^{(1)}
. We divide into two cases:starting with a success run or starting with a failure run. Thus, we distinguish between two types of sequences in the event

\left\{L_{n-k_{2}}^{(0)}<k_{2}\ \wedge\ F_{n-k_{2}}=n-k_{2}-i\ \wedge\ X_{n-k_{2}}=1\right\},

respectively named
(s,\ s-1)
-type and
(s,\ s)
-type, which are defined as follows.

\begin{split}(s,\ s-1)\text{-type}\ :&\quad\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{1}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{2}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{2}}\mid\ldots\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s-1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{s}},\\
\end{split}

with
n-k_{2}-i

0
’s and
i

1
’s, where
x_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s)
represents the length of a run of
1
’s and
y_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s-1)
represents the length of a run of
0
’s. And all integers
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}
, and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1}
satisfy the conditions

\begin{split}0<x_{j}\mbox{\ for\ }j=1,...,s,\mbox{\ and\ }x_{1}+\cdots+x_{s}=i,\end{split}

\begin{split}0<y_{j}<k_{2}\mbox{\ for\ }j=1,...,s-1,\mbox{\ and\ }y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}=n-k_{2}-i.\end{split}

\begin{split}(s,\ s)\text{-type}\ :&\quad\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{1}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{2}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{2}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{3}}\mid\ldots\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{s}},\end{split}

with
n-k_{2}-i

0
’s and
i

1
’s, where
x_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s)
represents the length of a run of
1
’s and
y_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s)
represents the length of a run of
0
’s. Here all of
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s},
and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}
are integers, and they satisfy

\begin{split}0<x_{j}\mbox{\ for\ }j=1,...,s,\mbox{\ and\ }x_{1}+\cdots+x_{s}=i,\end{split}

\begin{split}0<y_{j}<k_{2}\mbox{\ for\ }j=1,...,s,\mbox{\ and\ }y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}=n-k_{2}-i.\end{split}

Then the probability of the event
E_{n-k_{2},i}^{(1)}
is given by

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(E_{n-k_{2},\ i}^{(1)}&\Big)=\\
\sum_{s=1}^{i}\Bigg[\bigg\{&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{0}\end{subarray}}{\hskip 14.22636pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}=n-k_{2}-i\\
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{2}-1\}\end{subarray}}}\big(\theta q^{0}\big)^{x_{1}}\big(1-\theta q^{0}\big)\cdots(1-\theta q^{y_{1}-1})\times\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}}\Big)^{x_{2}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}-1}\big)\times\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\Big)^{x_{3}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}+y_{3}-1}\big)\times\\
&\quad\quad\quad\vdots\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}}\Big)^{x_{s}}\bigg\}+\\
\end{split}

\begin{split}\quad\quad\quad&\bigg\{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{0}\end{subarray}}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}=n-k_{2}-i\\
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{2}-1\}\end{subarray}}\big(1-\theta q^{0}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}-1}\big)\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}}\Big)^{x_{1}}\times\\
&\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}-1}\big)\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\Big)^{x_{2}}\times\\
&\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}+y_{3}-1}\big)\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}+y_{3}}\Big)^{x_{3}}\times\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\vdots\\
&\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}-1}\big)\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}}\Big)^{x_{s}}\bigg\}\Bigg].\\
\end{split}

Using simple exponentiation algebra arguments to simplify,

\begin{split}&P_{q,\theta}\Big(E_{n-k_{2},\ i}^{(1)}\Big)=\\
&\theta^{i}\ {\prod_{j=1}^{n-k_{2}-i}}\ (1-\theta q^{j-1})\times\\
&\sum_{s=1}^{i}\Bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{0}\end{subarray}}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}=n-k_{2}-i\\
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{2}-1\}\end{subarray}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s}}+\\
&\quad\quad\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{0}\end{subarray}}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}=n-k_{2}-i\\
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{2}-1\}\end{subarray}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s})x_{s}}\Bigg].\\
\end{split}

Using Lemma
3.9
and Lemma
3.11
, we can rewrite as follows.

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(E_{n-k_{2},\ i}^{(1)}\Big)=\theta^{i}\ {\prod_{j=1}^{n-k_{2}-i}}\ \left(1-\theta q^{j-1}\right)\sum_{s=1}^{i}\bigg[\overline{G}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}&(i,\ n-k_{2}-i,\ s)\\
&+\overline{H}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(i,\ n-k_{2}-i,\ s)\bigg],\\
\end{split}

where

\begin{split}\overline{G}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(i,\ n-k_{2}-i,\ s)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{0}\end{subarray}}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}=n-k_{2}-i\\
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{2}-1\}\end{subarray}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s}},\end{split}

and

\begin{split}\overline{H}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(i,\ n-k_{2}-i,\ s)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{0}\end{subarray}}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}=n-k_{2}-i\\
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{2}-1\}\end{subarray}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s})x_{s}}.\end{split}

Therefore we can compute the probability of the event
\widehat{W}_{S}^{(0)}=n
as follows

\begin{split}\widehat{P}_{q,S}^{(0)}(n)=&\sum_{i=1}^{\min(n-k_{2},k_{1}-1)}P\Big(E_{n-k_{2},\ i}^{(1)}\Big){\prod_{j=n-k_{2}-i+1}^{n-i}}(1-\theta q^{j-1})\\
=&\sum_{i=1}^{\min(n-k_{2},k_{1}-1)}\theta^{i}{\prod_{j=1}^{n-k_{2}-i}}\ (1-\theta q^{j-1})\sum_{s=1}^{i}\bigg[\overline{G}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(i,\ n-k_{2}-i,\ s)\\
&+\overline{H}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(i,\ n-k_{2}-i,\ s)\bigg]\times{\prod_{j=n-k_{2}-i+1}^{n-i}}(1-\theta q^{j-1}).\\
\end{split}

Finally, applying typical factorization algebra arguments,
\widehat{P}_{S}^{(0)}(n)
can be rewritten as follows

\begin{split}\widehat{P}_{q,S}^{(0)}(n)=\sum_{i=1}^{\min(n-k_{2},k_{1}-1)}\theta^{i}\ {\prod_{j=1}^{n-i}}(1-\theta q^{j-1})\sum_{s=1}^{i}\bigg[\overline{G}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}&(i,\ n-k_{2}-i,\ s)\\
&+\overline{H}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(i,\ n-k_{2}-i,\ s)\bigg].\\
\end{split}

Thus proof is completed.
∎

It is worth mentioning here that the PMF
\widehat{f}_{q,S}(n;\theta)
approaches the probability function of the sooner waiting time distribution of order
(k_{1},k_{2})

in the limit as
q
tends to 1 when a frequency quota on runs of successes and a run quota on failures are imposed of IID model. The details are presented in the following remark.

Remark 15
.

For
q=1
, the PMF
\widehat{f}_{q,S}(n;\theta)
reduces to the PMF
\widehat{f}_{S}(n;\theta)=P_{\theta}(\widehat{W}_{S}=n)
for
n\geq min(k_{1},k_{2})
is given by

\begin{split}P(\widehat{W}_{S}=n)=\widehat{P}_{S}^{(1)}(n)+\widehat{P}_{S}^{(0)}(n),\end{split}

where
\widehat{P}_{S}^{(1)}(k_{1})=\theta^{k_{1}}
and
\widehat{P}_{S}^{(0)}(k_{2})=(1-\theta)^{k_{2}}
,

\begin{split}\widehat{P}_{S}^{(1)}(n)=\theta^{k_{1}}(1-\theta)^{n-k_{1}}\sum_{s=1}^{k_{1}}M(s,\ k_{1})\bigg[S(s,\ k_{2},\ n-k_{1})+S(s-1,\ k_{2},\ n-k_{1})\bigg],\ n>k_{1}\\
\end{split}

and

\begin{split}\widehat{P}_{S}^{(0)}(n)=\sum_{i=1}^{\min(n-k_{2},k_{1}-1)}\theta^{i}\ (1-\theta)^{n-i}\sum_{s=1}^{i}M(s,\ i)\bigg[&S(s-1,\ k_{2},\ n-k_{2}-i)\\
&+S(s,\ k_{2},\ n-k_{2}-i)\bigg],\ n>k_{2},\\
\end{split}

where

\begin{split}M(a,\ b)={b-1\choose a-1}.\end{split}

and

\begin{split}S(a,\ b,\ c)=\sum_{j=0}^{min(a,\left[\frac{c-a}{b-1}\right])}(-1)^{j}{a\choose j}{c-j(b-1)-1\choose a-1}.\end{split}

See, e.g.
Charalambides, 2002
.

4.1.2
Later waiting time

The problem of waiting time that will be discussed in this section is one of the ’later cases’ and it emerges when a frequency quota on successes and a run quota on failures are imposed. More specifically, Binary (zero and one) trials with probability of ones varying according to a geometric rule, are performed sequentially until
k_{1}
successes in total or
k_{2}
consecutive failures are observed, whichever event gets observed later.
Let random variable
\widehat{W}_{L}
denote the waiting time until both
k_{1}
successes in total and
k_{2}
consecutive failures have observed, whichever event gets observed later. Let random variable
\widehat{W}_{L}
denote the waiting time until both
k_{1}
successes in total and
k_{2}
consecutive failures have observed, whichever event gets observed later. We now make some Lemma for the proofs of Theorem in the sequel.

Definition 4.1
.

For
0<q\leq 1
, we define

\begin{split}\overline{I}_{q}(u,v,s)={\sum_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}}}\ \sum_{y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s}},\ \end{split}

where the summation is over all integers
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s},
and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1}
satisfying

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{u,s}^{0},\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{v,s-1}^{0}.\end{split}

The following gives a recurrence relation useful for the computation of
\overline{I}_{q}(u,v,s)
.

Lemma 4.1
.

\overline{I}_{q}(u,v,s)
obeys the following recurrence relation.

\begin{split}\overline{I}&{}_{q}(u,v,s)\\
&=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\sum_{b=1}^{v-(s-2)}\sum_{a=1}^{u-(s-1)}q^{va}\overline{I}_{q}(u-a,v-b,s-1),&\text{for}\ s>1,\ s\leq u\ \text{and}\ (s-1)\leq v\\
1,&\text{for}\ s=1,\ 1\leq u,\ \text{and}\ v=0\\
0,&\text{otherwise.}\\
\end{array}\right.\end{split}

Proof.

For
s>1
,
s\leq u
and
(s-1)\leq v
, we observe that
x_{s}
may assume any value
1,\ldots,u-(s-1)
, then
\overline{I}_{q}(u,v,s)
can be written as

\begin{split}\overline{I}&{}_{q}(u,v,s)\\
=&\sum_{x_{s}=1}^{u-(s-1)}{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{u-x_{s},s-1}^{0}\end{subarray}}}\ {\sum_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{v,s-1}^{0}}}q^{vx_{s}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2})x_{s-1}}.\end{split}

Similarly, we observe that since
y_{s-1}
can assume the values
1,\ldots,v-(s-2)
, then
\overline{I}_{q}(u,v,s)
can be rewritten as

\begin{split}\overline{I}&{}_{q}(u,v,s)\\
=&\sum_{y_{s-1}=1}^{v-(s-2)}\sum_{x_{s}=1}^{u-(s-1)}{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{u-x_{s},s-1}^{0}\end{subarray}}}\ {\sum_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-2})\in S_{v-y_{s-1},s-2}^{0}}}q^{vx_{s}}\ q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2})x_{s-1}}\\
=&\sum_{b=1}^{v-(s-2)}\sum_{a=1}^{u-(s-1)}q^{va}\overline{I}_{q}(u-a,v-b,s-1)\end{split}

The other cases are obvious and thus the proof is completed.
∎

Remark 16
.

We observe that
\overline{I}_{1}(u,v,s)
is the number of integer solutions
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})
and
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})
of

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{u,s}^{0},\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{v,s-1}^{0}\end{split}

given by

\begin{split}\overline{I}_{1}(u,v,s)=M(s,\ u)M(s-1,\ v),\end{split}

where
M(a,\ b)
denotes the total number of integer solution
x_{1}+x_{2}+\cdots+x_{a}=b
such that
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{a})\in S_{b,a}^{0}
. The number is given by

\begin{split}M(a,\ b)={b-1\choose a-1}.\end{split}

See, e.g.
Charalambides, 2002
.

Definition 4.2
.

For
0<q\leq 1
, we define

\begin{split}I_{q,0,k_{2}}(m,r,s)={\sum_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}}}\ \sum_{y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s}},\ \end{split}

where the summation is over all integers
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s},
and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1}
satisfying

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{m,s}^{0},\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{r,s-1}^{k_{2}-1}.\end{split}

The following gives a recurrence relation useful for the computation of
I_{q,0,k_{2}}(m,r,s)
.

Lemma 4.2
.

I_{q,0,k_{2}}(m,r,s)
obeys the following recurrence relation.

\begin{split}I&{}_{q,0,k_{2}}(m,r,s)\\
&=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\sum_{b=1}^{k_{2}-1}\sum_{a=1}^{m-(s-1)}q^{ra}I_{q,0,k_{2}}(m-a,r-b,s-1)\\
+\sum_{b=k_{2}}^{r-(s-2)}\sum_{a=1}^{m-(s-1)}q^{ra}\overline{I}_{q}(m-a,r-b,s-1),&\text{for}\ s>1,\ s\leq m\\
&\text{and}\ (s-2)+k_{2}\leq r\\
0,&\text{otherwise.}\\
\end{array}\right.\end{split}

Proof.

For
s>1
,
s\leq m
and
(s-2)+k_{2}\leq r
, we observe that
x_{s}
may assume any value
1,\ldots,m-(s-1)
, then
I_{q,0,k_{2}}(m,r,s)
can be written as

\begin{split}I&{}_{q,0,k_{2}}(m,r,s)\\
=&\sum_{x_{s}=1}^{m-(s-1)}{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{m-x_{s},s-1}^{0}\end{subarray}}}\ {\sum_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{r,s-1}^{k_{2}-1}}}q^{rx_{s}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2})x_{s-1}}.\end{split}

Similarly, we observe that since
y_{s-1}
can assume the values
1,\ldots,k_{2}-1
, then
I_{q,0,k_{2}}(m,r,s)
can be rewritten as

\begin{split}I&{}_{q,0,k_{2}}(m,r,s)\\
=&\sum_{y_{s-1}=1}^{k_{2}-1}\sum_{x_{s}=1}^{m-(s-1)}{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{m-x_{s},s-1}^{0}\end{subarray}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-2})\in S_{r-y_{s-1},s-2}^{k_{2}-1}}}\hskip-8.5359ptq^{rx_{s}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2})x_{s-1}}\\
&+\sum_{y_{s-1}=k_{2}}^{r-(s-2)}\sum_{x_{s}=1}^{m-(s-1)}{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{m-x_{s},s-1}^{0}\end{subarray}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-2})\in S_{r-y_{s-1},s-2}^{0}}}\hskip-8.5359ptq^{rx_{s}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2})x_{s-1}}\\
=&\sum_{b=1}^{k_{2}-1}\sum_{a=1}^{m-(s-1)}q^{ra}I_{q,0,k_{2}}(m-a,r-b,s-1)+\sum_{b=k_{2}}^{r-(s-2)}\sum_{a=1}^{m-(s-1)}q^{ra}\overline{I}_{q}(m-a,r-b,s-1).\end{split}

The other cases are obvious and thus the proof is completed.
∎

Remark 17
.

We observe that
I_{1,0,k_{2}}(m,r,s)
is the number of integer solutions
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})
and
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})
of

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{m,s}^{0},\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{r,s-1}^{k_{2}-1}\end{split}

given by

\begin{split}I_{1,0,k_{2}}(m,r,s)=M(s,\ m)R(s-1,\ k_{2},\ r),\end{split}

where
M(a,\ b)
denotes the total number of integer solution
x_{1}+x_{2}+\cdots+x_{a}=b
such that
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{a})\in S_{b,a}^{0}
. The number is given by

\begin{split}M(a,\ b)={b-1\choose a-1}.\end{split}

where
R(a,\ b,\ c)
denotes the total number of integer solution
y_{1}+x_{2}+\cdots+y_{a}=c
such that
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{a})\in S_{r,a}^{k_{2}}
. The number is given by

\begin{split}R(a,\ b,\ c)=\sum_{j=1}^{min\left(a,\ \left[\frac{c-a}{b-1}\right]\right)}(-1)^{j+1}{a\choose j}{c-j(b-1)-1\choose a-1}.\end{split}

See, e.g.
Charalambides, 2002
.

Definition 4.3
.

For
0<q\leq 1
, we define

\begin{split}\overline{J}_{q}(u,v,s)={\sum_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}}}\ \sum_{y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s})x_{s}},\ \end{split}

where the summation is over all integers
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s},
and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}
satisfying

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{u,s}^{0},\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{v,s}^{0}.\end{split}

The following gives a recurrence relation useful for the computation of
\overline{J}_{q}(u,v,s)
.

Lemma 4.3
.

\overline{J}_{q}(u,v,s)
obeys the following recurrence relation.

\begin{split}\overline{J}&{}_{q}(u,v,s)\\
&=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\sum_{b=1}^{v-(s-1)}\sum_{a=1}^{u-(s-1)}q^{va}\overline{J}_{q}(u-a,v-b,s-1),&\text{for}\ s>1,\ s\leq u\ \text{and}\ s\leq v\\
1,&\text{for}\ s=1,\ 1\leq u,\ \text{and}\ 1\leq v\\
0,&\text{otherwise.}\\
\end{array}\right.\end{split}

Proof.

For
s>1
,
s\leq u
and
s\leq v
, we observe that
x_{s}
may assume any value
1,\ldots,u-(s-1)
, then
\overline{J}_{q}(u,v,s)
can be written as

\begin{split}\overline{J}&{}_{q}(u,v,s)\\
=&\sum_{x_{s}=1}^{u-(s-1)}{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{u-x_{s},s-1}^{0}\end{subarray}}}\ {\sum_{(y_{1},\ \ldots,\ y_{s})\in S_{v,s}^{0}}}q^{vx_{s}}\ \ q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s})x_{s}}.\end{split}

Similarly, we observe that since
y_{s}
can assume the values
1,\ldots,v-(s-1)
, then
\overline{J}_{q}(u,v,s)
can be rewritten as

\begin{split}\overline{J}&{}_{q}(u,v,s)\\
=&\sum_{y_{s}=1}^{v-(s-1)}\sum_{x_{s}=1}^{u-(s-1)}{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{u-x_{s},s-1}^{0}\end{subarray}}}\ {\sum_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{v-y_{s},s-1}^{0}}}q^{vx_{s}}\ q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s})x_{s}}\\
=&\sum_{b=1}^{v-(s-1)}\sum_{a=1}^{u-(s-1)}q^{va}\overline{J}_{q}(u-a,v-b,s-1).\end{split}

The other cases are obvious and thus the proof is completed.
∎

Remark 18
.

We observe that
\overline{J}_{1}(u,v,s)
is the number of integer solutions
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})
and
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})
of

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{u,s}^{0},\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{v,s}^{0}\end{split}

given by
