\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(&S_{n}=n-k_{2}\ \wedge\ F_{n}=k_{2}\ \wedge\ X_{n}=0\Big)\\
&=\theta^{n-k_{2}}\ {\prod_{j=1}^{k_{2}}}\ (1-\theta q^{j-1})\ \sum_{s=1}^{k_{2}}\bigg[\overline{K}_{q}(n-k_{2},\ k_{2},\ s)+\overline{L}_{q}(n-k_{2},\ k_{2}\ s)\bigg],\\
\end{split}

where

\begin{split}\overline{K}_{q}(n-k_{2}&,\ k_{2},\ s)\\
&=\sum_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{n-k_{2},s-1}^{0}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{k_{2},s}^{0}}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s-1}},\end{split}

and

\begin{split}\overline{L}_{q}(n-k_{2}&,\ k_{2},\ s)\\
&=\sum_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{n-k_{2},s}^{0}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{k_{2},s}^{0}}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s}}.\end{split}

Therefore we can compute the probability of the event
\Big\{\overline{W}_{L}^{(0)}=n\Big\}
as follows.

\begin{split}\overline{P}_{q,S}^{(0)}(n)=\theta^{n-k_{2}}\ {\prod_{j=1}^{k_{2}}}\ (1-\theta q^{j-1})\ \sum_{s=1}^{k_{2}}\bigg[\overline{K}_{q}(n-k_{2},\ k_{2},\ s)+\overline{L}_{q}(n-k_{2},\ k_{2}\ s)\bigg].\\
\end{split}

Thus proof is completed.
∎

It is worth mentioning here that the PMF
\overline{f}_{q,S}(n;\theta)
approaches the probability function of the sooner waiting time distribution of order
(k_{1},k_{2})

in the limit as
q
tends to 1 when a frequency quota is imposed on runs of successes and failures of IID model. The details are presented in the following remark.

Remark 27
.

For
q=1
, the PMF
\overline{f}_{q,S}(n;\theta)
reduces to the PMF
\overline{f}_{S}(n;\theta)=P_{\theta}(\overline{W}_{S}=n)
for
n=\text{min}(k_{1},k_{2}),\ldots,k_{1}+k_{2}-1
is given by

\begin{split}P(\overline{W}_{S}=n)=\overline{P}_{S}^{(1)}(n)+\overline{P}_{S}^{(0)}(n),\end{split}

where

\begin{split}\overline{P}_{S}^{(1)}(n)=\theta^{k_{1}}\ (1-\theta)^{n-k_{1}}\ \sum_{s=1}^{k_{1}}M(s,\ k_{1})\bigg[M(s-1,\ n-k_{1})+M(s,\ n-k_{1})\bigg].\\
\end{split}

and

\begin{split}\overline{P}_{S}^{(0)}(n)=\theta^{n-k_{2}}(1-\theta)^{k_{2}}\sum_{s=1}^{k_{2}}\bigg[M(s-1,\ n-k_{2})+M(s,\ n-k_{2})\bigg]M(s,\ k_{2}),\end{split}

where

\begin{split}M(a,\ b)={b-1\choose a-1}.\end{split}

See, e.g.
Charalambides, 2002
.

Theorem 5.2
.

The PMF
P_{q,\theta}(\overline{W}_{S}=n)
for
n=\text{min}(k_{1},k_{2}),\ldots,k_{1}+k_{2}-1
is given by

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}(\overline{W}_{S}=n)&=\sum_{x=n-k_{2}}^{k_{1}-1}{n-1\brack x}_{q}\theta^{x}\prod_{i=1}^{n-1-x}(1-\theta q^{i-1})-\sum_{x=n+1-k_{2}}^{k_{1}-1}{n\brack x}_{q}\theta^{x}\prod_{i=1}^{n-x}(1-\theta q^{i-1}).\end{split}

Proof.

It follows from the definition of
\overline{W}_{S}
that

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}(\overline{W}_{S}\geq n)&=P_{q,\theta}(\overline{W}_{S}^{(1)}\geq n,\ \overline{W}_{S}^{(0)}\geq n)\\
&=P_{q,\theta}(F_{n-1}\leq k_{2}-1,\ S_{n-1}\leq k_{1}-1)\\
&=P_{q,\theta}(n-k_{2}\leq S_{n-1}\leq k_{1}-1)\\
&=\sum_{x=n-k_{2}}^{k_{1}-1}P_{q,\theta}(S_{n-1}=x).\\
\end{split}

Thus the proof is completed by noting that

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}(\overline{W}_{S}=n)&=P_{q,\theta}(\overline{W}_{S}\geq n)-P_{q,\theta}(W_{S}\geq n+1)\\
&=\sum_{x=n-k_{2}}^{k_{1}-1}P_{q,\theta}(S_{n-1}=x)-\sum_{m=n+1-k_{2}}^{k_{1}-1}P_{q,\theta}(S_{n}=x)\\
&=\sum_{x=n-k_{2}}^{k_{1}-1}{n-1\brack x}_{q}\theta^{x}\prod_{i=1}^{n-1-x}(1-\theta q^{i-1})-\sum_{x=n+1-k_{2}}^{k_{1}-1}{n\brack x}_{q}\theta^{x}\prod_{i=1}^{n-x}(1-\theta q^{i-1}).\end{split}

∎

It is worth mentioning here that the PMF
\overline{f}_{q,S}(n;\theta)
approaches the probability function of the sooner waiting time distribution of order
(k_{1},k_{2})

in the limit as
q
tends to 1 when a frequency quota is imposed on runs of successes and failures of IID model. The details are presented in the following remark.

Remark 28
.

For
q=1
, the PMF
\overline{f}_{q,S}(n;\theta)
reduces to the PMF
\overline{f}_{S}(n;\theta)=P_{\theta}(\overline{W}_{S}=n)
for
n=\text{min}(k_{1},k_{2}),\ldots,k_{1}+k_{2}-1
is given by

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}(\overline{W}_{S}=n)&=\sum_{x=n-k_{2}}^{k_{1}-1}\binom{n-1}{x}\theta^{x}(1-\theta)^{n-1-x}-\sum_{x=n+1-k_{2}}^{k_{1}-1}\binom{n}{x}\theta^{x}(1-\theta)^{n-x}.\end{split}

5.2
Later waiting time

The problem of waiting time that will be discussed in this section is one of the ’later cases’ and it emerges when a frequency quota on successes and failures are imposed. More specifically, Binary (zero and one) trials with probability of ones varying according to a geometric rule, are performed sequentially until
k_{1}
successes in total or
k_{2}
failures in total are observed, whichever event gets observed later.
Let random variable
\widehat{W}_{L}
denote the waiting time until both
k_{1}
successes in total and
k_{2}
failures in total have observed, whichever event gets observed later. Let random variable
\widehat{W}_{L}
denote the waiting time until both
k_{1}
successes in total and
k_{2}
failures in total have observed, whichever event gets observed later.
The probability function of the
q
-later waiting time distribution impose a frequency quotas on both successes and failures is obtained by the following theorem. It is evident that

P_{q,\theta}(\widehat{W}_{L}=n)=0\ \text{for}\ n<k_{1}+k_{2}

and so we shall focus on determining the probability mass function for
n\geq k_{1}+k_{2}
.

Theorem 5.3
.

The PMF
P_{q,\theta}(\overline{W}_{L}=n)
satisfies

\begin{split}P(\overline{W}_{L}=n)=\overline{P}_{q,L}^{(1)}(n)+\overline{P}_{q,L}^{(0)}(n),\ n\geq k_{1}+k_{2},\end{split}

where

\begin{split}\overline{P}_{q,L}^{(1)}(n)=\theta^{k_{1}}\ {\prod_{j=1}^{n-k_{1}}}\ (1-\theta q^{j-1})\ \sum_{s=1}^{k_{1}}\bigg[\overline{I}_{q}(k_{1},\ n-k_{1},\ s)+\overline{J}_{q}(k_{1},\ n-k_{1},\ s)\bigg]\\
\end{split}

and

\begin{split}\overline{P}_{q,L}^{(0)}(n)=\theta^{n-k_{2}}\ {\prod_{j=1}^{k_{2}}}\ (1-\theta q^{j-1})\ \sum_{s=1}^{k_{2}}\bigg[\overline{K}_{q}(n-k_{2},\ k_{2},\ s)+\overline{L}_{q}(n-k_{2},\ k_{2}\ s)\bigg].\\
\end{split}

Proof.

We start with the study of
\overline{P}_{q,L}^{(1)}(n)
. From now on we assume
n\geq k_{1}+k_{2},
and we can write
\overline{P}_{q,L}^{(1)}(n)
as follows.

\begin{split}\overline{P}_{q,L}^{(1)}(n)&=P_{q,\theta}\left(S_{n}=k_{1}\ \wedge\ F_{n}=n-k_{1}\ \wedge\ X_{n}=1\right).\\
\end{split}

We are going to focus on the event
\left\{S_{n}=k_{1}\ \wedge\ F_{n}=n-k_{1}\ \wedge\ X_{n}=1\right\}
. A typical element of the event
\left\{S_{n}=k_{1}\ \wedge\ F_{n}=n-k_{1}\ \wedge\ X_{n}=1\right\}
is an ordered sequence which consists of
k_{1}
successes and
n-k_{1}
failures. The number of these sequences can be derived as follows. First we will distribute the
k_{1}
successes. Let
s

(1\leq s\leq k_{1})
be the number of runs of successes in the event
\left\{S_{n}=k_{1}\ \wedge\ F_{n}=n-k_{1}\ \wedge\ X_{n}=1\right\}
. We divide into two cases: starting with a success run or starting with a failure run. Thus, we distinguish between two types of sequences in the event

\Big\{S_{n}=k_{1}\ \wedge\ F_{n}=n-k_{1}\ \wedge\ X_{n}=1\Big\},

respectively named
(s,\ s-1)
-type and
(s,\ s)
-type, which are defined as follows.

\begin{split}(s,\ s-1)\text{-type}\ :&\quad\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{1}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{2}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{2}}\mid\ldots\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{s-1}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s-1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{s}},\end{split}

with
n-k_{1}

0
’s and
k_{1}

1
’s, where
x_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s)
represents a length of run of
1
’s and
y_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s-1)
represents the length of a run of
0
’s. Here all of
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s},
and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1}
are integers, and they satisfy

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{k_{1},s}^{0},\text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{n-k_{1},s-1}^{0},\end{split}

\begin{split}(s,\ s)\text{-type}\ :&\quad\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{1}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{2}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{2}}\mid\ldots\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s-1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{s-1}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{s}},\\
\end{split}

with
n-k_{1}

0
’s and
k_{1}

1
’s, where
x_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s)
represents a length of run of
1
’s and
y_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s)
represents the length of a run of
0
’s. And all integers
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}
, and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}
satisfy the conditions

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{k_{1},s}^{0},\text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{n-k_{1},s}^{0},\end{split}

Then the probability of the event
\Big\{S_{n}=k_{1}\ \wedge\ F_{n}=n-k_{1}\ \wedge\ X_{n}=1\Big\}
is given by

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(S_{n}=&k_{1}\ \wedge\ F_{n}=n-k_{1}\ \wedge\ X_{n}=1\Big)=\\
\sum_{s=1}^{k_{1}}\Bigg[\bigg\{&\sum_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{k_{1},s}^{0}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{n-k_{1},s-1}^{0}}}\big(\theta q^{0}\big)^{x_{1}}\big(1-\theta q^{0}\big)\cdots(1-\theta q^{y_{1}-1})\times\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}}\Big)^{x_{2}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}-1}\big)\times\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\vdots\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2}}\Big)^{x_{s-1}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}-1}\big)\times\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}}\Big)^{x_{s}}\bigg\}+\\
\end{split}

\begin{split}\quad\bigg\{&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}\in S_{k_{1},s}^{0}\end{subarray}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{n-k_{1},s}^{0}}}\big(1-\theta q^{0}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}-1}\big)\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}}\Big)^{x_{1}}\times\\
&\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}-1}\big)\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\Big)^{x_{2}}\times\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\vdots\\
&\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}-1}\big)\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}}\Big)^{x_{s}}\bigg\}\Bigg].\\
\end{split}

Using simple exponentiation algebra arguments to simplify,

\begin{split}&P_{q,\theta}\Big(S_{n}=k_{1}\ \wedge\ F_{n}=n-k_{1}\ \wedge\ X_{n}=1\Big)=\\
&\theta^{k_{1}}{\prod_{j=1}^{n-k_{1}}}\ (1-\theta q^{j-1})\times\\
&\sum_{s=1}^{k_{1}}\Bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{k_{1},s}^{0}\end{subarray}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{n-k_{1},s-1}^{0}}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s}}+\\
&\quad\quad\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{k_{1},s}^{0}\end{subarray}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{(y_{1},\ \ldots,\ y_{s})\in S_{n-k_{1},s}^{0}}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s})x_{s}}\Bigg].\\
\end{split}

Using Lemma
4.1
and Lemma
4.3
, we can rewrite as follows.

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(&S_{n}=k_{1}\ \wedge\ F_{n}=n-k_{1}\ \wedge\ X_{n}=1\Big)\\
&=\theta^{k_{1}}\ {\prod_{j=1}^{n-k_{1}}}\ (1-\theta q^{j-1})\ \sum_{s=1}^{k_{1}}\bigg[\overline{I}_{q}(k_{1},\ n-k_{1},\ s)+\overline{J}_{q}(k_{1},\ n-k_{1},\ s)\bigg],\\
\end{split}

where

\begin{split}\overline{I}_{q}(k_{1}&,\ n-k_{1},\ s)\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{k_{1},s}^{0}\end{subarray}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{n-k_{1},s-1}^{0}}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s}},\end{split}

and

\begin{split}\overline{J}_{q}(k_{1}&,\ n-k_{1},\ s)\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{k_{1},s}^{0}\end{subarray}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{n-k_{1},s}^{0}}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s})x_{s}}.\end{split}

Therefore we can compute the probability of the event
\overline{W}_{L}^{(1)}=n
as follows.

\begin{split}\overline{P}_{q,L}^{(1)}(n)=\theta^{k_{1}}\ {\prod_{j=1}^{n-k_{1}}}\ (1-\theta q^{j-1})\ \sum_{s=1}^{k_{1}}\bigg[\overline{I}_{q}(k_{1},\ n-k_{1},\ s)+\overline{J}_{q}(k_{1},\ n-k_{1},\ s)\bigg].\\
\end{split}

We start with the study of
\overline{P}_{q,L}^{(0)}(n)
. From now on we assume
n\geq k_{1}+k_{2},
and we can write
\overline{P}_{q,L}^{(0)}(n)
as follows.

\begin{split}\overline{P}_{q,L}^{(0)}(n)&=P_{q,\theta}\Big(S_{n}=n-k_{2}\ \wedge\ F_{n}=k_{2}\ \wedge\ X_{n}=0\Big).\\
\end{split}

We are going to focus on the event
\left\{S_{n}=n-k_{2}\ \wedge\ F_{n}=k_{2}\ \wedge\ X_{n}=0\right\}
. A typical element of the event
\left\{S_{n}=n-k_{2}\ \wedge\ F_{n}=k_{2}\ \wedge\ X_{n}=0\right\}
is an ordered sequence which consists of
n-k_{2}
successes and
k_{2}
failures. The number of these sequences can be derived as follows. First we will distribute the
k_{2}
failures. Let
s

(1\leq s\leq k_{2})
be the number of runs of failures in the event
\Big\{S_{n}=n-k_{2}\ \wedge\ F_{n}=k_{2}\ \wedge\ X_{n}=0\Big\}
. We divide into two cases: starting with a success run or starting with a failure run. Thus, we distinguish between two types of sequences in the event

\Big\{S_{n}=n-k_{2}\ \wedge\ F_{n}=k_{2}\ \wedge\ X_{n}=0\Big\},

respectively named
(s-1,\ s)
-type and
(s,\ s)
-type, which are defined as follows.

\begin{split}(s-1,\ s)\text{-type}\ :&\quad\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{1}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{2}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{2}}\mid\ldots\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s-1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{s-1}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s}},\\
\end{split}

with
k_{2}

0
’s and
n-k_{2}

1
’s, where
x_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s-1)
represents a length of run of
1
’s and
y_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s)
represents the length of a run of
0
’s. And all integers
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1}
, and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}
satisfy the conditions

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{n-k_{2},s-1}^{0}\mbox{\ and\ }\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{k_{2},s}^{0},\end{split}

\begin{split}(s,\ s)\text{-type}\ :&\quad\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{1}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{2}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{2}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{3}}\mid\ldots\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s-1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{s}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s}},\end{split}

with
k_{2}

0
’s and
n-k_{2}

1
’s, where
x_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s)
represents a length of run of
1
’s and
y_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s)
represents the length of a run of
0
’s. Here all of
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1},
and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}
are integers, and they satisfy

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{n-k_{2},s}^{0}\mbox{\ and\ }\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{k_{2},s}^{0},\end{split}

Then the probability of the event
\Big\{S_{n}=n-k_{2}\ \wedge\ F_{n}=k_{2}\ \wedge\ X_{n}=0\Big\}
is given by

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(S_{n}=&n-k_{2}\ \wedge\ F_{n}=k_{2}\ \wedge\ X_{n}=0\Big)=\\
\sum_{s=1}^{k_{1}}\Bigg[\bigg\{&\sum_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{n-k_{2},s-1}^{0}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{k_{2},s}^{0}}}\big(1-\theta q^{0}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}-1}\big)\times\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}}\Big)^{x_{1}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}-1}\big)\times\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\Big)^{x_{2}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}+y_{3}-1}\big)\times\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\vdots\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}}\Big)^{x_{s-1}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}-1}\big)\bigg\}+\\
\end{split}

\begin{split}\quad\quad\quad\quad\bigg\{&\sum_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{n-k_{2},s}^{0}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{k_{2},s}^{0}}}\big(\theta q^{0}\big)^{x_{1}}\big(1-\theta q^{0}\big)\cdots(1-\theta q^{y_{1}-1})\times\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}}\Big)^{x_{2}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}-1}\big)\times\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\Big)^{x_{3}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}+y_{3}-1}\big)\times\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\vdots\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}}\Big)^{x_{s}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}-1}\big)\bigg\}\Bigg].\\
\end{split}

Using simple exponentiation algebra arguments to simplify,

\begin{split}&P_{q,\theta}\Big(S_{n}=n-k_{2}\ \wedge\ F_{n}=k_{2}\ \wedge\ X_{n}=0\Big)=\\
&\theta^{n-k_{2}}{\prod_{j=1}^{k_{2}}}\ (1-\theta q^{j-1})\times\\
&\sum_{s=1}^{k_{2}}\Bigg[\sum_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{n-k_{2},s-1}^{0}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{k_{2},s}^{0}}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s-1}}+\\
&\quad\quad\sum_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{n-k_{2},s}^{0}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{k_{2},s}^{0}}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s}}\Bigg].\\
\end{split}

Using Lemma
4.5
and Lemma
4.7
, we can rewrite as follows.

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(&S_{n}=n-k_{2}\ \wedge\ F_{n}=k_{2}\ \wedge\ X_{n}=0\Big)\\
&=\theta^{n-k_{2}}\ {\prod_{j=1}^{k_{2}}}\ (1-\theta q^{j-1})\ \sum_{s=1}^{k_{2}}\bigg[\overline{K}_{q}(n-k_{2},\ k_{2},\ s)+\overline{L}_{q}(n-k_{2},\ k_{2}\ s)\bigg],\\
\end{split}

where

\begin{split}\overline{K}_{q}(n-k_{2}&,\ k_{2},\ s)\\
&=\sum_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{n-k_{2},s-1}^{0}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{k_{2},s}^{0}}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s-1}},\end{split}

and

\begin{split}\overline{L}_{q}(n-k_{2}&,\ k_{2},\ s)\\
&=\sum_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{n-k_{2},s}^{0}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{k_{2},s}^{0}}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s}}.\end{split}

Therefore we can compute the probability of the event
\overline{W}_{L}^{(0)}=n
as follows.

\begin{split}\overline{P}_{q,L}^{(0)}(n)=\theta^{n-k_{2}}\ {\prod_{j=1}^{k_{2}}}\ (1-\theta q^{j-1})\ \sum_{s=1}^{k_{2}}\bigg[\overline{K}_{q}(n-k_{2},\ k_{2},\ s)+\overline{L}_{q}(n-k_{2},\ k_{2}\ s)\bigg].\\
\end{split}

Thus proof is completed.
∎

It is worth mentioning here that the PMF
f_{q,L}(n;\theta)
approaches the probability function of the later waiting time distribution of order
(k_{1},k_{2})

in the limit as
q
tends to 1 when a frequency quota is imposed on runs of successes and failures of IID model. The details are presented in the following remark.

Remark 29
.

For
q=1
, the PMF
\overline{f}_{q,L}(n;\theta)
reduces to the PMF
\overline{f}_{L}(n;\theta)=P_{\theta}(\overline{W}_{L}=n)
for
n\geq k_{1}+k_{2}
is given by

\begin{split}P(\overline{W}_{L}=n)=\overline{P}_{L}^{(1)}(n)+\overline{P}_{L}^{(0)}(n),\ n\geq k_{1}+k_{2},\end{split}

where

\begin{split}\overline{P}_{L}^{(1)}(n)=\theta^{k_{1}}\ (1-\theta)^{n-k_{1}}\ \sum_{s=1}^{k_{1}}M(s,\ k_{1})\bigg[M(s-1,\ n-k_{1})+M(s,\ n-k_{1})\bigg].\\
\end{split}

and

\begin{split}\overline{P}_{L}^{(0)}(n)=\theta^{n-k_{2}}(1-\theta)^{k_{2}}\sum_{s=1}^{k_{2}}\bigg[M(s-1,\ n-k_{2})+M(s,\ n-k_{2})\bigg]M(s,\ k_{2}),\end{split}

where

M(a,\ b)=\binom{b-1}{a-1}.

See, e.g.
Charalambides, 2002
.

6
Longest run for
q
-binary trials

In this section, we shall study of the distribution of the lengths of the longest success run. More specifically binary (zero and one) trials with probability of ones varing according to a geometric rule. First, we obtain the exact PMF of the distribution of
\left(L_{n}^{(1)}=k\right)
. Next, we obtain the exact PMF of the distribution of
\left(L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k\right)
. The result are derived by means of enumerative combinatorics.

6.1
Closed expression for the PMF of
\left(L_{n}^{(1)}=k\right)

We now make some useful Definition and Lemma for the proofs of Theorem in the sequel.

Definition 6.1
.

For
0<q\leq 1
, we define

\begin{split}U_{q}^{k}(r,s,t)={\sum_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{r}}}q^{x_{2}+2x_{3}+\cdots+(r-1)x_{r}},\ \end{split}

where the summation is over all integers
x_{1},\ldots,x_{r}
satisfying

\begin{split}0\leq x_{j}\leq k\ \text{for}\ j=1,\ldots,r,\end{split}

\begin{split}x_{1}+\cdots+x_{r}=s,\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}\delta_{y_{1},k}+\cdots+\delta_{y_{r},k}=t.\end{split}

The following gives a recurrence relation useful for the computation of
U_{q}^{k}(r,s,t)
.

Lemma 6.1
.

For
0<q\leq 1
,
U_{q}^{k}(r,s,t)
obeys the following recurrence relation.

\begin{split}U_{q}&{}^{k}(r,s,t)\\
&=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\sum_{a=0}^{k-1}q^{a(r-1)}U_{q}^{k}(r-1,s-a,t)+\\
\quad\quad\ q^{k(r-1)}U_{q}^{k}(r-1,s-k,t-1)&\text{for}\ r>1,\ tk\leq s\leq rk\ \text{and}\ t\leq r<s,\\
1&\text{for}\ r=1,\ s=k,\ \text{and}\ t=1,\\
&\text{or}\ r=1,\ 0\leq s<k,\ \text{and}\ t=0,\text{or}\\
0&\text{otherwise.}\\
\end{array}\right.\end{split}

Proof.

For
r>1
,
tk\leq s\leq rk
and
t\leq r<s
, we observe that since
x_{r}
can assume the values
1,\ldots,k
, then
U_{q}^{k}(r,s,t)
can be written as follows.

\begin{split}U_{q}^{k}(r,s,t)=&\sum_{x_{r}=0}^{k-1}\ q^{x_{r}(r-1)}{\hskip 14.22636pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}+\cdots+x_{s-1}=m-x_{s}\\
x_{1},\ldots,x_{r-1}\in\{0,1,\ldots,k\}\\
\delta_{y_{1},k}+\cdots+\delta_{y_{r},k}=t\end{subarray}}}\quad q^{x_{2}+2x_{3}+\cdots+(r-2)x_{r-1}}\\
&+q^{k(r-1)}{\hskip 14.22636pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}+\cdots+x_{s-1}=m-x_{s}\\
x_{1},\ldots,x_{r-1}\in\{0,1,\ldots,k\}\\
\delta_{y_{1},k}+\cdots+\delta_{y_{r},k}=t-1\end{subarray}}}\quad\ q^{x_{2}+2x_{3}+\cdots+(r-2)x_{r-1}}\\
\end{split}

Using simple algebraic arguments to simplify, then
U_{q}^{k}(r,s,t)
can be rewritten as follows.

\begin{split}U_{q}^{k}(r,s,t)=&\sum_{a=0}^{k-1}q^{a(r-1)}U_{q}^{k}(r-1,s-a,t)+q^{k(r-1)}U_{q}^{k}(r-1,s-k,t-1).\end{split}

The other cases are obvious and thus the proof is completed.
∎

Remark 30
.

We observe that
U_{1}^{k}(r,s,t)
is the number of integer solutions
(x_{1},\ \ldots,\ x_{r})
of

\begin{split}0\leq x_{j}\leq k\ \text{for}\ j=1,\ldots,r,\end{split}

\begin{split}x_{1}+\cdots+x_{r}=s,\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}\delta_{y_{1},k}+\cdots+\delta_{y_{r},k}=t.\end{split}

which is

\begin{split}U_{1}^{k}(r,s,t)={r\choose t}C(s-tk,\ r-t,\ k-1),\end{split}

denotes the number of ways of distributing
s
identical balls into
r
different cells, so that each of
t
of them receives
k
balls is occupied,
remaining
s-tk
balls placed in the remaining
r-t
cells with no cell receiving more than
k-1
balls.

where
C(a,\ b,\ c)
denotes the total number of integer solutions
x_{1}+x_{2}+\cdots+x_{b}=a
such that
0\leq x_{i}\leq c
for
i=1,2,\ldots,b
. Alternatively, it is the number of ways of distributing
a
identical balls into
b
different cells , so that each cell is occupied by at most
c
balls. The number can be expressed as

\begin{split}C(a,\ b,\ c)=\sum_{j=0}^{b}(-1)^{j}{b\choose j}{a-(c+1)j+b-1\choose b-1}.\end{split}

if
a>0
:
1
if
a=0
: and
0
otherwise (See, e.g.
Charalambides, 2002
.

We shall study of the joint distribution of
\left(L_{n}^{(1)}=k\right)
. The probability function of the
P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}=k\Big)
is obtained by the following theorem. It is evident that

P_{q,\theta}\left(L_{n}^{(1)}=0\right)={\prod_{j=1}^{n}}\left(1-\theta q^{j-1}\right)\ \text{for}\ k=0

and so we shall focus on determining the probability mass function for
1\leq k\leq n
.

Theorem 6.1
.

The PMF
P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}=k\Big)
for
k=1,\ldots,n,

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}=k\Big)=&\sum_{y=[n/(k+1)]}^{n-k}\theta^{n-y}\ {\prod_{j=1}^{y}}\ \left(1-\theta q^{j-1}\right)\ \sum_{i=1}^{y+1}U_{q}^{k}(y+1,\ n-y,\ i).\end{split}

Proof.

We partition the event
\left\{L_{n}^{(1)}=k\right\}
into disjoint events given by
F_{n}=y,
for
y=[n/(k+1)],\ldots,n-k
. Adding the probabilities we have

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}=k\Big)=\sum_{y=[n/(k+1)]}^{n-k}P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}=k\ \wedge\ F_{n}=y\Big).\\
\end{split}

We are going to focus on the event
\left\{L_{n}^{(1)}=k\ \wedge\ F_{n}=y\right\}
. A typical element of the event
\Big\{L_{n}^{(1)}=k\ \wedge\ F_{n}=y\Big\}
is an ordered sequence which consists of
n-y
successes and
y
failures such that the length of the longest success run is equal to
k
. The number of these sequences can be derived as follows. First we will distribute the
y
failures which form
y+1
cells. Next, we will distribute the
n-y
successes in the
y+1
distinguishable cells and they satisfy the conditions,
i
(
i=1,\ldots,\text{min}\{y+1,[(n-y)/k]\})
specified cells among the
y+1
cells occupied exactly
k
successes balls. And the remaining
n-y-ik
successes can be distributed in the remaining
y+1-i
cells with no cell receiving more than
k-1
successes. Thus, the event
\left\{L_{n}^{(1)}=k\ \wedge\ F_{n}=y\right\}
is defined as follows.

\begin{split}\underbrace{1\ldots 1}_{0\leq x_{1}\leq k}0\underbrace{1\ldots 1}_{0\leq x_{2}\leq k}0\underbrace{1\ldots 1}_{0\leq x_{3}\leq k}0\ldots 0\underbrace{1\ldots 1}_{0\leq x_{y}\leq k}0\underbrace{1\ldots 1}_{0\leq x_{y+1}\leq k},\\
\end{split}

with
y

0
’s and
n-y

1
’s, where
x_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,y+1
represents a length of run of
1
’s . And all integers
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}
satisfy the conditions

\begin{split}0\leq x_{j}\leq k\mbox{\ for\ }j=1,...,y+1,\ x_{1}+\cdots+x_{y+1}=n-y,\mbox{\ and}\end{split}

\begin{split}\delta_{x_{1},k}+\delta_{x_{2},k}+\cdots+\delta_{x_{y+1},k}=i.\end{split}

Then the probability of the event
\left\{L_{n}^{(1)}=k\ \wedge\ F_{n}=y\right\}
is given by

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}=k\ \wedge\ F_{n}=y\Big)&\\
=\sum_{i=1}^{y+1}\Bigg[\bigg\{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}+\cdots+x_{y+1}=n-y\\
x_{1},\ldots,x_{y+1}\in\{0.1,\ldots,k\}\\
\delta_{x_{1},k}+\cdots+\delta_{x_{y+1},k}=i\end{subarray}}&\Big(\theta q^{0}\Big)^{x_{1}}\Big(1-\theta q^{0}\Big)\Big(\theta q^{1}\Big)^{x_{2}}\Big(1-\theta q^{1}\Big)\times\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\vdots\\
&\Big(1-\theta q^{y-2}\Big)\Big(\theta q^{y-1}\Big)^{x_{y}}\Big(1-\theta q^{y-1}\Big)\Big(\theta q^{y}\Big)^{x_{y+1}}\bigg\}\Bigg].\\
\end{split}

Using simple exponentiation algebra arguments to simplify,

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(&L_{n}^{(1)}=k\ \wedge\ F_{n}=y\Big)=\\
&\theta^{n-y}\ {\prod_{j=1}^{y}}\ \left(1-\theta q^{j-1}\right)\ \sum_{i=1}^{y+1}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}+\cdots+x_{y+1}=n-y\\
x_{1},\ldots,x_{y+1}\in\{0,1,\ldots,k\}\\
\delta_{x_{1},k}+\cdots+\delta_{x_{y+1},k}=i\end{subarray}}q^{x_{2}+2y_{3}\cdots+(y-1)x_{y}+yx_{y+1}}.\\
\end{split}

Using Lemma
6.1
, we can rewrite as follows.

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}=k\ \wedge\ F_{n}=y\Big)=\theta^{n-y}\ {\prod_{j=1}^{y}}\ \left(1-\theta q^{j-1}\right)\ \sum_{i=1}^{y+1}U_{q}^{k}(y+1,\ n-y,\ i),\\
\end{split}

where

\begin{split}U_{q}^{k}(y+1,\ n-y,\ i)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}+\cdots+x_{y+1}=n-y\\
x_{1},\ldots,x_{y+1}\in\{0.1,\ldots,k\}\\
\delta_{x_{1},k}+\cdots+\delta_{x_{y+1},k}=i\end{subarray}}q^{x_{2}+2y_{3}\cdots+(y-1)x_{y}+yx_{y+1}}.\end{split}

Therefore we can compute the probability of the event
\left\{L_{n}^{(1)}=k\right\}
as follows.

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}=k\Big)=&\sum_{y=[n/(k+1)]}^{n-k}\theta^{n-y}\ {\prod_{j=1}^{y}}\ \left(1-\theta q^{j-1}\right)\ \sum_{i=1}^{y+1}U_{q}^{k}(y+1,\ n-y,\ i).\\
\end{split}

Thus proof is completed.
∎

It is worth mentioning here that the PMF
P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}=k\Big)
approaches the probability function of
P_{\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}=k\Big)
in the limit as
q
tends to 1 of IID model. The details are presented in the following remark.

Remark 31
.

For
q=1
, the PMF
P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}=k\Big)
reduces to the PMF
P_{\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}=k\Big)
. Then
P_{\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}=0\Big)=(1-\theta)^{n}
and for
1\leq k\leq n
is given by

\begin{split}P_{\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}=k\Big)=&\sum_{y=[n/(k+1)]}^{n-k}\theta^{n-y}(1-\theta)^{y}\ \sum_{i=1}^{y+1}U_{1}^{k}(y+1,\ n-y,\ i)\\
=&\sum_{y=[n/(k+1)]}^{n-k}\theta^{n-y}(1-\theta)^{y}\ \sum_{i=1}^{y+1}{y+1\choose i}C(n-y+ik,\ y+1-i,\ k-1),\end{split}

where

\begin{split}C(a,\ b,\ c)=\sum_{j=0}^{b}(-1)^{j}{b\choose j}{a-(c+1)j+b-1\choose b-1}.\end{split}

if
a>0
:
1
if
a=0
: and
0
otherwise (See, e.g.
Charalambides, 2002
).

6.2
Closed expression for the PMF of
\left(L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k\right)

We now make some useful Definition and Lemma for the proofs of Theorem in the sequel.

Definition 6.2
.

For
0<q\leq 1
, we define

\begin{split}V_{q}(r,s,k)={\sum_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{r}}}q^{x_{2}+2x_{3}+\cdots+(r-1)x_{r}},\ \end{split}

where the summation is over all integers
x_{1},\ldots,x_{r}
satisfying

\begin{split}0\leq x_{j}\leq k\quad\text{for}\quad j=1,\ldots,r,\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}x_{1}+\cdots+x_{r}=s\end{split}

The following gives a recurrence relation useful for the computation of
V_{q}(r,s,k)
.

Lemma 6.2
.

For
0<q\leq 1
,
V_{q}(r,s,k)
obeys the following recurrence relation.

\begin{split}V_{q}&{}^{k}(r,s,k)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\sum_{a=0}^{k}q^{a(r-1)}V_{q}(r-1,s-a,k)&\text{for}\ r>1,\ \text{and}\ 0\leq s\leq kr\\
1&\text{for}\ r=1,\ \text{and}\ 0\leq s\leq k,\ \text{or}\\
0&\text{otherwise.}\\
\end{array}\right.\end{split}

Proof.

For
r>1
and
0\leq s\leq kr
, we observe that since
x_{r}
can assume the values
1,\ldots,k
, then
V_{q}(r,s,k)
can be written as follows.

\begin{split}V_{q}(r,s,k)=&\sum_{x_{r}=0}^{k}\ q^{x_{r}(r-1)}{\hskip 14.22636pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}+\cdots+x_{s-1}=m-x_{s}\\
x_{1},\ldots,x_{r-1}\in\{0,1,\ldots,k\}\end{subarray}}}\quad q^{x_{2}+2x_{3}+\cdots+(r-2)x_{r-1}}\end{split}

Using simple algebraic arguments to simplify, then
V_{q}(r,s,k)
can be rewritten as follows.

\begin{split}V_{q}&(r,s,k)=\sum_{a=0}^{k}q^{a(r-1)}V_{q}(r-1,s-a,k).\end{split}

The other cases are obvious and thus the proof is completed.
∎

Remark 32
.

We observe that
V_{1}(r,s,k)
is the number of integer solutions
(x_{1},\ \ldots,\ x_{r})
of

\begin{split}0\leq x_{j}\leq k\quad\text{for}\quad j=1,\ldots,r,\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}x_{1}+\cdots+x_{r}=s\end{split}

which is

\begin{split}V_{1}(r,s,k)=C(s,\ r,\ k),\end{split}

where
C(a,\ b,\ c)
denotes the total number of integer solutions
x_{1}+x_{2}+\cdots+x_{b}=a
such that
0\leq x_{i}\leq c
for
i=1,2,\ldots,b
. Alternatively, it is the number of ways of distributing
a
identical balls into
b
different cells , so that each cell is occupied by at most
c
balls. The number can be expressed as

\begin{split}C(a,\ b,\ c)=\sum_{j=0}^{b}(-1)^{j}{b\choose j}{a-(c+1)j+b-1\choose b-1}.\end{split}

if
a>0
:
1
if
a=0
: and
0
otherwise (See, e.g.
Charalambides, 2002
.

We shall study of the cumulative distribution function
P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k\Big)
of the length
L_{n}^{(1)}
of the longest success run. The probability function of the
P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k\Big)
is obtained by the following theorem.

Theorem 6.2
.

The PMF
P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k\Big)
is given by

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k\Big)=&\sum_{y=[n/(k+1)]}^{n-k}\theta^{n-y}\ {\prod_{j=1}^{y}}\ \left(1-\theta q^{j-1}\right)\ V_{q}(y+1,\ n-y,\ k).\end{split}

Proof.

We partition the event
\left\{L_{n}^{(1)}=k\right\}
into disjoint events given by
F_{n}=y,
for
y=[n/(k+1)],\ldots,n-k
. Adding the probabilities we have

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}=k\Big)=\sum_{y=[n/(k+1)]}^{n-k}P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}=k\ \wedge\ F_{n}=y\Big).\\
\end{split}

We are going to focus on the event
\left\{L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k\ \wedge\ F_{n}=y\right\}
. A typical element of the event
\Big\{L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k\ \wedge\ F_{n}=y\Big\}
is an ordered sequence which consists of
n-y
successes and
y
failures such that the length of the longest success run is less and equal to
k
. The number of these sequences can be derived as follows. First we will distribute the
y
failures which form
y+1
cells. Next, we will distribute the
n-y
successes in the
y+1
distinguishable cells, so that each cell occupied by at most
k
successes balls. Thus, the event
\left\{L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k\ \wedge\ F_{n}=y\right\}
is defined as follows.

\begin{split}\underbrace{1\ldots 1}_{0\leq x_{1}\leq k}0\underbrace{1\ldots 1}_{0\leq x_{2}\leq k}0\underbrace{1\ldots 1}_{0\leq x_{3}\leq k}0\ldots 0\underbrace{1\ldots 1}_{0\leq x_{y}\leq k}0\underbrace{1\ldots 1}_{0\leq x_{y+1}\leq k},\\
\end{split}

with
y

0
’s and
n-y

1
’s, where
x_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,y+1
represents a length of run of
1
’s . And all integers
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}
satisfy the conditions

\begin{split}0\leq x_{j}\leq k\mbox{\ for\ }j=1,...,y+1,\ \mbox{\ and}\ x_{1}+\cdots+x_{y+1}=n-y,\end{split}

Then the probability of the event
\left\{L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k\ \wedge\ F_{n}=y\right\}
is given by

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k\ \wedge\ F_{n}=y\Big)&\\
=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}+\cdots+x_{y+1}=n-y\\
x_{1},\ldots,x_{y+1}\in\{1,\ldots,k\}\end{subarray}}&\Big(\theta q^{0}\Big)^{x_{1}}\Big(1-\theta q^{0}\Big)\Big(\theta q^{1}\Big)^{x_{2}}\Big(1-\theta q^{1}\Big)\times\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\vdots\\
&\Big(1-\theta q^{y-2}\Big)\Big(\theta q^{y-1}\Big)^{x_{y}}\Big(1-\theta q^{y-1}\Big)\Big(\theta q^{y}\Big)^{x_{y+1}}.\\
\end{split}

Using simple exponentiation algebra arguments to simplify,

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(&L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k\ \wedge\ F_{n}=y\Big)=\\
&\theta^{n-y}\ {\prod_{j=1}^{y}}\ \left(1-\theta q^{j-1}\right)\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}+\cdots+x_{y+1}=n-y\\
x_{1},\ldots,x_{y+1}\in\{0,1,\ldots,k\}\end{subarray}}q^{x_{2}+2y_{3}\cdots+(y-1)x_{y}+yx_{y+1}}.\\
\end{split}

Using Lemma
6.2
, we can rewrite as follows.

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k\ \wedge\ F_{n}=y\Big)=\theta^{n-y}\ \left(1-\theta q^{j-1}\right)\ V_{q}(y+1,\ n-y,\ k),\\
\end{split}

where

\begin{split}V_{q}(y+1,\ n-y,\ k)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}+\cdots+x_{y+1}=n-y\\
x_{1},\ldots,x_{y+1}\in\{0,1,\ldots,k\}\end{subarray}}q^{x_{2}+2y_{3}\cdots+(y-1)x_{y}+yx_{y+1}}.\end{split}

Therefore we can compute the probability of the event
\left\{L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k\right\}
as follows.

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k\Big)=&\sum_{y=[n/(k+1)]}^{n}\theta^{n-y}\ {\prod_{j=1}^{y}}\ \left(1-\theta q^{j-1}\right)\ V_{q}(y+1,\ n-y,\ k).\\
\end{split}

Thus proof is completed.
∎

It is worth mentioning here that the PMF
P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k\Big)
approaches the probability function of
P_{\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k\Big)
in the limit as
q
tends to 1 of IID model. The details are presented in the following remark.

Remark 33
.

For
q=1
, the PMF
P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k\Big)
reduces to the PMF
P_{\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k\Big)
is given by

\begin{split}P_{\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k\Big)=&\sum_{y=[n/(k+1)]}^{n}\theta^{n-y}(1-\theta)^{y}\ V_{1}(y+1,\ n-y,\ k)\\
=&\sum_{y=[n/(k+1)]}^{n}\theta^{n-y}(1-\theta)^{y}\ C(n-y,\ y+1,\ k),\end{split}

where

\begin{split}C(a,\ b,\ c)=\sum_{j=0}^{b}(-1)^{j}{b\choose j}{a-(c+1)j+b-1\choose b-1}.\end{split}

if
a>0
:
1
if
a=0
: and
0
otherwise (See, e.g.
Charalambides, 2002
).

7
Joint distribution of the longest success and failure runs for
q
-binary trials

In this section, we study the joint distribution of the lengths of the longest success and longest failure runs.
Let
\{X_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
be an arbitrary sequence of
n
binary (zero and one) trials ordered on a line, with probability of ones varying according to a geometric rule. We consider that an ordered sequence of
n
trials which consist of
n_{1}
of the total number of successes and
n_{2}
of the total number of failures, where
n_{1}+n_{2}=n
. We consider these
n
trials how they are arranged to form a random sequence and we shall discuss various aspects of the representation of successes and failures trials which arise. We will distribute the
n_{1}
successes and the
n_{2}
failures. We first consider the failure runs. Let
s
(1\leq s\leq n_{2})
be the number of runs of failures and then
n_{1}
failures can be arranged to form
s
runs. It is true that whenever there are
s
runs of failures there are three alternatives for the successes :

•

there are
s-1
runs of successes and the series can begin only with a run of failures (B-type);

•

there are
s
runs of failures and the series can begin with a run of successes or a run of failures (D and A-type, respectively) and

•

there are
s+1
runs of successes and the series can begin only with a run of successes (C-type).

Thus sequence
X_{1}
,
\ldots
,
X_{n}
has one of the following four forms:

\begin{split}\text{A-type}\ :&\quad\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{1}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{2}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{2}}\mid\ldots\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{s}},\\
\text{B-type}\ :&\quad\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{1}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{2}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{2}}\mid\ldots\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s-1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{s-1}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s}},\\
\text{C-type}\ :&\quad\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{1}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{2}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{2}}\mid\ldots\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{s+1}},\\
\text{D-type}\ :&\quad\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{1}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{2}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{2}}\mid\ldots\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s-1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{s}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s}},\\
\end{split}

It is noteworthy that the representations of A to D-type are based on the arguments given in the proof of Theorem 3.2.1 of
Gibbons and Chakraborti, 2020
. It is of interest to note that, for the first and fourth forms total number of success and failure runs are both equal and we have
(s-1)
and
(s+1)
success runs for the second and third forms, respectively. Here, we derive the joint distribution of the lengths of the longest success and longest failure runs. More specifically, we derive the joint distributions for
P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\leq k_{2}\Big)
,
P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\geq k_{2}\Big)
,
P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\geq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\leq k_{2}\Big)
and
P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\geq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\geq k_{2}\Big)
.

7.1
Closed expression for the PMF of
\left(L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\leq k_{2}\right)

We shall study of the joint distribution of
\left(L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\leq k_{2}\right)
. The probability function of the
P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\leq k_{2}\Big)
is obtained by the following theorem.

Theorem 7.1
.

The PMF
P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\leq k_{2}\Big)
for
n\geq 1

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\leq k_{2}\Big)=&\sum_{i=1}^{n}\theta^{n-i}\ {\prod_{j=1}^{i}}\ (1-\theta q^{j-1})\sum_{s=1}^{i}\Big[D_{q}^{k_{1}+1,k_{2}+1}(n-i,\ i,\ s)\\
&+A_{q}^{k_{1}+1,k_{2}+1}(n-i,\ i,\ s)+C_{q}^{k_{1}+1,k_{2}+1}(n-i,\ i,\ s+1)\\
&+B_{q}^{k_{1}+1,k_{2}+1}(n-i,\ i,\ s)\Big]+\eta(n,k_{1})\theta^{n},\end{split}

where
\eta(n,k_{1})
is the function by
\eta(n,k_{1})=1
if
n\leq k_{1}
and
0
otherwise.

Proof.

We partition the event
\left\{L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\leq k_{2}\right\}
into disjoint events given by
F_{n}=i,
for
i=1,\ldots,n
. Adding the probabilities we have

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\leq k_{2}\Big)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\leq k_{2}\ \wedge\ F_{n}=i\Big).\\
\end{split}

We will write
E_{n,i}=\left\{L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\leq k_{2}\ \wedge\ F_{n}=i\right\}
.
We can now rewrite as follows

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\leq k_{2}\Big)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}P_{q,\theta}\left(E_{n,i}\right).\end{split}

We are going to focus on the event
E_{n,\ i}
. A typical element of the event
\left\{L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\leq k_{2}\right\}
is an ordered sequence which consists of
n-i
successes and
i
failures such that the length of the longest success run is less than or equal to
k_{1}
. The number of these sequences can be derived as follows. First we will distribute the
i
failures and the
n-i
successes. Let
s

(1\leq s\leq i)
be the number of runs of failures in the event
E_{n,\ i}
. We divide into two cases: starting with a success run or starting with a failure run. Thus, we distinguish between four types of sequences in the event
\left\{L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\leq k_{2}\right\}
, respectively named A, B, C and D-type, which are defined as follows.

\begin{split}\text{A-type}\ :&\quad\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{1}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{2}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{2}}\mid\ldots\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{s}},\\
\end{split}

with
i

0
’s and
n-i

1
’s, where
x_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s)
represents a length of run of
1
’s and
y_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s)
represents the length of a run of
0
’s. And all integers
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}
, and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}
satisfy the conditions

\begin{split}0<x_{j}<k_{1}+1\mbox{\ for\ }j=1,...,s,\mbox{\ and\ }x_{1}+\cdots+x_{s}=n-i,\end{split}

\begin{split}0<y_{j}<k_{2}+1\mbox{\ for\ }j=1,...,s,\mbox{\ and\ }y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}=i.\end{split}

\begin{split}\text{B-type}\ :&\quad\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{1}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{2}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{2}}\mid\ldots\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s-1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{s-1}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s}},\\
\end{split}

with
i

0
’s and
n-i

1
’s, where
x_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s-1)
represents a length of run of
1
’s and
y_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s)
represents the length of a run of
0
’s. And all integers
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1}
, and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}
satisfy the conditions

\begin{split}0<x_{j}<k_{1}+1\mbox{\ for\ }j=1,...,s-1,\mbox{\ and\ }x_{1}+\cdots+x_{s-1}=n-i,\end{split}

\begin{split}0<y_{j}<k_{2}+1\mbox{\ for\ }j=1,...,s,\mbox{\ and\ }y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}=i.\end{split}

\begin{split}\text{C-type}\ :&\quad\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{1}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{2}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{2}}\mid\ldots\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{s+1}},\\
\end{split}

with
i

0
’s and
n-i

1
’s, where
x_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s+1)
represents a length of run of
1
’s and
y_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s)
represents the length of a run of
0
’s. And all integers
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s+1}
, and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}
satisfy the conditions

\begin{split}0<x_{j}<k_{1}+1\mbox{\ for\ }j=1,...,s+1,\mbox{\ and\ }x_{1}+\cdots+x_{s+1}=n-i,\end{split}

\begin{split}0<y_{j}<k_{2}+1\mbox{\ for\ }j=1,...,s,\mbox{\ and\ }y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}=i.\end{split}

\begin{split}\text{D-type}\ :&\quad\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{1}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{2}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{2}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{3}}\mid\ldots\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s-1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{s}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s}},\\
\end{split}

with
i

0
’s and
n-i

1
’s, where
x_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s)
represents a length of run of
1
’s and
y_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s)
represents the length of a run of
0
’s. And all integers
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}
, and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}
satisfy the conditions

\begin{split}0<x_{j}<k_{1}+1\mbox{\ for\ }j=1,...,s,\mbox{\ and\ }x_{1}+\cdots+x_{s}=n-i,\end{split}

\begin{split}0<y_{j}<k_{2}+1\mbox{\ for\ }j=1,...,s,\mbox{\ and\ }y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}=i.\end{split}

Then the probability of the event
E_{n,\ i}
is given by

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(E_{n,\ i}\Big)&\\
=\sum_{s=1}^{i}\Bigg[\bigg\{&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}+\cdots+x_{s}=n-i\\
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{1}\}\end{subarray}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}=i\\
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{2}\}\end{subarray}}}\big(1-\theta q^{0}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}-1}\big)\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}}\Big)^{x_{1}}\times\\
&\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}-1}\big)\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\Big)^{x_{2}}\times\\
&\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}+y_{3}-1}\big)\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}+y_{3}}\Big)^{x_{3}}\times\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\vdots\\
&\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}-1}\big)\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}}\Big)^{x_{s}}\bigg\}+\\
\end{split}

\begin{split}\quad\bigg\{&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}+\cdots+x_{s-1}=n-i\\
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{1}\}\end{subarray}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}=i\\
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{2}\}\end{subarray}}}\big(1-\theta q^{0}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}-1}\big)\times\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}}\Big)^{x_{1}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}-1}\big)\times\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\Big)^{x_{2}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}+y_{3}-1}\big)\times\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\vdots\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}}\Big)^{x_{s-1}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}-1}\big)\bigg\}+\\
\end{split}

\begin{split}\quad\quad\quad\quad\bigg\{&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}+\cdots+x_{s+1}=n-i\\
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s+1}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{1}\}\end{subarray}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}=i\\
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{2}\}\end{subarray}}}\big(\theta q^{0}\big)^{x_{1}}\big(1-\theta q^{0}\big)\cdots(1-\theta q^{y_{1}-1})\times\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}}\Big)^{x_{2}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}-1}\big)\times\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\Big)^{x_{3}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}+y_{3}-1}\big)\times\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\vdots\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}}\Big)^{x_{s+1}}\bigg\}+\\
\end{split}

\begin{split}\quad\quad\quad\quad\bigg\{&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}+\cdots+x_{s}=n-i\\
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{1}\}\end{subarray}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}=i\\
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{2}\}\end{subarray}}}\big(\theta q^{0}\big)^{x_{1}}\big(1-\theta q^{0}\big)\cdots(1-\theta q^{y_{1}-1})\times\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}}\Big)^{x_{2}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}-1}\big)\times\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\Big)^{x_{3}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}+y_{3}-1}\big)\times\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\vdots\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}}\Big)^{x_{s}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}-1}\big)\bigg\}\Bigg].\\
\end{split}

Using simple exponentiation algebra arguments to simplify,

\begin{split}&P_{q,\theta}\Big(E_{n,i}\Big)=\\
&\theta^{n-i}{\prod_{j=1}^{i}}\ \left(1-\theta q^{j-1}\right)\times\\
&\sum_{s=1}^{i}\Bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}+\cdots+x_{s}=n-i\\
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{1}\}\end{subarray}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}=i\\
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{2}\}\end{subarray}}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s})x_{s}}+\\
\end{split}

\begin{split}&\quad\quad\quad\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}+\cdots+x_{s-1}=n-i\\
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{1}\}\end{subarray}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}=i\\
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{2}\}\end{subarray}}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s-1}}+\\
&\quad\quad\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}+\cdots+x_{s+1}=n-i\\
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s+1}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{1}\}\end{subarray}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}=i\\
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{2}\}\end{subarray}}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s})x_{s+1}}+\\
&\quad\quad\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}+\cdots+x_{s}=n-i\\
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{1}\}\end{subarray}}\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}=i\\
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{2}\}\end{subarray}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s}}\Bigg].\\
\end{split}

Using Lemma
3.1
, Lemma
3.2
, Lemma
3.3
and Lemma
3.4
, we can rewrite as follows.

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(E_{n,i}\Big)=\theta^{n-i}\ {\prod_{j=1}^{i}}\ (1-\theta q^{j-1})&\sum_{s=1}^{i}\Big[D_{q}^{k_{1}+1,k_{2}+1}(n-i,\ i,\ s)+A_{q}^{k_{1}+1,k_{2}+1}(n-i,\ i,\ s)\\
&+C_{q}^{k_{1}+1,k_{2}+1}(n-i,\ i,\ s+1)+B_{q}^{k_{1}+1,k_{2}+1}(n-i,\ i,\ s)\Big],\\
\end{split}

where

\begin{split}D_{q}^{k_{1}+1,k_{2}+1}(n&-i,\ i,\ s)\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}+\cdots+x_{s}=n-i\\
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{1}\}\end{subarray}}\ \hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}=i\\
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{2}\}\end{subarray}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s})x_{s}},\end{split}

\begin{split}A_{q}^{k_{1}+1,k_{2}+1}(n&-i,\ i,\ s)\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}+\cdots+x_{s-1}=n-i\\
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{1}\}\end{subarray}}\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}=i\\
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{2}\}\end{subarray}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s-1}},\end{split}

\begin{split}C_{q}^{k_{1}+1,k_{2}+1}(n&-i,\ i,\ s+1)\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}+\cdots+x_{s+1}=n-i\\
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s+1}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{1}\}\end{subarray}}\ \hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}=i\\
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{2}\}\end{subarray}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s})x_{s+1}},\end{split}

and

\begin{split}B_{q}^{k_{1}+1,k_{2}+1}(n&-i,\ i,\ s)\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}+\cdots+x_{s}=n-i\\
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{1}\}\end{subarray}}\ \hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}=i\\
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{2}\}\end{subarray}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s}}.\end{split}

Therefore we can compute the probability of the event
\left\{L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\leq k_{2}\right\}
as follows.

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\leq k_{2}\Big)=&\sum_{i=1}^{n}\theta^{n-i}\ {\prod_{j=1}^{i}}\ (1-\theta q^{j-1})\sum_{s=1}^{i}\Big[D_{q}^{k_{1}+1,k_{2}+1}(n-i,\ i,\ s)\\
&+A_{q}^{k_{1}+1,k_{2}+1}(n-i,\ i,\ s)+C_{q}^{k_{1}+1,k_{2}+1}(n-i,\ i,\ s+1)\\
&+B_{q}^{k_{1}+1,k_{2}+1}(n-i,\ i,\ s)\Big].\\
\end{split}

Thus proof is completed.
∎

It is worth mentioning here that the PMF
P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\leq k_{2}\Big)
approaches the probability function of
P_{\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\leq k_{2}\Big)
in the limit as
q
tends to 1 of IID model. The details are presented in the following remark.

Remark 34
.

For
q=1
, the PMF
P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\leq k_{2}\Big)
reduces to the PMF
P_{\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\leq k_{2}\Big)
for
n\geq 1
is given by

\begin{split}P_{\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\leq k_{2}\Big)=&\sum_{i=1}^{n}\theta^{n-i}(1-\theta)^{i}\sum_{s=1}^{i}\Big[S(s,\ k_{1}+1,\ n-i)\\
&+S(s,\ k_{1}+1,\ n-i)+S(s+1,\ k_{1}+1,\ n-i)\\
&+S(s,\ k_{1}+1,\ n-i)\Big]S(s,\ k_{2}+1,\ i)+\eta(n,k_{1})\theta^{n},\\
\end{split}

where
\ S(a,\ b,\ c)=\sum_{j=0}^{min(a,\left[\frac{c-a}{b-1}\right])}(-1)^{j}{a\choose j}{c-j(b-1)-1\choose a-1}
, and
\eta(n,k_{1})
is the function by
\eta(n,k_{1})=1
if
n\leq k_{1}
and
0
otherwise.

7.2
Closed expression for the PMF of
\left(L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\geq k_{2}\right)

We shall study of the joint distribution of
\left(L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\geq k_{2}\right)
. We now make some useful Definition and Lemma for the proofs of Theorem in the sequel.

Definition 7.1
.

For
0<q\leq 1
, we define

\begin{split}\overline{M}_{q,0,0}^{k_{1},\infty}(u,v,s)={\sum_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}}}\ \sum_{y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s})x_{s}},\ \end{split}

where the summation is over all integers
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s},
and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}
satisfying

\begin{split}0<x_{j}<k_{1}\mbox{\ for\ }j=1,...,s,\end{split}

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{u,a}^{0},\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{v,s}^{0}.\end{split}

The following gives a recurrence relation useful for the computation of
\overline{M}_{q,0,0}^{k_{1},\infty}(u,v,s)
.

Lemma 7.1
.

\overline{M}_{q,0,0}^{k_{1},\infty}(u,v,s)
obeys the following recurrence relation.

\begin{split}&\overline{M}_{q,0,0}^{k_{1},\infty}(u,v,s)\\
&=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\sum_{b=1}^{v-(s-1)}\sum_{a=1}^{k_{1}-1}q^{va}\overline{M}_{q,0,0}^{k_{1},\infty}(u-a,v-b,s-1),&\text{for}\ s>1,\ s\leq u\leq s(k_{1}-1)\\
&\text{and}\ s\leq v\\
1,&\text{for}\ s=1,\ 1\leq u\leq k_{1}-1,\ \text{and}\ 1\leq v\\
0,&\text{otherwise.}\\
\end{array}\right.\end{split}

Proof.

For
s>1
,
s\leq u\leq s(k_{1}-1)
and
s\leq v
, we observe that
x_{s}
may assume any value
1,\ldots,k_{1}-1
, then
\overline{M}_{q,0,0}^{k_{1},\infty}(u,v,s)
can be written as

\begin{split}\overline{M}&{}_{q,0,0}^{k_{1},\infty}(u,v,s)\\
=&\sum_{x_{s}=1}^{k_{1}-1}{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{1}-1\}\\
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{u-x_{s},s-1}^{0}\end{subarray}}}\ {\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{v,s}^{0}\end{subarray}}}q^{vx_{s}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s-1}}.\end{split}

Similarly, we observe that since
y_{s}
can assume the values
1,\ldots,v-(s-1)
, then
\overline{M}_{q,0,0}^{k_{1},\infty}(u,v,s)
can be rewritten as

\begin{split}\overline{M}&{}_{q,0,0}^{k_{1},\infty}(u,v,s)\\
=&\sum_{y_{s}=1}^{v-(s-1)}\sum_{x_{s}=1}^{k_{1}-1}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{1}-1\}\\
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{u-x_{s},s-1}^{0}\end{subarray}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{v,s}^{0}\end{subarray}}}q^{vx_{s}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s-1}}\\
=&\sum_{b=1}^{v-(s-1)}\sum_{a=1}^{k_{1}-1}q^{va}\overline{M}_{q,0,0}^{k_{1},\infty}(u-a,v-b,s-1)\end{split}

The other cases are obvious and thus the proof is completed.
∎

Remark 35
.

We observe that
\overline{M}_{1,0,0}^{k_{1},\infty}(u,v,s)
is the number of integer solutions
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})
and
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})
of

\begin{split}0<x_{j}<k_{1}\mbox{\ for\ }j=1,...,s,\end{split}

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{u,s}^{0},\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{v,s}^{0}.\end{split}

which is

\begin{split}\overline{M}_{1,0,0}^{k_{1},\infty}(u,v,s)=S(s,\ k_{1},\ u)M(s,\ v),\end{split}

where
S(a,\ b,\ c)
denotes the total number of integer solutions
x_{1}+x_{2}+\cdots+x_{a}=c
such that
0<x_{i}<b
for
i=1,2,\ldots,a
. The number can be expressed as

\begin{split}S(a,\ b,\ c)=\sum_{j=0}^{min(a,\left[\frac{c-a}{b-1}\right])}(-1)^{j}{a\choose j}{c-j(b-1)-1\choose a-1}.\end{split}

where
M(a,\ b)
denotes the total number of integer solution
y_{1}+y_{2}+\cdots+y_{a}=b
such that
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{a})\in S_{b,a}^{0}
. The number is given by

\begin{split}M(a,\ b)={b-1\choose a-1}.\end{split}

See, e.g.
Charalambides, 2002
.

Definition 7.2
.

For
0<q\leq 1
, we define

\begin{split}M_{q,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1},\infty}(m,r,s)={\sum_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}}}\ \sum_{y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s})x_{s}},\ \end{split}

where the summation is over all integers
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s},
and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}
satisfying

\begin{split}0<x_{j}<k_{1}\mbox{\ for\ }j=1,...,s,\end{split}

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{u,s}^{0},\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{v,s}^{k_{2}-1}.\end{split}

The following gives a recurrence relation useful for the computation of
M_{q,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1},\infty}(m,r,s)
.

Lemma 7.2
.

M_{q,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1},\infty}(m,r,s)
obeys the following recurrence relation.

\begin{split}M&{}_{q,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1},\infty}(m,r,s)\\
&=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\sum_{b=1}^{k_{2}-1}\sum_{a=1}^{k_{1}-1}q^{ra}M_{q,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1},\infty}(m-a,r-b,s-1)\\
+\sum_{b=k_{2}}^{r-(s-1)}\sum_{a=1}^{k_{1}-1}q^{ra}\overline{M}_{q}(m-a,r-b,s-1),&\text{for}\ s>1,\ s\leq m\leq s(k_{1}-1)\\
&\text{and}\ (s-1)+k_{2}\leq r\\
1,&\text{for}\ s=1,\ 1\leq m\leq k_{1}-1,\ \text{and}\ k_{2}\leq r\\
0,&\text{otherwise.}\\
\end{array}\right.\end{split}

Proof.

For
s>1
,
s\leq m\leq s(k_{1}-1)
and
(s-1)+k_{2}\leq r
, we observe that
x_{s}
may assume any value
1,\ldots,k_{1}-1
, then
M_{q,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1},\infty}(m,r,s)
can be written as

\begin{split}M&{}_{q,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1},\infty}(m,r,s)\\
=&\sum_{x_{s}=1}^{k_{1}-1}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{1}-1\}\\
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{m-x_{s},s-1}^{0}\end{subarray}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{r,s}^{k_{2}-1}\end{subarray}}}q^{rx_{s}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s-1}}.\end{split}

Similarly, we observe that since
y_{s}
can assume the values
1,\ldots,r-(s-1)
, then
M_{q,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1},\infty}(m,r,s)
can be rewritten as

\begin{split}M&{}_{q,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1},\infty}(m,r,s)\\
=&\sum_{y_{s}=1}^{k_{2}-1}\sum_{x_{s}=1}^{k_{1}-1}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{1}-1\}\\
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{m-x_{s},s-1}^{0}\end{subarray}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{r-y_{s},s-1}^{k_{2}-1}\end{subarray}}}q^{rx_{s}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s-1}}\\
&+\sum_{y_{s}=k_{2}}^{r-(s-1)}\sum_{x_{s}=1}^{k_{1}-1}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{1}-1\}\\
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{m-x_{s},s-1}^{0}\end{subarray}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{r-y_{s},s-1}^{0}\end{subarray}}}\hskip-14.22636ptq^{rx_{s}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s-1}}\\
=&\sum_{b=1}^{k_{2}-1}\sum_{a=1}^{k_{1}-1}q^{ra}M_{q,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1},\infty}(m-a,r-b,s-1)+\sum_{b=k_{2}}^{r-(s-1)}\sum_{a=1}^{k_{1}-1}q^{ra}\overline{M}_{q,0,0}^{k_{1},\infty}(m-a,r-b,s-1).\end{split}

The other cases are obvious and thus the proof is completed.
∎

Remark 36
.

We observe that
M_{1,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1},\infty}(m,r,s)
is the number of integer solutions
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})
and
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})
of

\begin{split}0<x_{j}<k_{1}\mbox{\ for\ }j=1,...,s,\end{split}

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{m,s}^{0},\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{r,s}^{k_{2}-1}.\end{split}

given by

\begin{split}M_{1,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1},\infty}(m,r,s)=S(s,\ k_{1},\ m)R(s,\ k_{2},\ r),\end{split}

where
S(a,\ b,\ c)
denotes the total number of integer solution
x_{1}+x_{2}+\cdots+x_{a}=c
such that
0<x_{i}<b
for
i=1,2,\ldots,a
. The number is given by

\begin{split}S(a,\ b,\ c)=\sum_{j=0}^{min\left(a,\ \left[\frac{c-a}{b-1}\right]\right)}(-1)^{j}{a\choose j}{c-j(b-1)-1\choose a-1}.\end{split}

where
R(a,\ b,\ c)
denotes the total number of integer solution
y_{1}+x_{2}+\cdots+y_{a}=c
such that
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{a})\in S_{r,a}^{k_{2}}
. The number is given by

\begin{split}R(a,\ b,\ c)=\sum_{j=1}^{min\left(a,\ \left[\frac{c-a}{b-1}\right]\right)}(-1)^{j+1}{a\choose j}{c-j(b-1)-1\choose a-1}.\end{split}

See, e.g.
Charalambides, 2002
.

Definition 7.3
.

For
0<q\leq 1
, we define

\begin{split}\overline{N}_{q,0,0}^{k_{1},\infty}(u,v,s)={\sum_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}}}\ \sum_{y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s}},\ \end{split}

where the summation is over all integers
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s},
and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1}
satisfying

\begin{split}0<x_{j}<k_{1}\mbox{\ for\ }j=1,...,s,\end{split}

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{u,s}^{0},\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{v,s-1}^{0}.\end{split}

The following gives a recurrence relation useful for the computation of
\overline{N}_{q,0,0}^{k_{1},\infty}(u,v,s)
.

Lemma 7.3
.

\overline{N}_{q,0,0}^{k_{1},\infty}(u,v,s)
obeys the following recurrence relation.

\begin{split}\overline{N}&{}_{q,0,0}^{k_{1},\infty}(u,v,s)\\
&=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\sum_{b=1}^{v-(s-2)}\sum_{a=1}^{k_{1}-1}q^{va}\overline{N}_{q,0,0}^{k_{1},\infty}(u-a,v-b,s-1),&\text{for}\ s>1,\ s\leq u\leq s(k_{1}-1)\\
&\text{and}\ s-1\leq v\\
1,&\text{for}\ s=1,\ 1\leq u\leq k_{1}-1,\ \text{and}\ v=0\\
0,&\text{otherwise.}\\
\end{array}\right.\end{split}

Proof.

For
s>1
,
s\leq u\leq s(k_{1}-1)
and
s-1\leq v
, we observe that
x_{s}
may assume any value
1,\ldots,k_{1}-1
, then
\overline{N}_{q,0,0}^{k_{1},\infty}(u,v,s)
can be written as

\begin{split}\overline{N}&{}_{q,0,0}^{k_{1},\infty}(u,v,s)\\
=&\sum_{x_{s}=1}^{k_{1}-1}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{1}-1\}\\
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{u-x_{s},s-1}^{0}\end{subarray}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{v,s-1}^{0}\end{subarray}}}q^{vx_{s}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2})x_{s-1}}.\end{split}

Similarly, we observe that since
y_{s-1}
can assume the values
1,\ldots,v-(s-2)
, then
\overline{N}_{q,0,0}^{k_{1},\infty}(u,v,s)
can be rewritten as

\begin{split}\overline{N}&{}_{q,0,0}^{k_{1},\infty}(u,v,s)\\
=&\sum_{y_{s-1}=1}^{v-(s-2)}\sum_{x_{s}=1}^{k_{1}-1}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{1}-1\}\\
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{u-x_{s},s-1}^{0}\end{subarray}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-2})\in S_{v-y_{s-1},s-2}^{0}\end{subarray}}}q^{vx_{s}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2})x_{s-1}}\\
=&\sum_{b=1}^{v-(s-2)}\sum_{a=1}^{k_{1}-1}q^{va}\overline{N}_{q,0,0}^{k_{1},\infty}(u-a,v-b,s-1)\end{split}

The other cases are obvious and thus the proof is completed.
∎

Remark 37
.

We observe that
\overline{N}_{1,0,0}^{k_{1},\infty}(u,v,s)
corresponds to the number of integer solutions
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})
and
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})
satisfying

\begin{split}0<x_{j}<k_{1}\mbox{\ for\ }j=1,...,s,\end{split}

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{u,s}^{0},\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{v,s-1}^{0}\end{split}

given by

\begin{split}\overline{N}_{1,0,0}^{k_{1},\infty}(u,v,s)=S(s,\ k_{1},\ u)M(s-1,\ v)\end{split}

where
S(a,\ b,\ c)
denotes the total number of integer solutions
x_{1}+x_{2}+\cdots+x_{a}=c
such that
0<x_{i}<b
for
i=1,2,\ldots,a
. The number can be expressed as

\begin{split}S(a,\ b,\ c)=\sum_{j=0}^{min(a,\left[\frac{c-a}{b-1}\right])}(-1)^{j}{a\choose j}{c-j(b-1)-1\choose a-1}.\end{split}

where
M(a,\ b)
denotes the total number of integer solution
y_{1}+y_{2}+\cdots+y_{a}=b
such that
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{a})\in S_{b,a}^{0}
. The number is given by

\begin{split}M(a,\ b)={b-1\choose a-1}.\end{split}

See, e.g.
Charalambides, 2002
.

Definition 7.4
.

For
0<q\leq 1
, we define

\begin{split}N_{q,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1},\infty}(m,r,s)={\sum_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}}}\ \sum_{y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s}},\ \end{split}

where the summation is over all integers
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s},
and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1}
satisfying

\begin{split}0<x_{j}<k_{1}\mbox{\ for\ }j=1,...,s,\end{split}

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{m,s}^{0},\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{r,s-1}^{k_{2}-1}\end{split}

The following gives a recurrence relation useful for the computation of
N_{q,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1},\infty}(m,r,s)
.

Lemma 7.4
.

N_{q,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1},\infty}(m,r,s)
obeys the following recurrence relation.

\begin{split}N&{}_{q,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1},\infty}(m,r,s)\\
&=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\sum_{b=1}^{k_{2}-1}\sum_{a=1}^{k_{1}-1}q^{ra}N_{q,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1},\infty}(m-a,r-b,s-1)\\
+\sum_{b=k_{2}}^{r-(s-2)}\sum_{a=1}^{k_{1}-1}q^{ra}\overline{N}_{q,0,0}^{k_{1},\infty}(m-a,r-b,s-1),&\text{for}\ s>1,\ s\leq m\leq s(k_{1}-1)\\
&\text{and}\ (s-2)+k_{2}\leq r\\
0,&\text{otherwise.}\\
\end{array}\right.\end{split}

Proof.

For
s>1
,
s\leq m\leq s(k_{1}-1)
and
(s-2)+k_{2}\leq r
, we observe that
x_{s}
may assume any value
1,\ldots,k_{1}-1
, then
N_{q,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1},\infty}(m,r,s)
can be written as

\begin{split}N&{}_{q,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1},\infty}(m,r,s)\\
=&\sum_{x_{s}=1}^{k_{1}-1}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{1}-1\}\\
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{m-x_{s},s-1}^{0}\end{subarray}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{r,s-1}^{k_{2}-1}\end{subarray}}}q^{rx_{s}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2})x_{s-1}}.\end{split}

Similarly, we observe that since
y_{s-1}
can assume the values
1,\ldots,r-(s-2)
, then
N_{q,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1},\infty}(m,r,s)
can be rewritten as

\begin{split}N&{}_{q,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1},\infty}(m,r,s)\\
=&\sum_{y_{s-1}=1}^{k_{2}-1}\sum_{x_{s}=1}^{k_{1}-1}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{1}-1\}\\
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{m-x_{s},s-1}^{k_{2}-1}\end{subarray}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-2})\in S_{r-y_{s-1},s-2}^{0}\end{subarray}}}q^{rx_{s}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2})x_{s-1}}\\
&+\sum_{y_{s-1}=k_{2}}^{r-(s-2)}\sum_{x_{s}=k_{1}}^{k_{1}-1}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{1}-1\}\\
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{m-x_{s},s-1}^{0}\end{subarray}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ \ldots,\ y_{s-2})\in S_{r-y_{s-1},s-2}^{0}\end{subarray}}}q^{rx_{s}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2})x_{s-1}}\\
=&\sum_{b=1}^{k_{2}-1}\sum_{a=1}^{k_{1}-1}q^{ra}N_{q,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1},\infty}(m-a,r-b,s-1)+\sum_{b=k_{2}}^{r-(s-2)}\sum_{a=1}^{k_{1}-1}q^{ra}\overline{N}_{q,0,0}^{k_{1},\infty}(m-a,r-b,s-1,k_{1},k_{2}).\end{split}

The other cases are obvious and thus the proof is completed.
∎

Remark 38
.

We observe that
N_{1,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1},\infty}(m,r,s)
is the number of integer solutions
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})
and
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})
of

\begin{split}0<x_{j}<k_{1}\mbox{\ for\ }j=1,...,s,\end{split}

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{m,s}^{0},\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{r,s-1}^{k_{2}-1}\end{split}

given by

\begin{split}N_{1,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1},\infty}(m,r,s)=S(s,\ k_{1},\ m)R(s-1,\ k_{2},\ r)\end{split}

where
S(a,\ b,\ c)
denotes the total number of integer solution
x_{1}+x_{2}+\cdots+x_{a}=c
such that
0<x_{i}<b
for
i=1,2,\ldots,a
. The number is given by

\begin{split}S(a,\ b,\ c)=\sum_{j=0}^{min\left(a,\ \left[\frac{c-a}{b-1}\right]\right)}(-1)^{j}{a\choose j}{c-j(b-1)-1\choose a-1}.\end{split}

where
R(a,\ b,\ c)
denotes the total number of integer solution
y_{1}+x_{2}+\cdots+y_{a}=c
such that
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{a})\in S_{r,a}^{k_{2}}
. The number is given by

\begin{split}R(a,\ b,\ c)=\sum_{j=1}^{min\left(a,\ \left[\frac{c-a}{b-1}\right]\right)}(-1)^{j+1}{a\choose j}{c-j(b-1)-1\choose a-1}.\end{split}

The probability function of the
P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\geq k_{2}\Big)
is obtained by the following theorem.

Theorem 7.2
.

The PMF
P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\geq k_{2}\Big)
for
n\geq k_{2}

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\geq k_{2}\Big)=&\sum_{i=k_{2}}^{n}\theta^{n-i}\ {\prod_{j=1}^{i}}\ (1-\theta q^{j-1})\sum_{s=1}^{i-k_{2}+1}\Big[M_{q,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1}+1,\infty}(n-i,\ i,\ s)\\
&+E_{q,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1}+1,\infty}(n-i,\ i,\ s)+N_{q,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1}+1,\infty}(n-i,\ i,\ s+1)\\
&+F_{q,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1}+1,\infty}(n-i,\ i,\ s)\Big].\\
\end{split}

Proof.

We partition the event
\left\{L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\geq k_{2}\right\}
into disjoint events given by
F_{n}=i,
for
i=k_{2},\ldots,n
. Adding the probabilities we have

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\geq k_{2}\Big)=\sum_{i=k_{2}}^{n}P\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\geq k_{2}\ \wedge\ F_{n}=i\Big).\\
\end{split}

We will write
E_{n,i}=\left\{L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\geq k_{2}\ \wedge\ F_{n}=i\right\}
.
We can now rewrite as follows

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\geq k_{2}\Big)=\sum_{i=k_{2}}^{n}P_{q,\theta}\left(E_{n,i}\right).\end{split}

We are going to focus on the event
E_{n,\ i}
which consists of
n-i
successes and
i
failures. First we will distribute the
i
failures and the
n-i
successes. Let
s

(1\leq s\leq i-k_{2}+1)
be the number of runs of failures in the event
E_{n,\ i}
. We distinguish between four types of sequences in the event
\left\{L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\geq k_{2}\ \wedge\ F_{n}=i\right\}
, respectively named A, B, C and D-type, which are defined as follows.

\begin{split}\text{A-type}\ :&\quad\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{1}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{2}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{2}}\mid\ldots\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{s}},\\
\end{split}

with
i

0
’s and
n-i

1
’s, where
x_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s)
represents a length of run of
1
’s and
y_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s)
represents the length of a run of
0
’s. And all integers
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}
, and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}
satisfy the conditions

\begin{split}0<x_{j}<k_{1}+1\mbox{\ for\ }j=1,...,s,\mbox{\ and\ }x_{1}+\cdots+x_{s}=n-i,\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{2}-1}.\end{split}

\begin{split}\text{B-type}\ :&\quad\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{1}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{2}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{2}}\mid\ldots\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s-1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{s-1}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s}},\\
\end{split}

with
i

0
’s and
n-i

1
’s, where
x_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s-1)
represents a length of run of
1
’s and
y_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s)
represents the length of a run of
0
’s. And all integers
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1}
, and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}
satisfy the conditions

\begin{split}0<x_{j}<k_{1}+1\mbox{\ for\ }j=1,...,s-1,\mbox{\ and\ }x_{1}+\cdots+x_{s-1}=n-i,\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{2}-1}.\end{split}

\begin{split}\text{C-type}\ :&\quad\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{1}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{2}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{2}}\mid\ldots\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{s+1}},\\
\end{split}

with
i

0
’s and
n-i

1
’s, where
x_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s+1)
represents a length of run of
1
’s and
y_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s)
represents the length of a run of
0
’s. And all integers
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s+1}
, and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}
satisfy the conditions

\begin{split}0<x_{j}<k_{1}+1\mbox{\ for\ }j=1,...,s+1,\mbox{\ and\ }x_{1}+\cdots+x_{s+1}=n-i,\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{2}-1}.\end{split}

\begin{split}\text{D-type}\ :&\quad\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{1}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{2}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{2}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{3}}\mid\ldots\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s-1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{s}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s}},\\
\end{split}

with
i

0
’s and
n-i

1
’s, where
x_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s)
represents a length of run of
1
’s and
y_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s)
represents the length of a run of
0
’s. And all integers
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}
, and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}
satisfy the conditions

\begin{split}0<x_{j}<k_{1}+1\mbox{\ for\ }j=1,...,s,\mbox{\ and\ }x_{1}+\cdots+x_{s}=n-i,\text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{2}-1}.\end{split}

Then the probability of the event
E_{n,\ i}
is given by

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(E_{n,\ i}\Big)\quad\ &\\
=\sum_{s=1}^{i-k_{2}+1}\Bigg[\bigg\{&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}+\cdots+x_{s}=n-i\\
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{1}\}\end{subarray}}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{2}-1}\end{subarray}}\big(1-\theta q^{0}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}-1}\big)\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}}\Big)^{x_{1}}\times\\
&\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}-1}\big)\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\Big)^{x_{2}}\times\\
&\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}+y_{3}-1}\big)\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}+y_{3}}\Big)^{x_{3}}\times\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\vdots\\
&\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}-1}\big)\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}}\Big)^{x_{s}}\bigg\}+\\
\end{split}

\begin{split}\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\bigg\{&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}+\cdots+x_{s-1}=n-i\\
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{1}\}\end{subarray}}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{2}-1}\end{subarray}}\big(1-\theta q^{0}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}-1}\big)\times\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}}\Big)^{x_{1}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}-1}\big)\times\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\Big)^{x_{2}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}+y_{3}-1}\big)\times\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\vdots\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}}\Big)^{x_{s-1}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}-1}\big)\bigg\}+\\
\end{split}

\begin{split}\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad&\bigg\{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}+\cdots+x_{s+1}=n-i\\
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s+1}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{1}\}\end{subarray}}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{2}-1}\end{subarray}}\big(\theta q^{0}\big)^{x_{1}}\big(1-\theta q^{0}\big)\cdots(1-\theta q^{y_{1}-1})\times\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}}\Big)^{x_{2}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}-1}\big)\times\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\Big)^{x_{3}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}+y_{3}-1}\big)\times\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\vdots\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}}\Big)^{x_{s+1}}\bigg\}+\\
\end{split}

\begin{split}\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad&\bigg\{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}+\cdots+x_{s}=n-i\\
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{1}\}\end{subarray}}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{2}-1}\end{subarray}}\big(\theta q^{0}\big)^{x_{1}}\big(1-\theta q^{0}\big)\cdots(1-\theta q^{y_{1}-1})\times\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}}\Big)^{x_{2}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}-1}\big)\times\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\Big)^{x_{3}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}+y_{3}-1}\big)\times\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\vdots\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}}\Big)^{x_{s}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}-1}\big)\bigg\}\Bigg].\\
\end{split}

Using simple exponentiation algebra arguments to simplify,

\begin{split}&P_{q,\theta}\Big(E_{n,i}\Big)=\\
&\theta^{n-i}{\prod_{j=1}^{i}}\ (1-\theta q^{j-1})\times\\
&\sum_{s=1}^{i-k_{2}+1}\Bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}+\cdots+x_{s}=n-i\\
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{1}\}\end{subarray}}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{2}-1}\end{subarray}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s})x_{s}}+\\
&\quad\quad\quad\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}+\cdots+x_{s-1}=n-i\\
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{1}\}\end{subarray}}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{2}-1}\end{subarray}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s-1}}+\\
&\quad\quad\quad\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}+\cdots+x_{s+1}=n-i\\
x_{1},\ \ldots,\ x_{s+1}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{1}\}\end{subarray}}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{2}-1}\end{subarray}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s})x_{s+1}}+\\
&\quad\quad\quad\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}+\cdots+x_{s}=n-i\\
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{1}\}\end{subarray}}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{2}-1}\end{subarray}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s}}\Bigg].\\
\end{split}

Using Lemma
3.6
, Lemma
3.8
, Lemma
7.2
and Lemma
7.4
, we can rewrite as follows.

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(E_{n,i}\Big)=&\theta^{n-i}\ {\prod_{j=1}^{i}}\ (1-\theta q^{j-1})\sum_{s=1}^{i-k_{2}+1}\Big[M_{q,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1}+1,\infty}(n-i,\ i,\ s)+E_{q,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1}+1,\infty}(n-i,\ i,\ s)\\
&+N_{q,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1}+1,\infty}(n-i,\ i,\ s+1)+F_{q,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1}+1,\infty}(n-i,\ i,\ s)\Big],\\
\end{split}

where

\begin{split}M_{q,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1}+1,\infty}(n&-i,\ i,\ s)\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}+\cdots+x_{s}=n-i\\
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{1}\}\end{subarray}}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{2}-1}\end{subarray}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s})x_{s}},\end{split}

\begin{split}E_{q,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1}+1,\infty}(n&-i,\ i,\ s)\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}+\cdots+x_{s-1}=n-i\\
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{1}\}\end{subarray}}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{2}}\end{subarray}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s-1}},\end{split}

\begin{split}N_{q,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1}+1,\infty}(n&-i,\ i,\ s+1)\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}+\cdots+x_{s+1}=n-i\\
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s+1}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{1}\}\end{subarray}}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{2}-1}\end{subarray}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s})x_{s+1}},\end{split}

and

\begin{split}F_{q,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1}+1,\infty}(n&-i,\ i,\ s)\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1}+\cdots+x_{s}=n-i\\
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{1}\}\end{subarray}}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{2}-1}\end{subarray}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s}}.\end{split}

Therefore we can compute the probability of the event
\left\{L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\geq k_{2}\right\}
as follows

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\geq k_{2}\Big)=&\sum_{i=k_{2}}^{n}\theta^{n-i}\ {\prod_{j=1}^{i}}\ (1-\theta q^{j-1})\sum_{s=1}^{i-k_{2}+1}\Big[M_{q,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1}+1,\infty}(n-i,\ i,\ s)\\
&+E_{q,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1}+1,\infty}(n-i,\ i,\ s)+N_{q,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1}+1,\infty}(n-i,\ i,\ s+1)\\
&+F_{q,0,k_{2}}^{k_{1}+1,\infty}(n-i,\ i,\ s)\Big].\\
\end{split}

Thus proof is completed.
∎

It is worth mentioning here that the PMF
P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\geq k_{2}\Big)
approaches the probability function of
P_{\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\geq k_{2}\Big)
for
n\geq 1
in the limit as
q
tends to 1 of IID model. The details are presented in the following remark.

Remark 39
.

For
q=1
, the PMF
P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\geq k_{2}\Big)
for
n\geq k_{2}
reduces to the PMF
P_{\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\geq k_{2}\Big)
for
n\geq 1
is given by

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\leq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\geq k_{2}\Big)=&\sum_{i=k_{2}}^{n}\theta^{n-i}(1-\theta)^{i}\ \sum_{s=1}^{i-k_{2}+1}\Big[S(s,\ k_{1}+1,\ n-i)\\
&+S(s-1,\ k_{1}+1,\ n-i)+S(s+1,\ k_{1}+1,\ n-i)\\
&+S(s,\ k_{1}+1,\ n-i)\Big]R(s,\ k_{2},\ i),\\
\end{split}

where

\begin{split}S(a,\ b,\ c)=\sum_{j=0}^{min(a,\left[\frac{c-a}{b-1}\right])}(-1)^{j}{a\choose j}{c-j(b-1)-1\choose a-1}\end{split}

and

\begin{split}R(a,\ b,\ c)=\sum_{j=1}^{min\left(a,\ \left[\frac{c-a}{b-1}\right]\right)}(-1)^{j+1}{a\choose j}{c-j(b-1)-1\choose a-1}.\end{split}

See, e.g.
Charalambides, 2002
.

7.3
Closed expression for the PMF of
\left(L_{n}^{(1)}\geq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\leq k_{2}\right)

We shall study of the joint distribution of
\left(L_{n}^{(1)}\geq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\leq k_{2}\right)
. We now make some useful Definition and Lemma for the proofs of Theorem in the sequel.

Definition 7.5
.

For
0<q\leq 1
, we define
