\begin{split}\overline{O}_{q,0,0}^{0,k_{2}}(u,v,s)={\sum_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1}}}\ \sum_{y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s-1}},\ \end{split}

where the summation is over all integers
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1},
and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}
satisfying

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{u,s-1}^{0},\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}0<y_{j}<k_{2}\ \text{for}\ j=1,\ldots,s,\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{v,s}^{0}.\end{split}

The following gives a recurrence relation useful for the computation of
\overline{O}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(u,v,s)
.

Lemma 7.5
.

\overline{O}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(u,v,s)
obeys the following recurrence relation.

\begin{split}\overline{O}&{}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(u,v,s)\\
&=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\sum_{a=1}^{u-(s-2)}\sum_{b=1}^{k_{2}-1}q^{a(v-b)}\overline{O}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(u-a,v-b,s-1),&\text{for}\ s>1,\ s-1\leq u\\
&\text{and}\ s\leq v\leq s(k_{2}-1)\\
1,&\text{for}\ s=1,\ u=0,\ \text{and}\ 1\leq v\leq k_{2}-1\\
0,&\text{otherwise.}\\
\end{array}\right.\end{split}

Proof.

For
s>1
,
s-1\leq u
and
s\leq v\leq s(k_{2}-1)
, we observe that
y_{s}
may assume any value
1,\ldots,k_{2}-1
, then
\overline{O}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(u,v,s)
can be written as

\begin{split}\overline{O}&{}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(u,v,s)\\
=&\sum_{y_{s}=1}^{k_{2}-1}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{u,s-1}^{0}\end{subarray}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0<y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1}<k_{2}\\
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{v-y_{s},s-1}^{0}\end{subarray}}}q^{x_{s-1}(v-y_{s})}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2})x_{s-2}}\end{split}

Similarly, we observe that since
x_{s-1}
can assume the values
1,\ldots,r-(s-2)
, then
\overline{O}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(u,v,s)
can be rewritten as

\begin{split}\overline{O}&{}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(u,v,s)\\
=&\sum_{x_{s-1}=1}^{u-(s-2)}\sum_{y_{s}=1}^{k_{2}-1}q^{x_{s-1}(v-y_{s})}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-2})\in S_{u-x_{s-1},s-2}^{0}\end{subarray}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0<y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1}<k_{2}\\
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{v-y_{s},s-1}{0}\end{subarray}}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2})x_{s-2}}\\
=&\sum_{a=1}^{u-(s-2)}\sum_{b=1}^{k_{2}-1}q^{a(v-b)}\overline{O}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(u-a,v-b,s-1).\end{split}

The other cases are obvious and thus the proof is completed.
∎

Remark 40
.

We observe that
\overline{O}_{1,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(m,r,s)
is the number of integer solutions
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})
and
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})
of

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{u,s-1}^{0},\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}0<y_{j}<k_{2}\ \text{for}\ j=1,\ldots,s,\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{v,s}^{0}.\end{split}

given by

\begin{split}\overline{O}_{1,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(m,r,s)=M(s-1,\ u)S(s,\ k_{2},\ v),\end{split}

where
M(a,\ b)
denotes the total number of integer solution
y_{1}+x_{2}+\cdots+y_{a}=b
such that
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{a})\in S_{b,a}^{0}
. The number is given by

\begin{split}M(a,\ b)={b-1\choose a-1}.\end{split}

where
S(a,\ b,\ c)
denotes the total number of integer solution
x_{1}+x_{2}+\cdots+x_{a}=c
such that
0<x_{i}<b
for
i=1,2,\ldots,a
. The number is given by

\begin{split}S(a,\ b,\ c)=\sum_{j=0}^{min\left(a,\ \left[\frac{c-a}{b-1}\right]\right)}(-1)^{j}{a\choose j}{c-j(b-1)-1\choose a-1}.\end{split}

See, e.g.
Charalambides, 2002
.

Definition 7.6
.

For
0<q\leq 1
, we define

\begin{split}O_{q,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(m,r,s)={\sum_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1}}}\ \sum_{y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s-1}},\ \end{split}

where the summation is over all integers
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1},
and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}
satisfying

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{u,s-1}^{k_{1}-1},\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}0<y_{j}<k_{2}\ \text{for}\ j=1,\ldots,s,\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{v,s}^{0}.\end{split}

The following gives a recurrence relation useful for the computation of
O_{q,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(m,r,s)
.

Lemma 7.6
.

O_{q,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(m,r,s)
obeys the following recurrence relation.

\begin{split}O&{}_{q,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(m,r,s)\\
&=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\sum_{a=1}^{k_{1}-1}\sum_{b=1}^{k_{2}-1}q^{a(r-b)}O_{q,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(m-a,r-b,s-1)\\
+\sum_{a=k_{1}}^{m-(s-2)}\sum_{b=1}^{k_{2}-1}q^{a(r-b)}\overline{O}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(m-a,r-b,s-1),&\text{for}\ s>1,\ (s-2)+k_{1}\leq m)\\
&\text{and}\ s\leq r\leq s(k_{2}-1)\\
1,&\text{for}\ s=1,\ m=0,\ \text{and}\ 1\leq r\leq k_{2}-1\\
0,&\text{otherwise.}\\
\end{array}\right.\end{split}

Proof.

For
s>1
,
(s-2)+k_{1}\leq m)
and
s\leq r\leq s(k_{2}-1)
, we observe that
y_{s}
may assume any value
1,\ldots,k_{2}-1
, then
O_{q,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(m,r,s)
can be written as

\begin{split}O&{}_{q,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(m,r,s)\\
=&\sum_{y_{s}=1}^{k_{2}-1}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{m,s-1}^{k_{1}-1}\end{subarray}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0<y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1}<k_{2}\\
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{r-y_{s},s-1}^{0}\end{subarray}}}q^{x_{s-1}(r-y_{s})}\ q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2})x_{s-2}}\end{split}

Similarly, we observe that since
x_{s-1}
can assume the values
1,\ldots,m-(s-2)
, then
O_{q,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(m,r,s)
can be rewritten as

\begin{split}O&{}_{q,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(m,r,s)\\
=&\sum_{x_{s-1}=1}^{k_{1}-1}\sum_{y_{s}=1}^{k_{2}-1}q^{x_{s-1}(r-y_{s})}{\hskip-14.22636pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-2})\in S_{m-x_{s-1},s-2}^{k_{1}-1}\end{subarray}}}\quad{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0<y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1}<k_{2}\\
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{r-y_{s},s-1}^{0}\end{subarray}}}\ q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2})x_{s-2}}\\
&+\sum_{x_{s-1}=k_{1}}^{m-(s-2)}\sum_{y_{s}=1}^{k_{2}-1}q^{x_{s-1}(r-y_{s})}{\hskip-14.22636pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-2})\in S_{m-x_{s-1},s-2}^{0}\end{subarray}}}\quad{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0<y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1}<k_{2}\\
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{r-y_{s},s-1}^{0}\end{subarray}}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2})x_{s-2}}\\
=&\sum_{a=1}^{k_{1}-1}\ \sum_{b=1}^{k_{2}-1}\ q^{a(r-b)}\ O_{q,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(m-a,r-b,s-1)+\sum_{a=k_{1}}^{m-(s-2)}\ \sum_{b=1}^{k_{2}-1}\ q^{a(r-b)}\ \overline{O}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(m-a,r-b,s-1).\end{split}

The other cases are obvious and thus the proof is completed.
∎

Remark 41
.

We observe that
O_{1,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(m,r,s)
is the number of integer solutions
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})
and
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})
of

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{m,s-1}^{k_{1}-1},\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}0<y_{j}<k_{2}\ \text{for}\ j=1,\ldots,s,\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{r,s}^{0},\end{split}

given by

\begin{split}O_{1,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(m,r,s)=R(s-1,\ k_{1},\ m)S(s,\ k_{2},\ r),\end{split}

where
R(a,\ b,\ c)
denotes the total number of integer solution
y_{1}+x_{2}+\cdots+y_{a}=c
such that
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{a})\in S_{r,a}^{k_{2}}
. The number is given by

\begin{split}R(a,\ b,\ c)=\sum_{j=1}^{min\left(a,\ \left[\frac{c-a}{b-1}\right]\right)}(-1)^{j+1}{a\choose j}{c-j(b-1)-1\choose a-1}.\end{split}

where
S(a,\ b,\ c)
denotes the total number of integer solution
x_{1}+x_{2}+\cdots+x_{a}=c
such that
0<x_{i}<b
for
i=1,2,\ldots,a
. The number is given by

\begin{split}S(a,\ b,\ c)=\sum_{j=0}^{min\left(a,\ \left[\frac{c-a}{b-1}\right]\right)}(-1)^{j}{a\choose j}{c-j(b-1)-1\choose a-1}.\end{split}

See, e.g.
Charalambides, 2002
.

Definition 7.7
.

For
0<q\leq 1
, we define

\begin{split}\overline{P}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(u,v,s)={\sum_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}}}\ \sum_{y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s}},\ \end{split}

where the summation is over all integers
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s},
and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}
satisfying

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{u,s}^{0},\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}0<y_{j}<k_{2}\ \text{for}\ j=1,\ldots,s,\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{v,s}^{0}.\end{split}

The following gives a recurrence relation useful for the computation of
\overline{P}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(u,v,s)
.

Lemma 7.7
.

\overline{P}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(u,v,s)
obeys the following recurrence relation.

\begin{split}\overline{P}&{}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(u,v,s)\\
&=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\sum_{a=1}^{u-(s-1)}\sum_{b=1}^{k_{2}-1}q^{a(v-b)}\overline{P}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(u-a,v-b,s-1),&\text{for}\ s>1,\ s\leq u\\
&\text{and}\ s\leq v\leq s(k_{2}-1)\\
1,&\text{for}\ s=1,\ 1\leq u,\ \text{and}\ 1\leq v\leq k_{2}-1\\
0,&\text{otherwise.}\\
\end{array}\right.\end{split}

Proof.

For
s>1
,
s\leq u
and
s\leq v\leq s(k_{2}-1)
, we observe that
y_{s}
may assume any value
1,\ldots,k_{2}-1
, then
\overline{P}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(u,v,s)
can be written as

\begin{split}\overline{P}&{}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(u,v,s)\\
=&\sum_{y_{s}=1}^{k_{2}-1}{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{u,s}^{0}\end{subarray}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0<y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1}<k_{2}\\
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{v-y_{s},s-1}^{0}\end{subarray}}}q^{x_{s}(v-y_{s})}\ q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2})x_{s-1}}\end{split}

Similarly, we observe that since
x_{s}
can assume the values
1,\ldots,u-(s-1)
, then
\overline{P}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(u,v,s)
can be rewritten as

\begin{split}\overline{P}&{}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(u,v,s)\\
=&\sum_{x_{s}=1}^{u-(s-1)}\sum_{y_{s}=1}^{k_{2}-1}q^{x_{s}(v-y_{s})}{\hskip-8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{u-x_{s},s-1}^{0}\end{subarray}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0<y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1}<k_{2}\\
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{v-y_{s},s-1}^{0}\end{subarray}}}\ q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2})x_{s-1}}\\
=&\sum_{a=1}^{u-(s-1)}\sum_{b=1}^{k_{2}-1}q^{a(v-b)}\overline{P}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(u-a,v-b,s-1).\end{split}

The other cases are obvious and thus the proof is completed.
∎

Definition 7.8
.

For
0<q\leq 1
, we define

\begin{split}P_{q,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(m,r,s)={\sum_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}}}\ \sum_{y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s}},\ \end{split}

where the summation is over all integers
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s},
and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}
satisfying

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{m,s}^{k_{1}-1},\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}0<y_{j}<k_{2}\ \text{for}\ j=1,\ldots,s,\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{r,s}^{0}.\end{split}

The following gives a recurrence relation useful for the computation of
P_{q,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(m,r,s)
.

Lemma 7.8
.

P_{q,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(m,r,s)
obeys the following recurrence relation,

\begin{split}P&{}_{q,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(m,r,s)\\
&=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\sum_{b=1}^{k_{2}-1}\ \sum_{a=1}^{k_{1}-1}q^{a(r-b)}P_{q,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(m-a,r-b,s-1)\\
+\sum_{b=k_{2}}^{r-(s-1)}\ \sum_{a=1}^{k_{1}-1}q^{a(r-b)}\overline{P}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(m-a,r-b,s-1),&\text{for}\quad s>1,\ (s-1)+k_{1}\leq m\\
&\text{and}\quad s\leq r\leq s(k_{2}-1)\\
1,&\text{for}\quad s=1,\ k_{1}\leq m\ \text{and}\\
&1\leq r\leq k_{2}-1\\
0,&\text{otherwise.}\\
\end{array}\right.\end{split}

Proof.

For
s>1
,
(s-1)+k_{1}\leq m
and
s\leq r\leq s(k_{2}-1)
, we observe that
y_{s}
may assume any value
1,\ldots,k_{2}-1
, then
P_{q,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(m,r,s)
can be written as

\begin{split}P&{}_{q,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(m,r,s)\\
=&\sum_{y_{s}=1}^{k_{2}-1}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{m,s}^{k_{1}-1}\end{subarray}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0<y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1}<k_{2}\\
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{r-y_{s},s-1}^{0}\end{subarray}}}q^{x_{s}(r-y_{s})}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2})x_{s-1}}\end{split}

Similarly, we observe that since
x_{s}
can assume the values
1,\ldots,m-(s-1)
, then
L_{q,k_{1},0}(m,r,s)
can be rewritten as

\begin{split}P&{}_{q,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(m,r,s)\\
=&\sum_{x_{s}=1}^{k_{1}-1}\sum_{y_{s}=1}^{k_{2}-1}q^{x_{s}(r-y_{s})}{\hskip-8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{m-x_{s},s-1}^{k_{1}-1}\end{subarray}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{r-y_{s},s-1}^{0}\end{subarray}}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2})x_{s-1}}\\
&+\sum_{x_{s}=k_{1}}^{m-(s-1)}\sum_{y_{s}=1}^{k_{2}-1}q^{x_{s}(r-y_{s})}{\hskip-8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{m-x_{s},s-1}^{0}\end{subarray}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{r-y_{s},s-1}^{0}\end{subarray}}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2})x_{s-1}}\\
=&\sum_{a=1}^{k_{1}-1}\ \sum_{b=1}^{k_{2}-1}q^{a(r-b)}P_{q,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(m-a,r-b,s-1)+\sum_{a=k_{1}}^{m-(s-1)}\ \sum_{b=1}^{k_{2}-1}q^{a(r-b)}\overline{P}_{q,0,0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(m-a,r-b,s-1).\end{split}

The other cases are obvious and thus the proof is completed.
∎

Remark 43
.

We observe that
P_{1,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(m,r,s)
is the number of integer solutions
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})
and
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})
of

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{m,s}^{k_{1}-1},\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}0<y_{j}<k_{2}\ \text{for}\ j=1,\ldots,s,\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{r,s}^{0},\end{split}

given by

\begin{split}P_{1,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}}(m,r,s)=R(s,\ k_{1},\ m)S(s,\ k_{2},\ r),\end{split}

where
R(a,\ b,\ c)
denotes the total number of integer solution
y_{1}+x_{2}+\cdots+y_{a}=c
such that
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{a})\in S_{r,a}^{k_{2}}
. The number is given by

\begin{split}R(a,\ b,\ c)=\sum_{j=1}^{min\left(a,\ \left[\frac{c-a}{b-1}\right]\right)}(-1)^{j+1}{a\choose j}{c-j(b-1)-1\choose a-1}.\end{split}

where
S(a,\ b,\ c)
denotes the total number of integer solution
x_{1}+x_{2}+\cdots+x_{a}=c
such that
0<x_{i}<b
for
i=1,2,\ldots,a
. The number is given by

\begin{split}S(a,\ b,\ c)=\sum_{j=0}^{min\left(a,\ \left[\frac{c-a}{b-1}\right]\right)}(-1)^{j}{a\choose j}{c-j(b-1)-1\choose a-1}.\end{split}

See, e.g.
Charalambides, 2002
.

The probability function of
P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\geq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\leq k_{2}\Big)
for
n\geq k_{1}
is obtained by the following theorem.

Theorem 7.3
.

The PMF
P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\geq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\leq k_{2}\Big)
for
n\geq k_{1}

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\geq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\leq k_{2}\Big)=&\sum_{i=1}^{n-k_{1}}\theta^{n-i}\ {\prod_{j=1}^{i}}\ (1-\theta q^{j-1})\sum_{s=1}^{i}\Big[H_{q,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}+1}(n-i,\ i,\ s)\\
&+O_{q,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}+1}(n-i,\ i,\ s)+G_{q,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}+1}(n-i,\ i,\ s+1)\\
&+P_{q,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}+1}(n-i,\ i,\ s)\Big]J(n,k_{1}+1)+J(n,k_{1})\theta^{n},\\
\end{split}

where
J(n,k_{1})
is the function defined by
J(n,k_{1})=1
if
n\geq k_{1}
and
0
otherwise.

Proof.

We partition the event
\left\{L_{n}^{(1)}\geq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\leq k_{2}\right\}
into disjoint events given by
F_{n}=i,
for
i=k_{2},\ldots,\ n
. Adding the probabilities we have

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\geq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\leq k_{2}\Big)=\sum_{i=1}^{n-k_{1}}P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\geq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\leq k_{2}\ \wedge\ F_{n}=i\Big).\\
\end{split}

We will write
E_{n,i}=\left\{L_{n}^{(1)}\geq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\leq k_{2}\ \wedge\ F_{n}=i\right\}
.
We can now rewrite as follows

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\geq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\leq k_{2}\Big)=\sum_{i=1}^{n-k_{1}}P_{q,\theta}\left(E_{n,i}\right).\end{split}

We are going to focus on the event
E_{n,\ i}
. A typical element of the event
\Big\{L_{n}^{(1)}\geq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\leq k_{2}\ \wedge\ F_{n}=i\Big\}
is an ordered sequence which consists of
n-i
successes and
i
failures such that the length of the longest success run is greater than or equal to
k_{1}
and the length of the longest failure run is less than or equal to
k_{2}
. The number of these sequences can be derived as follows. First we will distribute the
i
failures and the
n-i
successes. Let
s

(1\leq s\leq i)
be the number of runs of failures in the event
E_{n,\ i}
. We divide into two cases: starting with a failure run or starting with a success run. Thus, we distinguish between four types of sequences in the event
\Big\{L_{n}^{(1)}\geq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\leq k_{2}\ \wedge\ F_{n}=i\Big\}
, respectively named A, B, C and D-type, which are defined as follows.

\begin{split}\text{A-type}\ :&\quad\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{1}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{2}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{2}}\mid\ldots\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{s}},\\
\end{split}

with
i

0
’s and
n-i

1
’s, where
x_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s)
represents a length of run of
1
’s and
y_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s)
represents the length of a run of
0
’s. And all integers
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}
, and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}
satisfy the conditions

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{n-i,s}^{k_{1}-1},\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}0<y_{j}<k_{2}+1\mbox{\ for\ }j=1,...,s,\mbox{\ and\ }y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}=i.\end{split}

\begin{split}\text{B-type}\ :&\quad\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{1}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{2}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{2}}\mid\ldots\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s-1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{s-1}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s}},\\
\end{split}

with
i

0
’s and
n-i

1
’s, where
x_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s-1)
represents a length of run of
1
’s and
y_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s)
represents the length of a run of
0
’s. And all integers
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1}
, and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}
satisfy the conditions

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{n-i,s-1}^{k_{1}-1},\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}0<y_{j}<k_{2}+1\mbox{\ for\ }j=1,...,s,\mbox{\ and\ }y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}=i.\end{split}

\begin{split}\text{C-type}\ :&\quad\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{1}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{2}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{2}}\mid\ldots\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{s+1}},\\
\end{split}

with
i

0
’s and
n-i

1
’s, where
x_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s+1)
represents a length of run of
1
’s and
y_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s)
represents the length of a run of
0
’s. And all integers
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s+1}
, and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}
satisfy the conditions

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s+1})\in S_{n-i,s+1}^{k_{1}-1},\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}0<y_{j}<k_{2}+1\mbox{\ for\ }j=1,...,s,\mbox{\ and\ }y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}=i.\end{split}

\begin{split}\text{D-type}\ :&\quad\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{1}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{2}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{2}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{3}}\mid\ldots\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s-1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{s}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s}},\\
\end{split}

with
i

0
’s and
n-i

1
’s, where
x_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s)
represents a length of run of
1
’s and
y_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s)
represents the length of a run of
0
’s. And all integers
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}
, and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}
satisfy the conditions

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{n-i,s}^{k_{1}-1},\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}0<y_{j}<k_{2}+1\mbox{\ for\ }j=1,...,s,\mbox{\ and\ }y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}=i.\end{split}

Then the probability of the event
E_{n,\ i}
is given by

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(E_{n,\ i}\Big)&\\
=\sum_{s=1}^{i}\Bigg[\bigg\{&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{n-i,s}^{k_{1}-1}\end{subarray}}\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}=i\\
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{2}\}\end{subarray}}\big(1-\theta q^{0}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}-1}\big)\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}}\Big)^{x_{1}}\times\\
&\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}-1}\big)\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\Big)^{x_{2}}\times\\
&\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}+y_{3}-1}\big)\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}+y_{3}}\Big)^{x_{3}}\times\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\vdots\\
&\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}-1}\big)\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}}\Big)^{x_{s}}\bigg\}+\\
\end{split}

\begin{split}\quad\ &\bigg\{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{n-i,s-1}^{k_{1}-1}\end{subarray}}\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}=i\\
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{2}\}\end{subarray}}\big(1-\theta q^{0}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}-1}\big)\times\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}}\Big)^{x_{1}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}-1}\big)\times\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\Big)^{x_{2}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}+y_{3}-1}\big)\times\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\vdots\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}}\Big)^{x_{s-1}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}-1}\big)\bigg\}+\\
\end{split}

\begin{split}\quad\quad\quad\quad&\bigg\{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s+1})\in S_{n-i,s+1}^{k_{1}-1}\end{subarray}}\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}=i\\
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{2}\}\end{subarray}}\big(\theta q^{0}\big)^{x_{1}}\big(1-\theta q^{0}\big)\cdots(1-\theta q^{y_{1}-1})\times\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}}\Big)^{x_{2}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}-1}\big)\times\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\Big)^{x_{3}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}+y_{3}-1}\big)\times\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\vdots\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}}\Big)^{x_{s+1}}\bigg\}+\\
\end{split}

\begin{split}\quad\quad\quad&\bigg\{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{n-i,s}^{k_{1}-1}\end{subarray}}\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}=i\\
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{2}\}\end{subarray}}\big(\theta q^{0}\big)^{x_{1}}\big(1-\theta q^{0}\big)\cdots(1-\theta q^{y_{1}-1})\times\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}}\Big)^{x_{2}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}-1}\big)\times\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\Big)^{x_{3}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}+y_{3}-1}\big)\times\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\vdots\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}}\Big)^{x_{s}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}-1}\big)\bigg\}\Bigg].\\
\end{split}

Using simple exponentiation algebra arguments to simplify,

\begin{split}&P_{q,\theta}\Big(E_{n,i}\Big)=\\
&\theta^{n-i}{\prod_{j=1}^{i}}\ (1-\theta q^{j-1})\times\\
&\sum_{s=1}^{i}\Bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{n-i,s}^{k_{1}-1}\end{subarray}}\quad\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}=i\\
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{2}\}\end{subarray}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s})x_{s}}+\\
&\quad\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{n-i,s-1}^{k_{1}-1}\end{subarray}}\quad\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}=i\\
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{2}\}\end{subarray}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s-1}}+\\
&\quad\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s+1})\in S_{n-i,s+1}^{k_{1}-1}\end{subarray}}\quad\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}=i\\
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{2}\}\end{subarray}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s})x_{s+1}}+\\
&\quad\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{n-i,s}^{k_{1}-1}\end{subarray}}\quad\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}=i\\
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{2}\}\end{subarray}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s}}\Bigg].\\
\end{split}

Using Lemma
3.10
, Lemma
3.12
, Lemma
7.6
and Lemma
7.8
, we can rewrite as follows.

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(E_{n,i}\Big)=\theta^{n-i}\ {\prod_{j=1}^{i}}\ (1-\theta q^{j-1})\sum_{s=1}^{i}&\Big[H_{q,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}+1}(n-i,\ i,\ s)+O_{q,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}+1}(n-i,\ i,\ s)\\
&+G_{q,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}+1}(n-i,\ i,\ s+1)+P_{q,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}+1}(n-i,\ i,\ s)\Big],\\
\end{split}

where

\begin{split}H_{q,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}+1}(n&-i,\ i,\ s)\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{n-i,s}^{k_{1}-1}\end{subarray}}\quad\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}=i\\
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{2}\}\end{subarray}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s})x_{s}},\end{split}

\begin{split}O_{q,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}+1}(n&-i,\ i,\ s)\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{n-i,s-1}^{k_{1}-1}\end{subarray}}\quad\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}=i\\
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{2}\}\end{subarray}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s-1}},\end{split}

\begin{split}G_{q,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}+1}(n&-i,\ i,\ s+1)\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s+1})\in S_{n-i,s+1}^{k_{1}-1}\end{subarray}}\quad\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}=i\\
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{2}\}\end{subarray}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s})x_{s+1}},\end{split}

and

\begin{split}P_{q,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}+1}(n&-i,\ i,\ s)\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{n-i,s}^{k_{1}-1}\end{subarray}}\quad\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}=i\\
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,k_{2}\}\end{subarray}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s}}.\end{split}

Therefore we can compute the probability of the event
\left\{L_{n}^{(1)}\geq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\leq k_{2}\right\}
as follows

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\geq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\leq k_{2}\Big)=&\sum_{i=1}^{n-k_{1}}\theta^{n-i}\ {\prod_{j=1}^{i}}\ (1-\theta q^{j-1})\sum_{s=1}^{i}\Big[H_{q,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}+1}(n-i,\ i,\ s)\\
&+O_{q,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}+1}(n-i,\ i,\ s)+G_{q,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}+1}(n-i,\ i,\ s+1)\\
&+P_{q,k_{1},0}^{\infty,k_{2}+1}(n-i,\ i,\ s)\Big].\\
\end{split}

Thus proof is completed.
∎

It is worth mentioning here that the PMF
P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\geq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\leq k_{2}\Big)
approaches the probability function of
P_{\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\geq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\leq k_{2}\Big)
of IID model. The details are presented in the following remark.

Remark 44
.

For
q=1
, the PMF
P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\geq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\leq k_{2}\Big)
for
n\geq k_{1}
reduces to the PMF
P_{\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\geq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\leq k_{2}\Big)
for
n\geq k_{1}
is given by

\begin{split}P\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\geq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\leq k_{2}\Big)=&\sum_{i=1}^{n-k_{1}}\theta^{n-i}\ (1-\theta)^{i}\sum_{s=1}^{i}S(s,\ k_{2}+1,\ i)\Big[R(s,\ k_{1},\ n-i)\\
&+R(s-1,\ k_{1},\ n-i)+S(s+1,\ k_{1},\ n-i)\\
&+R(s,\ k_{1},\ n-i)\Big]J(n,k_{1}+1)+J(n,k_{1})\theta^{n},\\
\end{split}

where

\begin{split}S(a,\ b,\ c)=\sum_{j=0}^{min(a,\left[\frac{c-a}{b-1}\right])}(-1)^{j}{a\choose j}{c-j(b-1)-1\choose a-1},\end{split}

\begin{split}R(a,\ b,\ c)=\sum_{j=1}^{min\left(a,\ \left[\frac{c-a}{b-1}\right]\right)}(-1)^{j+1}{a\choose j}{c-j(b-1)-1\choose a-1}.\end{split}

and
J(n,k_{1})
is the function defined by
J(n,k_{1})=1
if
n\geq k_{1}
and
0
otherwise.

7.4
Closed expression for the PMF of
\left(L_{n}^{(1)}\geq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\geq k_{2}\right)

We shall study of the joint distribution of
\left(L_{n}^{(1)}\geq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\geq k_{2}\right)
. We now make some useful Definition and Lemma for the proofs of Theorem in the sequel.

Definition 7.9
.

For
0<q\leq 1
, we define

\begin{split}\overline{Q}_{q,k_{1},0}(u,v,s)={\sum_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}}}\ \sum_{y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s})x_{s}},\ \end{split}

where the summation is over all integers
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s},
and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}
satisfying

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{u,s}^{k_{1}-1},\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{v,s}^{0}.\end{split}

The following gives a recurrence relation useful for the computation of
\overline{Q}_{q,k_{1},0}(u,v,s)
.

Lemma 7.9
.

\overline{Q}_{q,k_{1},0}(u,v,s)
obeys the following recurrence relation.

\begin{split}\overline{Q}&{}_{q,k_{1},0}(u,v,s)\\
&=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\sum_{a=1}^{k_{1}-1}\sum_{b=1}^{v-(s-1)}q^{va}\overline{Q}_{q,k_{1},0}(u-a,v-b,s-1)\\
+\sum_{a=k_{1}}^{u-(s-1)}\sum_{b=1}^{v-(s-1)}q^{va}\overline{J}_{q}(u-a,v-b,s-1),&\text{for}\ s>1,\ (s-1)+k_{1}\leq u\\
&\text{and}\ s\leq v\\
1,&\text{for}\ s=1,\ k_{1}\leq u,\ \text{and}\ 1\leq v\\
0,&\text{otherwise.}\\
\end{array}\right.\end{split}

Proof.

For
s>1
,
(s-1)+k_{1}\leq u
and
s\leq v
, we observe that
y_{s}
may assume any value
1,\ldots,v-(s-1)
, then
\overline{Q}_{q}(u,v,s)
can be written as

\begin{split}\overline{Q}&{}_{q,k_{1},0}(u,v,s)\\
=&\sum_{y_{s}=1}^{v-(s-1)}{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{u,s}^{k_{1}-1}\end{subarray}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{v-y_{s},s-1}^{0}\end{subarray}}}q^{vx_{s}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s-1}}.\end{split}

Similarly, we observe that since
x_{s}
can assume the values
1,\ldots,u-(s-1)
, then
\overline{Q}_{q}(u,v,s)
can be rewritten as

\begin{split}\overline{Q}&{}_{q,k_{1},0}(u,v,s)\\
=&\sum_{x_{s}=1}^{k_{1}-1}\sum_{y_{s}=1}^{v-(s-1)}{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{u-x_{s},s-1}^{k_{1}-1}\end{subarray}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{v-y_{s},s-1}^{0}\end{subarray}}}q^{vx_{s}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s-1}}\\
&+\sum_{x_{s}=k_{1}}^{u-(s-1)}\sum_{y_{s}=1}^{v-(s-1)}{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{u-x_{s},s-1}^{0}\end{subarray}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{v-y_{s},s-1}^{0}\end{subarray}}}q^{vx_{s}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s-1}}\\
=&\sum_{a=1}^{k_{1}-1}\sum_{b=1}^{v-(s-1)}q^{va}\overline{Q}_{q,k_{1},0}(u-a,v-b,s-1)+\sum_{a=k_{1}}^{u-(s-1)}\sum_{b=1}^{v-(s-1)}q^{va}\overline{J}_{q}(u-a,v-b,s-1).\end{split}

The other cases are obvious and thus the proof is completed.
∎

Remark 45
.

We observe that
\overline{Q}_{1,k_{1},0}(u,v,s)
is the number of integer solutions
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})
and
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})
of

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{u,s}^{k_{1}-1},\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{v,s}^{0}.\end{split}

given by

\begin{split}\overline{Q}_{1,k_{1},0}(u,v,s)=R(s,\ k_{1},\ u)M(s,\ v),\end{split}

where
R(a,\ b,\ c)
denotes the total number of integer solution
y_{1}+x_{2}+\cdots+y_{a}=c
such that
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{a})\in S_{r,a}^{k_{2}}
. The number is given by

\begin{split}R(a,\ b,\ c)=\sum_{j=1}^{min\left(a,\ \left[\frac{c-a}{b-1}\right]\right)}(-1)^{j+1}{a\choose j}{c-j(b-1)-1\choose a-1}.\end{split}

where
M(a,\ b)
denotes the total number of integer solution
x_{1}+x_{2}+\cdots+x_{a}=b
such that
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{a})\in S_{b,a}^{0}
. The number is given by

\begin{split}M(a,\ b)={b-1\choose a-1}.\end{split}

See, e.g.
Charalambides, 2002
.

Definition 7.10
.

For
0<q\leq 1
, we define

\begin{split}Q_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(m,r,s)={\sum_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}}}\ \sum_{y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s})x_{s}},\ \end{split}

where the summation is over all integers
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s},
and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}
satisfying

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{m,s}^{k_{1}-1},\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{r,s}^{k_{2}-1}.\end{split}

The following gives a recurrence relation useful for the computation of
Q_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(m,r,s)
.

Lemma 7.10
.

Q_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(m,r,s)
obeys the following recurrence relation.

\begin{split}Q&{}_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(m,r,s)\\
&=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\sum_{b=1}^{k_{2}-1}\sum_{a=1}^{k_{1}-1}q^{ra}Q_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(m-a,r-b,s-1)\\
+\sum_{b=k_{2}}^{r-(s-1)}\sum_{a=1}^{k_{1}-1}q^{ra}\overline{Q}_{q,k_{1},0}(m-a,r-b,s-1)\\
+\sum_{b=1}^{k_{2}-1}\sum_{a=k_{1}}^{m-(s-1)}q^{ra}J_{q,0,k_{2}}(m-a,r-b,s-1)\\
+\sum_{b=k_{2}}^{r-(s-1)}\sum_{a=k_{1}}^{m-(s-1)}q^{ra}\overline{J}_{q}(m-a,r-b,s-1),&\text{for}\ s>1,\ (s-1)+k_{1}\leq m\\
&\text{and}\ (s-1)+k_{2}\leq r\\
1,&\text{for}\ s=1,\ k_{1}\leq m,\ \text{and}\ k_{2}\leq r\\
0,&\text{otherwise.}\\
\end{array}\right.\end{split}

Proof.

For
s>1
,
(s-1)+k_{1}\leq m
and
(s-1)+k_{2}\leq r
, we observe that
x_{s}
may assume any value
1,\ldots,m-(s-1)
, then
Q_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(m,r,s)
can be written as

\begin{split}Q&{}_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(m,r,s)\\
=&\sum_{x_{s}=1}^{k_{1}-1}{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{m-x_{s},s-1}^{k_{1}-1}\end{subarray}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{r,s}^{k_{2}-1}\end{subarray}}}q^{rx_{s}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s-1}}\\
&+\sum_{x_{s}=k_{1}}^{m-(s-1)}{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{m-x_{s},s-1}^{0}\end{subarray}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{r,s}^{k_{2}-1}\end{subarray}}}q^{rx_{s}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s-1}}.\end{split}

Similarly, we observe that since
y_{s}
can assume the values
1,\ldots,r-(s-1)
, then
Q_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(m,r,s)
can be rewritten as

\begin{split}Q&{}_{q}(m,r,s)\\
=&\sum_{y_{s}=1}^{k_{2}-1}\sum_{x_{s}=1}^{k_{1}-1}{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{m-x_{s},s-1}^{k_{1}-1}\end{subarray}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{r-y_{s},s-1}^{k_{2}-1}\end{subarray}}}q^{rx_{s}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s-1}}\\
&+\sum_{y_{s}=k_{2}}^{r-(s-1)}\sum_{x_{s}=1}^{k_{1}-1}{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{m-x_{s},s-1}^{k_{1}-1}\end{subarray}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{r-y_{s},s-1}^{0}\end{subarray}}}q^{rx_{s}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s-1}}\\
&+\sum_{y_{s}=1}^{k_{2}-1}\sum_{x_{s}=k_{1}}^{m-(s-1)}{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{m-x_{s},s-1}^{0}\end{subarray}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{r-y_{s},s-1}^{k_{2}-1}\end{subarray}}}q^{rx_{s}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s-1}}\\
&+\sum_{y_{s}=k_{2}}^{r-(s-1)}\sum_{x_{s}=k_{1}}^{m-(s-1)}{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{m-x_{s},s-1}^{0}\end{subarray}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{r-y_{s},s-1}^{0}\end{subarray}}}q^{rx_{s}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s-1}}\\
=&\sum_{b=1}^{k_{2}-1}\sum_{a=1}^{k_{1}-1}q^{ra}Q_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(m-a,r-b,s-1)+\sum_{b=k_{2}}^{r-(s-1)}\sum_{a=1}^{k_{1}-1}q^{ra}\overline{Q}_{q,k_{1},0}(m-a,r-b,s-1)\\
&+\sum_{b=1}^{k_{2}-1}\sum_{a=k_{1}}^{m-(s-1)}q^{ra}J_{q,0,k_{2}}(m-a,r-b,s-1)+\sum_{b=k_{2}}^{r-(s-1)}\sum_{a=k_{1}}^{m-(s-1)}q^{ra}\overline{J}_{q}(m-a,r-b,s-1).\end{split}

The other cases are obvious and thus the proof is completed.
∎

.

We observe that
Q_{1,k_{1},k_{2}}(m,r,s)

and

of

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{m,s}^{k_{1}-1},\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{r,s}^{k_{2}-1}\end{split}

given by

\begin{split}Q_{1,k_{1},k_{2}}(m,r,s)=R(s,\ k_{1},\ m)R(s,\ k_{2},\ r),\end{split}

where
R(a,\ b,\ c)
denotes the total number of integer solution
y_{1}+x_{2}+\cdots+y_{a}=c
such that
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{a})\in S_{r,a}^{k_{2}}
. The number is given by

\begin{split}R(a,\ b,\ c)=\sum_{j=1}^{min\left(a,\ \left[\frac{c-a}{b-1}\right]\right)}(-1)^{j+1}{a\choose j}{c-j(b-1)-1\choose a-1}.\end{split}

Definition 7.11
.

For
0<q\leq 1
, we define

\begin{split}\overline{R}_{q,0,k_{2}}(u,v,s)={\sum_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1}}}\ \sum_{y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s-1}},\ \end{split}

where the summation is over all integers
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1},
and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}
satisfying

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{u,s-1}^{0},\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{v,s}^{k_{2}-1}.\end{split}

The following gives a recurrence relation useful for the computation of
\overline{R}_{q,0,k_{2}}(u,v,s)
.

Lemma 7.11
.

\overline{R}_{q,0,k_{2}}(u,v,s)
obeys the following recurrence relation.

\begin{split}\overline{R}&{}_{q,0,k_{2}}(u,v,s)\\
&=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\sum_{b=1}^{k_{2}-1}\sum_{a=1}^{u-(s-2)}q^{a(v-b)}\overline{R}_{q,0,k_{2}}(u-a,v-b,s-1)\\
+\sum_{b=k_{2}}^{v-(s-1)}\sum_{a=1}^{u-(s-2)}q^{a(v-b)}\overline{K}_{q}(u-a,v-b,s-1),&\text{for}\ s>1,\ s-1\leq u\ \text{and}\\
&(s-1)+k_{2}\leq v\\
1,&\text{for}\ s=1,\ u=0,\ \text{and}\ k_{2}\leq v\\
0,&\text{otherwise.}\\
\end{array}\right.\end{split}

Proof.

For
s>1
,
s-1\leq u
and
(s-1)+k_{2}\leq v
, we observe that
x_{s-1}
may assume any value
1,\ldots,u-(s-2)
, then
\overline{R}_{q,0,k_{2}}(u,v,s)
can be written as

\begin{split}\overline{R}&{}_{q,0,k_{2}}(u,v,s)\\
=&\sum_{x_{s-1}=1}^{u-(s-2)}{\sum_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{u,s-1}^{0}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{v,s}^{k_{2}-1}}}q^{x_{s-1}(v-y_{s})}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2})x_{s-2}}\end{split}

Similarly, we observe that since
y_{s}
can assume the values
1,\ldots,v-(s-1)
, then
\overline{R}_{q,0,k_{2}}(u,v,s)
can be rewritten as

\begin{split}\overline{R}&{}_{q,0,k_{2}}(u,v,s)\\
=&\sum_{y_{s}=1}^{k_{2}-1}\sum_{x_{s-1}=1}^{u-(s-2)}q^{x_{s-1}(v-y_{s})}{\sum_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{u-x_{s-1},s-1}^{0}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{v-y_{s},s-1}^{k_{2}-1}}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2})x_{s-2}}\\
&+\sum_{y_{s}=k_{2}}^{v-(s-1)}\sum_{x_{s-1}=1}^{u-(s-2)}q^{x_{s-1}(v-y_{s})}{\hskip-25.6073pt\sum_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{u-x_{s-1},s-1}^{0}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{v-y_{s},s-1}^{0}}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2})x_{s-2}}\\
=&\sum_{b=1}^{k_{2}-1}\sum_{a=1}^{u-(s-2)}q^{a(v-b)}\overline{R}_{q,0,k_{2}}(u-a,v-b,s-1)+\sum_{b=k_{2}}^{v-(s-1)}\sum_{a=1}^{u-(s-2)}q^{a(v-b)}\overline{K}_{q}(u-a,v-b,s-1).\end{split}

The other cases are obvious and thus the proof is completed.
∎

Definition 7.12
.

For
0<q\leq 1
, we define

\begin{split}R_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(m,r,s)={\sum_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1}}}\sum_{y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s-1}},\ \end{split}

where the summation is over all integers
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1},
and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}
satisfying

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{m,s-1}^{k_{1}-1},\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{r,s}^{k_{2}-1}.\end{split}

The following gives a recurrence relation useful for the computation of
R_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(m,r,s)
.

Lemma 7.12
.

R_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(m,r,s)
obeys the following recurrence relation.

\begin{split}R&{}_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(m,r,s)\\
&=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\sum_{b=1}^{k_{2}-1}\sum_{a=1}^{k_{1}-1}q^{a(r-b)}R_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(m-a,r-b,s-1)\\
+\sum_{b=k_{2}}^{r-(s-1)}\sum_{a=1}^{k_{1}-1}q^{a(r-b)}K_{q,k_{1},0}(m-a,r-b,s-1)\\
+\sum_{b=1}^{k_{2}-1}\sum_{a=k_{1}}^{m-(s-2)}q^{a(r-b)}\overline{R}_{q,0,k_{2}}(m-a,r-b,s-1)\\
+\sum_{b=k_{2}}^{r-(s-1)}\sum_{a=k_{1}}^{m-(s-2)}q^{a(r-b)}\overline{K}_{q}(m-a,r-b,s-1),&\text{for}\ s>1,\ (s-2)+k_{1}\leq m\\
&\text{and}\ (s-1)+k_{2}\leq r\\
0,&\text{otherwise.}\\
\end{array}\right.\end{split}

Proof.

For
s>1
,
(s-2)+k_{1}\leq m
and
(s-1)+k_{2}\leq r
, we observe that
x_{s-1}
may assume any value
1,\ldots,m-(s-2)
, then
R_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(m,r,s)
can be written as

\begin{split}R&{}_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(m,r,s)\\
=&\sum_{x_{s-1}=1}^{k_{1}-1}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-2})\in S_{m-x_{s-1},s-2}^{k_{1}-1}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{r,s}^{k_{2}-1}}}q^{x_{s-1}(r-y_{s})}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2})x_{s-2}}\\
&+\sum_{x_{s-1}=k_{1}}^{m-(s-2)}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-2})\in S_{m-x_{s-1},s-2}^{0}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{r,s}^{k_{2}-1}}}q^{x_{s-1}(r-y_{s})}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2})x_{s-2}}.\end{split}

Similarly, we observe that since
y_{s}
can assume the values
1,\ldots,r-(s-1)
, then
R_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(m,r,s)
can be rewritten as

\begin{split}R&{}_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(m,r,s)\\
=&\sum_{y_{s}=1}^{k_{2}-1}\sum_{x_{s-1}=1}^{k_{1}-1}q^{x_{s-1}(r-y_{s})}{\hskip-14.22636pt\sum_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-2})\in S_{m-x_{s-1},s-2}^{k_{1}-1}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{r-y_{s},s-1}^{k_{2}-1}}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2})x_{s-2}}\\
&+\sum_{y_{s}=k_{2}}^{r-(s-1)}\sum_{x_{s-1}=1}^{k_{1}-1}q^{x_{s-1}(r-y_{s})}{\hskip-14.22636pt\sum_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-2})\in S_{m-x_{s-1},s-2}^{k_{1}-1}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{r-y_{s},s-1}^{0}}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2})x_{s-2}}\\
&+\sum_{y_{s}=1}^{k_{2}-1}\sum_{x_{s-1}=k_{1}}^{m-(s-2)}q^{x_{s-1}(r-y_{s})}{\hskip-14.22636pt\sum_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-2})\in S_{m-x_{s-1},s-2}^{0}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{(y_{1},\ \ldots,\ y_{s-1})\in S_{r-y_{s},s-1}^{k_{2}-1}}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2})x_{s-2}}\\
&+\sum_{y_{s}=k_{2}}^{r-(s-1)}\sum_{x_{s-1}=k_{1}}^{m-(s-2)}q^{x_{s-1}(r-y_{s})}{\hskip-14.22636pt\sum_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-2})\in S_{m-x_{s-1},s-2}^{0}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{r-y_{s},s-1}^{0}}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2})x_{s-2}}\\
=&\sum_{b=1}^{k_{2}-1}\sum_{a=1}^{k_{1}-1}q^{a(r-b)}R_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(m-a,r-b,s-1)+\sum_{b=k_{2}}^{r-(s-1)}\sum_{a=1}^{k_{1}-1}q^{a(r-b)}K_{q,k_{1},0}(m-a,r-b,s-1)\\
&+\sum_{b=1}^{k_{2}-1}\sum_{a=k_{1}}^{m-(s-2)}q^{a(r-b)}\overline{R}_{q,0,k_{2}}(m-a,r-b,s-1)+\sum_{b=k_{2}}^{r-(s-1)}\sum_{a=k_{1}}^{m-(s-2)}q^{a(r-b)}\overline{K}_{q}(m-a,r-b,s-1).\end{split}

The other cases are obvious and thus the proof is completed.
∎

Remark 48
.

We observe that
R_{1,k_{1},k_{2}}(m,r,s)
is the number of integer solutions
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})
and
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})
of

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{m,s-1}^{k_{1}-1},\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{r,s}^{k_{2}-1}\end{split}

given by

\begin{split}R_{1,k_{1},k_{2}}(m,r,s)=R(s-1,\ k_{1},\ m)R(s,\ k_{2},\ r),\end{split}

where
R(a,\ b,\ c)
denotes the total number of integer solution
x_{1}+x_{2}+\cdots+x_{a}=c
such that
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{a})\in S_{r,a}^{k_{2}}
. The number is given by

\begin{split}R(a,\ b,\ c)=\sum_{j=1}^{min\left(a,\ \left[\frac{c-a}{b-1}\right]\right)}(-1)^{j+1}{a\choose j}{c-j(b-1)-1\choose a-1}.\end{split}

See, e.g.
Charalambides, 2002
.

Definition 7.13
.

For
0<q\leq 1
, we define

\begin{split}\overline{S}_{q,k_{1},0}(u,v,s)={\sum_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}}}\ \sum_{y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s}},\ \end{split}

where the summation is over all integers
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s},
and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1}
satisfying

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{u,s}^{k_{1}-1},\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{v,s-1}^{0}.\end{split}

The following gives a recurrence relation useful for the computation of
\overline{S}_{q,k_{1},0}(u,v,s)
.

Lemma 7.13
.

\overline{S}_{q,k_{1},0}(u,v,s)
obeys the following recurrence relation.

\begin{split}\overline{S}&{}_{q,k_{1},0}(u,v,s)\\
&=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\sum_{a=1}^{k_{1}-1}\sum_{b=1}^{v-(s-2)}q^{va}\overline{S}_{q,k_{1},0}(u-a,v-b,s-1)\\
+\sum_{a=k_{1}}^{u-(s-1)}\sum_{b=1}^{v-(s-2)}q^{va}\overline{I}_{q}(u-a,v-b,s-1),&\text{for}\ s>1,\ (s-1)+k_{1}\leq u\ \text{and}\ (s-1)\leq v\\
1,&\text{for}\ s=1,\ k_{1}\leq u,\ \text{and}\ v=0\\
0,&\text{otherwise.}\\
\end{array}\right.\end{split}

Proof.

For
s>1
,
(s-1)+k_{1}\leq u
and
(s-1)\leq v
, we observe that
y_{s-1}
may assume any value
1,\ldots,v-(s-2)
, then
\overline{S}_{q,k_{1},0}(u,v,s)
can be written as,

\begin{split}\overline{S}&{}_{q,k_{1},0}(u,v,s)\\
=&\sum_{y_{s-1}=1}^{v-(s-2)}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{u,s}^{k_{1}-1}\end{subarray}}}{\hskip 14.22636pt\sum_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-2})\in S_{v-y_{s-1},s-2}^{0}}}q^{vx_{s}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2})x_{s-1}}.\end{split}

Similarly, we observe that since
x_{s}
can assume the values
1,\ldots,u-(s-1)
, then
\overline{S}_{q,k_{1},0}(u,v,s)
can be rewritten as

\begin{split}\overline{R}&{}_{q,k_{1},0}(u,v,s)\\
=&\sum_{x_{s}=1}^{k_{1}-1}\sum_{y_{s-1}=1}^{v-(s-2)}{\hskip 5.69046pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{u-x_{s},s-1}^{k_{1}-1}\end{subarray}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-2})\in S_{v-y_{s-1},s-2}^{0}}}q^{vx_{s}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2})x_{s-1}}\\
&+\sum_{x_{s}=k_{1}}^{u-(s-1)}\sum_{y_{s-1}=1}^{v-(s-2)}{\hskip 5.69046pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{u-x_{s},s-1}^{0}\end{subarray}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-2})\in S_{v-y_{s-1},s-2}^{0}}}q^{vx_{s}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2})x_{s-1}}\\
=&\sum_{a=1}^{k_{1}-1}\sum_{b=1}^{v-(s-2)}q^{va}\overline{S}_{q,k_{1},0}(u-a,v-b,s-1)+\sum_{a=k_{1}}^{u-(s-1)}\sum_{b=1}^{v-(s-2)}q^{va}\overline{I}_{q}(u-a,v-b,s-1).\end{split}

The other cases are obvious and thus the proof is completed.
∎

Remark 49
.

We observe that
\overline{S}_{1,k_{1},0}(u,v,s)
is the number of integer solutions
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})
and
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})
of

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{u,s}^{k_{1}-1},\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{v,s-1}^{0}\end{split}

given by

\begin{split}\overline{S}_{1,k_{1},0}(u,v,s)=R(s,\ k_{1},\ u)M(s-1,\ v),\end{split}

where
R(a,\ b,\ c)
denotes the total number of integer solution
y_{1}+x_{2}+\cdots+y_{a}=c
such that
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{a})\in S_{r,a}^{k_{2}}
. The number is given by

\begin{split}R(a,\ b,\ c)=\sum_{j=1}^{min\left(a,\ \left[\frac{c-a}{b-1}\right]\right)}(-1)^{j+1}{a\choose j}{c-j(b-1)-1\choose a-1}.\end{split}

where
M(a,\ b)
denotes the total number of integer solution
x_{1}+x_{2}+\cdots+x_{a}=b
such that
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{a})\in S_{b,a}^{0}
. The number is given by

\begin{split}M(a,\ b)={b-1\choose a-1}.\end{split}

See, e.g.
Charalambides, 2002
.

Definition 7.14
.

For
0<q\leq 1
, we define

\begin{split}S_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(m,r,s)={\sum_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}}}\ \sum_{y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s}},\ \end{split}

where the summation is over all integers
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s},
and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1}
satisfying

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{m,s}^{k_{1}-1},\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{r,s-1}^{k_{2}-1}.\end{split}

The following gives a recurrence relation useful for the computation of
S_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(m,r,s)
.

Lemma 7.14
.

S_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(m,r,s)
obeys the following recurrence relation.

\begin{split}S&{}_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(m,r,s)\\
&=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\sum_{b=1}^{k_{2}-1}\sum_{a=1}^{k_{1}-1}q^{ra}S_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(u-a,v-b,s-1)\\
+\sum_{b=k_{2}}^{r-(s-2)}\sum_{a=1}^{k_{1}-1}q^{ra}\overline{S}_{q,k_{1},0}(u-a,v-b,s-1)\\
+\sum_{b=1}^{k_{2}-1}\sum_{a=k_{1}}^{m-(s-1)}q^{ra}I_{q,0,k_{2}}(u-a,v-b,s-1)\\
+\sum_{b=k_{2}}^{r-(s-2)}\sum_{a=k_{1}}^{m-(s-1)}q^{ra}\overline{I}_{q}(u-a,v-b,s-1),&\text{for}\ s>1,\ (s-1)+k_{1}\leq m\\
&\text{and}\ (s-2)+k_{2}\leq r\\
1,&\text{for}\ s=1,\ k_{1}\leq m,\ \text{and}\ r=0\\
0,&\text{otherwise.}\\
\end{array}\right.\end{split}

Proof.

For
s>1
,
(s-1)+k_{1}\leq m
and
(s-2)+k_{2}\leq r
, we observe that
x_{s}
may assume any value
1,\ldots,m-(s-1)
, then
S_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(m,r,s)
can be written as

\begin{split}S&{}_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(m,r,s)\\
=&\sum_{x_{s}=1}^{k_{1}-1}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{m-x_{s},s-1}^{k_{1}-1}\end{subarray}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{r,s-1}^{k_{2}-1}}}q^{rx_{s}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2})x_{s-1}}\\
&+\sum_{x_{s}=k_{1}}^{m-(s-1)}{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{m-x_{s},s-1}^{0}\end{subarray}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{r,s-1}^{k_{2}-1}}}q^{rx_{s}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2})x_{s-1}}.\end{split}

Similarly, we observe that since
y_{s}
can assume the values
1,\ldots,r-(s-2)
, then
S_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(m,r,s)
can be rewritten as

\begin{split}S&{}_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(m,r,s)\\
=&\sum_{y_{s-1}=1}^{k_{2}-1}\sum_{x_{s}=1}^{m-(s-1)}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{m-x_{s},s-1}^{k_{1}-1}\end{subarray}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-2})\in S_{r-y_{s-1},s-2}^{k_{2}-1}}}q^{rx_{s}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2})x_{s-1}}\\
&+\sum_{y_{s-1}=k_{2}}^{r-(s-2)}\sum_{x_{s}=1}^{k_{1}-1}\hskip 8.5359pt{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{m-x_{s},s-1}^{k_{1}-1}\end{subarray}}}\hskip 8.5359pt{\sum_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-2})\in S_{r-y_{s-1},s-2}^{0}}}q^{rx_{s}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2})x_{s-1}}\\
&+\sum_{y_{s-1}=1}^{k_{2}-1}\sum_{x_{s}=k_{1}}^{m-(s-1)}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{m-x_{s},s-1}^{0}\end{subarray}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-2})\in S_{r-y_{s-1},s-2}^{k_{2}-1}}}q^{rx_{s}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2})x_{s-1}}\\
&+\sum_{y_{s-1}=k_{2}}^{r-(s-2)}\sum_{x_{s}=k_{1}}^{m-(s-1)}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{m-x_{s},s-1}^{0}\end{subarray}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-2})\in S_{r-y_{s-1},s-2}^{0}}}q^{rx_{s}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2})x_{s-1}}\\
\end{split}

\begin{split}=&\sum_{b=1}^{k_{2}-1}\sum_{a=1}^{k_{1}-1}q^{ra}S_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(u-a,v-b,s-1)+\sum_{b=k_{2}}^{r-(s-2)}\sum_{a=1}^{k_{1}-1}q^{ra}\overline{S}_{q,k_{1},0}(u-a,v-b,s-1)\\
&+\sum_{b=1}^{k_{2}-1}\sum_{a=1}^{m-(s-1)}q^{ra}I_{q,0,k_{2}}(u-a,v-b,s-1)+\sum_{b=k_{2}}^{r-(s-2)}\sum_{a=1}^{m-(s-1)}q^{ra}\overline{I}_{q}(u-a,v-b,s-1).\end{split}

The other cases are obvious and thus the proof is completed.

∎

and

of

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{m,s}^{k_{1}-1},\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{r,s-1}^{k_{2}-1}\end{split}

given by

\begin{split}S_{1,k_{1},k_{2}}(m,r,s)=R(s,\ k_{1},\ u)R(s-1,\ k_{2},\ r),\end{split}

where
R(a,\ b,\ c)
denotes the total number of integer solution
y_{1}+x_{2}+\cdots+y_{a}=c
such that
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{a})\in S_{r,a}^{k_{2}}
. The number is given by

\begin{split}R(a,\ b,\ c)=\sum_{j=1}^{min\left(a,\ \left[\frac{c-a}{b-1}\right]\right)}(-1)^{j+1}{a\choose j}{c-j(b-1)-1\choose a-1}.\end{split}

Charalambides, 2002
.

Definition 7.15
.

For
0<q\leq 1
, we define

\begin{split}\overline{T}_{q}(u,v,s)={\sum_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1}}}\ \sum_{y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s}},\ \end{split}

where the summation is over all integers
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s},
and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}
satisfying

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\ \in S_{u,s}^{0},\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{v,s}^{k_{2}-1}.\end{split}

The following gives a recurrence relation useful for the computation of
\overline{T}_{q,0,k_{2}}(u,v,s)
.

Lemma 7.15
.

\overline{T}_{q,0,k_{2}}(u,v,s)
obeys the following recurrence relation.

\begin{split}\overline{T}&{}_{q,0,k_{2}}(u,v,s)\\
&=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\sum_{b=1}^{k_{2}-1}\sum_{a=1}^{u-(s-1)}q^{a(v-b)}\overline{T}_{q,0,k_{2}}(u-a,v-b,s-1)\\
+\sum_{b=k_{2}}^{v-(s-1)}\sum_{a=1}^{u-(s-1)}q^{a(v-b)}\overline{L}_{q}(u-a,v-b,s-1),&\text{for}\ s>1,\ s\leq u\ \text{and}\ (s-1)+k_{2}\leq v\\
1,&\text{for}\ s=1,\ 1\leq u\ \text{and}\ k_{2}\leq v\\
0,&\text{otherwise.}\\
\end{array}\right.\end{split}

Proof.

For
s>1
,
s\leq u
and
(s-1)+k_{2}\leq v
, we observe that
x_{s}
may assume any value
1,\ldots,u-(s-1)
, then
\overline{T}_{q,0,k_{2}}(u,v,s)
can be written as

\begin{split}\overline{T}&{}_{q,0,k_{2}}(u,v,s)\\
=&\sum_{x_{s}=1}^{u-(s-1)}{\sum_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{u-x_{s},s-1}^{0}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{v,s}^{k_{2}-1}}}q^{x_{s}(v-y_{s})}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2})x_{s-1}}\end{split}

Similarly, we observe that since
y_{s}
can assume the values
1,\ldots,v-(s-1)
, then
\overline{T}_{q,0,k_{2}}(u,v,s)
can be rewritten as

\begin{split}\overline{T}&{}_{q,0,k_{2}}(u,v,s)\\
=&\sum_{y_{s}=1}^{k_{2}-1}\sum_{x_{s}=1}^{u-(s-1)}q^{x_{s}(v-y_{s})}{\hskip-14.22636pt\sum_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{u-x_{s},s-1}^{0}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{v-y_{s},s-1}^{k_{2}-1}}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2})x_{s-1}}\\
&+\sum_{y_{s}=k_{2}}^{v-(s-1)}\sum_{x_{s}=1}^{u-(s-1)}q^{x_{s}(v-y_{s})}{\hskip-14.22636pt\sum_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{u-x_{s},s-1}^{0}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{v-y_{s},s-1}^{0}}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2})x_{s-1}}\\
=&\sum_{b=1}^{k_{2}-1}\sum_{a=1}^{u-(s-1)}q^{a(v-b)}\overline{T}_{q,0,k_{2}}(u-a,v-b,s-1)+\sum_{b=k_{2}}^{v-(s-1)}\sum_{a=1}^{u-(s-1)}q^{a(v-b)}\overline{L}_{q}(u-a,v-b,s-1).\end{split}

The other cases are obvious and thus the proof is completed.
∎

Definition 7.16
.

For
0<q\leq 1
, we define

\begin{split}T_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(m,r,s)={\sum_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}}}\ \sum_{y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s}},\ \end{split}

where the summation is over all integers
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s},
and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}
satisfying

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\ \in S_{m,s}^{k_{1}-1},\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{r,s}^{k_{2}-1}\end{split}

The following gives a recurrence relation useful for the computation of
T_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(m,r,s)
.

Lemma 7.16
.

T_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(m,r,s)
obeys the following recurrence relation,

\begin{split}T&{}_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(m,r,s)\\
&=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\sum_{b=1}^{k_{2}-1}\sum_{a=1}^{k_{1}-1}\ q^{a(r-b)}T_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(m-a,r-b,s-1)\\
+\sum_{b=k_{2}}^{r-(s-1)}\sum_{a=1}^{k_{1}-1}\ q^{a(r-b)}L_{q,k_{1},0}(m-a,r-b,s-1)\\
+\sum_{b=1}^{k_{2}-1}\sum_{a=k_{1}}^{m-(s-1)}\ q^{a(r-b)}\overline{T}_{q,0,k_{2}}(m-a,r-b,s-1)\\
+\sum_{b=k_{2}}^{r-(s-1)}\sum_{a=k_{1}}^{m-(s-1)}\ q^{a(r-b)}\overline{L}_{q}(m-a,r-b,s-1),&\text{for}\quad s>1,\ (s-1)+k_{1}\leq m\\
&\text{and}\ (s-1)+k_{2}\leq r\\
1,&\text{for}\ s=1,\ k_{1}\leq m,\ \text{and}\ k_{2}\leq r\\
0,&\text{otherwise.}\\
\end{array}\right.\end{split}

Proof.

For
s>1
,
(s-1)+k_{1}\leq m
and
(s-1)+k_{2}\leq r
, we observe that
x_{s}
may assume any value
1,\ldots,m-(s-1)
, then
T_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(m,r,s)
can be written as

\begin{split}T&{}_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(m,r,s)\\
=&\sum_{x_{s}=1}^{k_{1}-1}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{m-x_{s},s}^{k_{1}-1}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{r,s}^{k_{2}-1}}}q^{x_{s}(r-y_{s})}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2})x_{s-1}}\\
&+\sum_{x_{s}=k_{1}}^{m-(s-1)}{\sum_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{m,s}^{0}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{r,s}^{k_{2}-1}}}q^{x_{s}(r-y_{s})}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2})x_{s-1}}\end{split}

Similarly, we observe that since
y_{s}
can assume the values
1,\ldots,r-(s-1)
, then
T_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(m,r,s)
can be rewritten as

\begin{split}T&{}_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(m,r,s)\\
=&\sum_{y_{s}=1}^{k_{2}-1}\sum_{x_{s}=1}^{k_{1}-1}q^{x_{s}(r-y_{s})}{\hskip-8.5359pt\sum_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{m-x_{s},s}^{k_{1}-1}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{r-y_{s},s-1}^{k_{2}-1}}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2})x_{s-1}}\\
&+\sum_{y_{s}=k_{2}}^{r-(s-1)}\sum_{x_{s}=1}^{k_{1}-1}q^{x_{s}(r-y_{s})}{\hskip-8.5359pt\sum_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{m-x_{s},s}^{k_{1}-1}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{r-y_{s},s-1}^{0}}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2})x_{s-1}}\\
&+\sum_{y_{s}=1}^{k_{2}-1}\sum_{x_{s}=k_{1}}^{m-(s-1)}q^{x_{s}(r-y_{s})}{\hskip-8.5359pt\sum_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{m-x_{s},s}^{0}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{r-y_{s},s-1}^{k_{2}-1}}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2})x_{s-1}}\\
&+\sum_{y_{s}=k_{2}}^{r-(s-1)}\sum_{x_{s}=k_{1}}^{m-(s-1)}q^{x_{s}(r-y_{s})}{\hskip-8.5359pt\sum_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{m-x_{s},s}^{0}}}{\hskip 8.5359pt\sum_{(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s-1})\in S_{r-y_{s},s-1}^{0}}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-2})x_{s-1}}\\
=&\sum_{b=1}^{k_{2}-1}\sum_{a=1}^{k_{1}-1}\ q^{a(r-b)}T_{q,0,k_{2}}(m-a,r-b,s-1)+\sum_{b=k_{2}}^{r-(s-1)}\sum_{a=1}^{k_{1}-1}\ q^{a(r-b)}L_{q,k_{1},0}(m-a,r-b,s-1)\\
&+\sum_{b=1}^{k_{2}-1}\sum_{a=k_{1}}^{m-(s-1)}q^{a(r-b)}\overline{T}_{q,0,k_{2}}(m-a,r-b,s-1)+\sum_{b=k_{2}}^{r-(s-1)}\sum_{a=k_{1}}^{m-(s-1)}\ q^{a(r-b)}\overline{L}_{q}(m-a,r-b,s-1).\end{split}

The other cases are obvious and thus the proof is completed.
∎

Remark 52
.

We observe that
T_{1,k_{1},k_{2}}(m,r,s)
is the number of integer solutions
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})
and
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})
of

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\ \in S_{m,s}^{k_{1}-1},\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{r,s}^{k_{2}-1}\end{split}

given by

\begin{split}T_{1,k_{1},k_{2}}(m,r,s)=R(s,\ k_{1},\ m)R(s,\ k_{2},\ r),\end{split}

where
R(a,\ b,\ c)
denotes the total number of integer solution
y_{1}+x_{2}+\cdots+y_{a}=c
such that
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{a})\in S_{r,a}^{k_{2}}
. The number is given by

\begin{split}R(a,\ b,\ c)=\sum_{j=1}^{min\left(a,\ \left[\frac{c-a}{b-1}\right]\right)}(-1)^{j+1}{a\choose j}{c-j(b-1)-1\choose a-1}.\end{split}

See, e.g.
Charalambides, 2002
.

The probability function of
P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\geq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\geq k_{2}\Big)
is obtained by the following theorem.

Theorem 7.4
.

The PMF
P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\geq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\geq k_{2}\Big)
for
n\geq k_{1}+k_{2}

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\geq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\geq k_{2}\Big)=&\sum_{i=k_{2}}^{n-k_{1}}\theta^{n-i}\ {\prod_{j=1}^{i}}\ (1-\theta q^{j-1})\sum_{s=1}^{i}\Big[Q_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(n-i,\ i,\ s)\\
&+R_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(n-i,\ i,\ s)+S_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(n-i,\ i,\ s+1)\\
&+T_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(n-i,\ i,\ s)\Big].\\
\end{split}

Proof.

We partition the event
\left\{L_{n}^{(1)}\geq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\geq k_{2}\right\}
into disjoint events given by
F_{n}=i,
for
i=k_{2},\ldots,\ n-k_{1}
. Adding the probabilities we have

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\geq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\geq k_{2}\Big)=\sum_{i=k_{2}}^{n-k_{1}}P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\geq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\geq k_{2}\ \wedge\ F_{n}=i\Big).\\
\end{split}

We will write
E_{n,i}=\left\{L_{n}^{(1)}\geq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\geq k_{2}\ \wedge\ F_{n}=i\right\}
.
We can now rewrite as follows

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\geq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\geq k_{2}\Big)=\sum_{i=k_{2}}^{n-k_{1}}P_{q,\theta}\left(E_{n,i}\right).\end{split}

We are going to focus on the event
E_{n,\ i}
. A typical element of the event
\Big\{L_{n}^{(1)}\geq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\geq k_{2}\ \wedge\ F_{n}=i\Big\}
is an ordered sequence which consists of
n-i
successes and
i
failures such that the length of the longest success run is greater than or equal to
k_{1}
and the length of the longest failure run is greater than or equal to
k_{2}
. The number of these sequences can be derived as follows. First we will distribute the
i
failures and the
n-i
successes. Let
s

(1\leq s\leq i)
be the number of runs of failures in the event
E_{n,\ i}
. We divide into two cases: starting with a failure run or starting with a success run. Thus, we distinguish between four types of sequences in the event
\Big\{L_{n}^{(1)}\geq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\geq k_{2}\ \wedge\ F_{n}=i\Big\}
, respectively named A, B, C and D-type, which are defined as follows.

\begin{split}\text{A-type}\ :&\quad\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{1}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{2}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{2}}\mid\ldots\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{s}},\\
\end{split}

with
i

0
’s and
n-i

1
’s, where
x_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s)
represents a length of run of
1
’s and
y_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s)
represents the length of a run of
0
’s. And all integers
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}
, and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}
satisfy the conditions

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{n-i,s}^{k_{1}-1},\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{2}-1}.\end{split}

\begin{split}\text{B-type}\ :&\quad\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{1}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{2}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{2}}\mid\ldots\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s-1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{s-1}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s}},\\
\end{split}

with
i

0
’s and
n-i

1
’s, where
x_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s-1)
represents a length of run of
1
’s and
y_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s)
represents the length of a run of
0
’s. And all integers
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1}
, and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}
satisfy the conditions

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{n-i,s-1}^{k_{1}-1},\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{2}-1}.\end{split}

\begin{split}\text{C-type}\ :&\quad\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{1}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{2}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{2}}\mid\ldots\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{s+1}},\\
\end{split}

with
i

0
’s and
n-i

1
’s, where
x_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s+1)
represents a length of run of
1
’s and
y_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s)
represents the length of a run of
0
’s. And all integers
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s+1}
, and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}
satisfy the conditions

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s+1})\in S_{n-i,s+1}^{k_{1}-1},\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{2}-1}.\end{split}

\begin{split}\text{D-type}\ :&\quad\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{1}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{2}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{2}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{3}}\mid\ldots\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s-1}}\mid\overbrace{1\ldots 1}^{x_{s}}\mid\overbrace{0\ldots 0}^{y_{s}},\\
\end{split}

with
i

0
’s and
n-i

1
’s, where
x_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s)
represents a length of run of
1
’s and
y_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,s)
represents the length of a run of
0
’s. And all integers
x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}
, and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{s}
satisfy the conditions

\begin{split}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{n-i,s}^{k_{1}-1},\ \text{and}\end{split}

\begin{split}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{2}-1}.\end{split}

Then the probability of the event
E_{n,\ i}
is given by

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(E_{n,\ i}\Big)\ &\\
=\sum_{s=1}^{i}\Bigg[\bigg\{&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{n-i,s}^{k_{1}-1}\end{subarray}}\quad\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{2}-1}\end{subarray}}\big(1-\theta q^{0}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}-1}\big)\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}}\Big)^{x_{1}}\times\\
&\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}-1}\big)\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\Big)^{x_{2}}\times\\
&\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}+y_{3}-1}\big)\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}+y_{3}}\Big)^{x_{3}}\times\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\vdots\\
&\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}-1}\big)\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}}\Big)^{x_{s}}\bigg\}+\\
\end{split}

\begin{split}\quad\ &\bigg\{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{n-i,s-1}^{k_{1}-1}\end{subarray}}\quad\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{2}-1}\end{subarray}}\big(1-\theta q^{0}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}-1}\big)\times\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}}\Big)^{x_{1}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}-1}\big)\times\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\Big)^{x_{2}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}+y_{3}-1}\big)\times\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\vdots\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}}\Big)^{x_{s-1}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}-1}\big)\bigg\}+\\
\end{split}

\begin{split}\quad\quad\quad\quad&\bigg\{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s+1})\in S_{n-i,s+1}^{k_{1}-1}\end{subarray}}\quad\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{2}-1}\end{subarray}}\big(\theta q^{0}\big)^{x_{1}}\big(1-\theta q^{0}\big)\cdots(1-\theta q^{y_{1}-1})\times\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}}\Big)^{x_{2}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}-1}\big)\times\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\Big)^{x_{3}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}+y_{3}-1}\big)\times\\
&\quad\quad\quad\vdots\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}}\Big)^{x_{s+1}}\bigg\}+\\
\end{split}

\begin{split}\quad\quad&\bigg\{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{n-i,s}^{k_{1}-1}\end{subarray}}\quad\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{2}-1}\end{subarray}}\big(\theta q^{0}\big)^{x_{1}}\big(1-\theta q^{0}\big)\cdots(1-\theta q^{y_{1}-1})\times\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}}\Big)^{x_{2}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}-1}\big)\times\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}}\Big)^{x_{3}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+y_{2}+y_{3}-1}\big)\times\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\vdots\\
&\Big(\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}}\Big)^{x_{s}}\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1}}\big)\cdots\big(1-\theta q^{y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s}-1}\big)\bigg\}\Bigg].\\
\end{split}

Using simple exponentiation algebra arguments to simplify,

\begin{split}&P_{q,\theta}\Big(E_{n,i}\Big)=\\
&\theta^{n-i}{\prod_{j=1}^{i}}\ (1-\theta q^{j-1})\times\\
&\sum_{s=1}^{i}\Bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{n-i,s}^{k_{1}-1}\end{subarray}}\quad\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{2}-1}\end{subarray}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s})x_{s}}+\\
&\quad\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{n-i,s-1}^{k_{1}-1}\end{subarray}}\quad\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{2}-1}\end{subarray}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s-1}}+\\
&\quad\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s+1})\in S_{n-i,s+1}^{k_{1}-1}\end{subarray}}\quad\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{2}-1}\end{subarray}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s})x_{s+1}}+\\
&\quad\quad\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{n-i,s}^{k_{1}-1}\end{subarray}}\quad\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{2}-1}\end{subarray}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s}}\Bigg].\\
\end{split}

Using Lemma
7.10
, Lemma
7.12
, Lemma
7.14
and Lemma
7.16
, we can rewrite as follows.

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(E_{n,i}\Big)=\theta^{n-i}\ {\prod_{j=1}^{i}}\ (1-\theta q^{j-1})\sum_{s=1}^{i}&\Big[Q_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(n-i,\ i,\ s)+R_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(n-i,\ i,\ s)\\
&+S_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(n-i,\ i,\ s+1)+T_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(n-i,\ i,\ s)\Big],\\
\end{split}

where

\begin{split}Q_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(n&-i,\ i,\ s)\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{n-i,s}^{k_{1}-1}\end{subarray}}\quad\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{2}-1}\end{subarray}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s})x_{s}},\end{split}

\begin{split}R_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(n&-i,\ i,\ s)\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s-1})\in S_{n-i,s-1}^{k_{1}-1}\end{subarray}}\quad\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{2}-1}\end{subarray}}q^{y_{1}x_{1}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{2}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s-1}},\end{split}

\begin{split}S_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(n&-i,\ i,\ s+1)\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s+1})\in S_{n-i,s+1}^{k_{1}-1}\end{subarray}}\quad\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{2}-1}\end{subarray}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s})x_{s+1}},\end{split}

and

\begin{split}T_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(n&-i,\ i,\ s)\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s})\in S_{n-i,s}^{k_{1}-1}\end{subarray}}\quad\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{s})\in S_{i,s}^{k_{2}-1}\end{subarray}}q^{y_{1}x_{2}+(y_{1}+y_{2})x_{3}+\cdots+(y_{1}+\cdots+y_{s-1})x_{s}}.\end{split}

Therefore we can compute the probability of the event
\left\{L_{n}^{(1)}\geq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\geq k_{2}\right\}
as follows

\begin{split}P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\geq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\geq k_{2}\Big)=&\sum_{i=k_{2}}^{n-k_{1}}\theta^{n-i}\ {\prod_{j=1}^{i}}\ (1-\theta q^{j-1})\sum_{s=1}^{i}\Big[Q_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(n-i,\ i,\ s)\\
&+R_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(n-i,\ i,\ s)+S_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(n-i,\ i,\ s+1)\\
&+T_{q,k_{1},k_{2}}(n-i,\ i,\ s)\Big].\\
\end{split}

Thus proof is completed.
∎

It is worth mentioning here that the PMF
P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\geq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\geq k_{2}\Big)
for
n\geq k_{1}+k_{2}
approaches the probability function of
P_{\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\geq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\geq k_{2}\Big)
in the limit as
q
tends to 1 of IID model. The details are presented in the following remark.

Remark 53
.

For
q=1
, the PMF
P_{q,\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\geq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\geq k_{2}\Big)
for
n\geq k_{1}+k_{2}
reduces to the PMF
P_{\theta}\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\geq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\geq k_{2}\Big)
for
n\geq k_{1}+k_{2}
is given by

\begin{split}P\Big(L_{n}^{(1)}\geq k_{1}\ \wedge\ L_{n}^{(0)}\geq k_{2}\Big)=&\sum_{i=k_{2}}^{n-k_{1}}\theta^{n-i}\ {\prod_{j=1}^{i}}\ (1-\theta q^{j-1})\sum_{s=1}^{i}\Big[R(s,n-i,\ k_{1},\ n-i)\\
&+R(s-1,\ k_{1},\ n-i)+R(s+1,\ k_{1},\ n-i)\\
&+R(s,\ k_{1},\ n-i)\Big]R(s,\ k_{2},\ i).\\
\end{split}

where

\begin{split}R(a,\ b,\ c)=\sum_{j=1}^{min\left(a,\ \left[\frac{c-a}{b-1}\right]\right)}(-1)^{j+1}{a\choose j}{c-j(b-1)-1\choose a-1}.\end{split}

See, e.g.
Charalambides, 2002
.
